- `IsValidPM W`: W ⪰ 0, and for all finite ancillas X, Y and all CPTP C_k, W ∗ C is the Choi matrix of a CPTP map from P × ∏X_k to F × ∏Y_k. With trivial source, sink and ancillas this is the AFNB normalisation Tr[W(⊗_k C_k)] = 1 [17].
- `UnitaryLink W`: for all finite ancillas X and all unitaries A_k : I_k × X_k → O_k × X_k, W ∗ (|A_k⟩⟩⟨⟨A_k|)_k = |M⟩⟩⟨⟨M| for some unitary M.
- `IsPurePM W := IsValidPM W ∧ UnitaryLink W`. This is AFNB's pure process [17, Def. 1] with finite ancillas.

*General lemmas:*
- `link_posSemidef`: a link of PSD matrices is PSD.
- `link_rankOne`: a link of rank-one matrices is rank one, with the linked vector.
- `isUnitaryM_of_vecOp_eq`: if |A⟩⟩⟨⟨A| = |M⟩⟩⟨⟨M| and M is unitary, then A is unitary.

*Scope.* These are finite-dimensional definitions: every type in them is finite.

**H.19.2 Bridges from the counterexample's definitions [PROVED (Lean)].** Write Wcl p = Σ_{i,o} p(i|o) |i,o⟩⟨i,o| for the diagonal process matrix of a classical process, with trivial source and sink.
- `isValidPM_Wcl_iff p : IsValidPM (Wcl p) ↔ Valid p`. So classical validity under products of stochastic maps is the same as AFNB validity under all CPTP local operations with finite ancillas. For the direction ⇐, each output distribution Σ_y C_k(…) is treated as a complex weight with fixed row sums. `valid_sum_complex` extends `Valid` multilinearly to such weights, by writing each local map in terms of the three deterministic maps (identity, NOT, constant).
- `isValidPM_Wcl_pfPoint_iff w : IsValidPM (Wcl (pfPoint w)) ↔ PFUL.Countable.IsProcessFunction (wBits w)`. The valid deterministic process matrices are exactly the process functions of the general definition.
- `isPure_iff_unitaryLink V : IsPure V ↔ UnitaryLink (choiMat V)`, for every V. The proof is `link_choiMat_vecOp`: |V⟩⟩⟨⟨V| ∗ (|A_k⟩⟩⟨⟨A_k|) = |compose V A⟩⟩⟨⟨compose V A|.
- `trace_link_Vsb` and `isValidPM_choiMat_Vsb`: W_sb = |Vsb⟩⟩⟨⟨Vsb| is valid under all CPTP local operations with any finite ancillas. The trace condition uses only trace preservation of the C_k. Its proof is the scalar-key identity of `isPure_Vsb` with the weights b_k(x,y,z) = Σ_y' C_k((z,ξ_k),(x,y'); (y,ξ'_k),(x,y')).
- `isPurePM_choiMat_Vsb : IsPurePM (choiMat Vsb)`.
- `inducedP_eq_born`: p(i|o) = Σ_{φ,π,π'} W(π,φ,(i,o); π',φ,(i,o)) ψ_π conj(ψ_π'), the Born rule.
- `purification_conjecture_false_choi`:

      ∃ p, IsValidPM (Wcl p) ∧ (∃ P F W ψ, IsPurePM W ∧ Σ|ψ|² = 1 ∧ ∀ i o, p(i|o) = Σ_{φ,π,π'} W(π,φ,(i,o); π',φ,(i,o)) ψ_π conj(ψ_π')) ∧ Wcl p ∉ convexHull ℝ {W | ∃ w, W = Wcl (pfPoint w) ∧ IsValidPM W}.

  Every predicate in it is a framework predicate. The hull statement is transported along the injective ℝ-linear map p ↦ Wcl p (`Wcl_mem_convexHull_iff`).

**H.19.3 What "pure" means with infinite-dimensional ancillas [definition; PROVED (Lean)].** *Definition.* V is pure against infinite ancillas if, for every family of complex Hilbert spaces K_k of any dimension and every family of unitaries A_k on C² ⊗ K_k, the closed loop is a unitary of C^P ⊗ ⨂_k K_k.

Mathlib has no Hilbert tensor product, so the Lean definition (`IsPureHilbert`) is stated through Hilbert bases:
- C² ⊗ K_k is any Hilbert space H_k with a Hilbert basis indexed by Bool × κ_k, where κ_k indexes a Hilbert basis of K_k. Every Hilbert space has one (Mathlib `exists_hilbertBasis`).
- The joint space is any Hilbert space G with a Hilbert basis indexed by P × ∏_k κ_k.
- The loop must be a unitary U of G whose matrix in that basis is the link product of V with the matrices ⟨b_r, A_k b_c⟩.

A Hilbert space is determined up to unitary equivalence by the cardinality of a Hilbert basis, so this is the tensor-product statement up to that identification [INTERPRETIVE: the identification only; Round 13 upgrades the local factor H_k = C² ⊗ K_k to PROVED (Lean), H.20.5]. The loop's matrix entries are finite sums over the Switchboard's indices of products of ancilla matrix entries, so they need no convergence argument. Only unitarity is an infinite-dimensional statement.
- `isIsoKernel_kerOf` and `isCoIsoKernel_kerOf`: in any Hilbert basis, the matrix of a unitary has orthonormal columns and rows.
- `isPureHilbert_iff_isUnitaryKer V`: for every V, purity with Hilbert-space ancillas is equivalent to the kernel form of Round 11. One direction transports along Hilbert bases. The other takes H_k = ℓ²(Bool × κ_k) and uses `unitary_of_unitaryKernel`.
- `isPureHilbert_Vsb`: the Switchboard is pure with ancillas in arbitrary Hilbert spaces, separable or not.

**H.19.4 Finite-ancilla purity implies infinite-ancilla purity [PROVED (paper); PROVED (Lean) in Round 13, H.20.1].** *Proposition.* Let V be any process on finite P with binary parties, the setting of `IsPure`. (For general finite I_k = O_k the argument is the same.) If V is pure against all finite ancillas, then for all ancilla index sets κ_k and all unitary kernels a_k, `compose V a` has orthonormal columns and rows. Hence `IsUnitaryKer V` holds, and so does `IsPureHilbert V` by H.19.3.

*Proof.*
1. *The column Gram entry.* Fix columns c = (π, α) and c' = (π', α'). Their inner product is

       Σ_φ Σ_{o,e,o',e'} conj V(e,φ; o,π) V(e',φ; o',π') ∏_k g_k(o_k, e_k; o'_k, e'_k),

   where g_k(x, y; x', y') = Σ_{b ∈ κ_k} conj a_k((x,b),(y,α_k)) a_k((x',b),(y',α'_k)) = ⟨(⟨x| ⊗ 1) A_k|y,α_k⟩, (⟨x'| ⊗ 1) A_k|y',α'_k⟩⟩_{K_k}. These series converge absolutely and do not depend on the basis.
2. *A finite-dimensional subspace.* The Gram entry depends only on the finitely many vectors A_k|y,α_k⟩ and A_k|y,α'_k⟩, y ∈ Bool. Let L_k ⊂ K_k be the finite-dimensional span of e_{α_k}, e_{α'_k} and the components (⟨x| ⊗ 1)A_k|y,α_k⟩ and (⟨x| ⊗ 1)A_k|y,α'_k⟩.
3. *Extension to a unitary.* A_k maps C² ⊗ span(e_{α_k}, e_{α'_k}) isometrically into C² ⊗ L_k. An isometry between subspaces of a finite-dimensional space extends to a unitary B_k of C² ⊗ L_k, since the orthogonal complements have equal dimension.
4. *Applying finite purity.* B_k is a local unitary with a finite ancilla. Use an orthonormal basis of L_k containing e_{α_k} and e_{α'_k}; these coincide or are orthogonal. The corresponding column Gram entry of compose V B is built from the same g_k, so it equals the entry for A. Since V is pure against finite ancillas, it is δ_{cc'}.
5. *Rows.* A row Gram entry involves only finitely many rows of the a_k, i.e. the vectors A_k†|x,β_k⟩ and A_k†|x,β'_k⟩. The same construction applies with A_k† in place of A_k.
6. *Conclusion.* `unitary_of_unitaryKernel` turns the unitary kernel into a bounded unitary. ∎

The converse (infinite-ancilla purity implies finite-ancilla purity) is `IsPureKer.isPure` in Lean. So for every finite V, purity against finite ancillas, purity against unitary kernels and purity against arbitrary Hilbert ancillas are equivalent; the first implication was on paper only in Round 12; Round 13 formalises it (`isPureHilbert_of_isPure`, H.20.1). The Switchboard does not need the proposition, because `isPureHilbert_Vsb` is proved directly.

**H.19.5 The Choi form with infinite ancillas [PROVED (Lean) for the Kraus/kernel form; OPEN as an operator link product].** W stays a finite matrix; only the local Choi operators become infinite. For a channel on C² ⊗ ℓ²(X_k), the Choi operator is not trace class, since its partial trace is the identity. So W ∗ J cannot be defined as a trace of a product of trace-class operators. This is the obstruction of Appendix A/F, and Lean uses the kernel form instead.
- On countable ℓ², every normal CPTP map is a Kraus channel (`isNormalCPTP_iff_kraus`).
- Every Choi kernel whose finite sections are PSD and which satisfies the trace-preservation condition is the Choi kernel of a Kraus kernel (`exists_kraus_of_choi`).
- `isIsoKernel_compose2_Vsb`: the closed loop of Vsb with isometric local kernels Bool × X_k → Bool × Y_k has orthonormal columns. The input and output ancillas may differ and may have any cardinality.
- `valid_normalCPTP_Vsb`: take normal CPTP local channels Φ_k : B(ℓ²(Bool × X_k)) → B(ℓ²(Bool × X'_k)) with countable X_k and X'_k. Each Φ_k is a Kraus channel with kernel V_k. The closed loop `krausLoop V` is the link product of Vsb with the Kraus kernels; its entries are finite sums over the Switchboard indices. By `isIsoKernel_krausLoop` it is an isometric Kraus kernel, so the induced global map is normal CPTP. In finite dimensions this is `isValidPM_choiMat_Vsb` restricted to those channels.
- *Not formalised:* (i) the closed loop's Choi kernel as a convergent series W ∗ J for non-trace-class J (this was done for process-function lifts in BornPairing.lean, but not for the Switchboard); (ii) channels on uncountable ancillas, since the repository's Kraus theorem needs countable alphabets. Round 13: (ii) is closed (`valid_normalCPTP_Vsb_any`), and (i) is replaced by an entrywise link product over ancillas of any cardinality (H.20.4).

**H.19.6 Status.**
- PROVED (Lean): H.19.1 lemmas, H.19.2, H.19.3, and the H.19.5 kernel statements.
- PROVED (paper): H.19.4.
- CITED: AFNB Def. 1 and Thm 2 [17]; the CDP link product [49].
- INTERPRETIVE: identifying "Hilbert space with a Hilbert basis indexed by Bool × κ" with C² ⊗ K.
- OPEN (formal): items (a)–(d) of Section 9. *Round 13 update:* all four are closed in Lean with the caveats of H.20.7; H.19.4 is PROVED (Lean), and the INTERPRETIVE item is upgraded for H_k (H.20.5).

### H.20 Round 13: closing the Round 12 formal gaps (Oct 24 2026) [labels: PROVED (Lean) / CITED / INTERPRETIVE / OPEN per item]

Lean files: lean/PFUL/Compression.lean, PureFiniteInf.lean, AFNBPure.lean, KrausAny.lean, InfiniteLink.lean, QubitTensor.lean, CounterexampleInfLink.lean (lean/STATUS.md Round 13). There is no `sorry`, no `native_decide` and no new axiom. `#print axioms` gives only propext, Classical.choice and Quot.sound for every theorem named below (lean/Round13_axioms.lean, lean/Round13_axioms.log). Gaps are labelled as in Section 9 (Round 12 version): (a) infinite-ancilla link product, (b) H.19.4 in Lean, (c) AFNB Thm 2, (d) normal CPTP with uncountable ancillas.

**H.20.1 Gap (b): finite purity implies purity against Hilbert ancillas, for every V [PROVED (Lean)].** This is the proof of H.19.4, formalised.
- `exists_finite_compression σ a ha α α'`. Let a be a kernel on (O × κ) ← (I × κ) with orthonormal columns, with I ≃ O finite and κ of any cardinality, and let α, α' ∈ κ. Then there are a finite type n, a unitary matrix B on (O × n) ← (I × n) and i, j ∈ n with i = j ↔ α = α', such that Σ_b conj a(x,b)(y,α) a(x',b)(y',α') = Σ_b conj B(x,b)(y,i) B(x',b)(y',j) for all x, y, x', y'. Construction: take coordinates of the finitely many vectors a(x,·)(y,α), a(x,·)(y,α') in an orthonormal basis of their span in ℓ²(κ), pad by two ancilla levels for i and j, and extend the resulting orthonormal family to an orthonormal basis (steps 2–3 of H.19.4).
- `isPureKer_of_isPure`, `isUnitaryKer_of_isPure`, `isPureHilbert_of_isPure : IsPure V → IsPureHilbert V`. The column Gram entry of `compose V a` depends on each a_k only through these cross Gram numbers, so it equals the corresponding entry of `compose V B`, which is δ_{cc'} by finite purity. Rows follow by applying the same argument to V† and a† (`compose_conjTranspose`).
- `isPure_iff_isPureKer`, `isPure_iff_isUnitaryKer`, `isPure_iff_isPureHilbert`: for every V : Proc P P, purity against finite ancillas, against isometric kernels, against unitary kernels and against arbitrary Hilbert ancillas are equivalent.

**H.20.2 Gap (c): AFNB Theorem 2 in the process-matrix framework [PROVED (Lean) for I_k ≃ O_k; CITED for the general statement [17, Thm 2]].**
- `exists_unitary_of_unitaryLink σ : UnitaryLink W → ∃ U, IsUnitaryM U ∧ W = choiPM U`, where choiPM U = |U⟩⟩⟨⟨U| for U : P × ∏O_k → F × ∏I_k. The proof is AFNB's: close each slot k with the unitary that swaps the wire with a copy of it in the ancilla (X_k = I_k). The link is then |U⟩⟩⟨⟨U| up to the relabelling σ.
- `unitaryLink_choiPM (hPF : |P| = |F|) U : IsValidPM (choiPM U) → UnitaryLink (choiPM U)`. A link of rank-one matrices is rank one (`link_rankOne`), so W ∗ |A⟩⟩⟨⟨A| = |M⟩⟩⟨⟨M|. Trace preservation of the link (validity) gives M†M = 1, and M is square because |P| = |F|, so MM† = 1.
- `isPurePM_iff σ hPF W : IsPurePM W ↔ IsValidPM W ∧ ∃ U, IsUnitaryM U ∧ W = choiPM U`. The parties are indexed by any finite type, so this is the multipartite form; AFNB state the theorem bipartite and note that the multipartite generalisation is straightforward (Appendix H audit, ref. 17).
- *Hypotheses.* `UnitaryLink` uses one ancilla X_k on both sides of party k, so the SWAP construction needs σ k : I_k ≃ O_k. AFNB allow different input and output ancillas, which removes this restriction there. Validity is a hypothesis on the right-hand side, as in AFNB.

**H.20.3 Gap (d): Kraus theorem and normal-CPTP validity without countability [PROVED (Lean)].** The countability hypotheses of the Round 12 Kraus theorem entered only through a Gram–Schmidt construction of a Hilbert basis along an enumeration. Round 13 replaces this step with Mathlib's `exists_hilbertBasis` (Zorn's lemma).
- `KernelPSD.exists_factor_any` and `exists_kraus_of_choi_any`: a Choi kernel on alphabets of any cardinality, with PSD finite sections and the trace-preservation condition, is the Choi kernel of an isometric Kraus kernel.
- `exists_kraus_of_normalCPTP_any`, `isNormalCPTP_iff_kraus_any`: on ℓ²(A) and ℓ²(O) for arbitrary A, O, a map is normal CPTP iff it is a Kraus channel.
- `valid_normalCPTP_Vsb_any`: `valid_normalCPTP_Vsb` with no `Countable` hypothesis on X_k, X'_k.

**H.20.4 Gap (a): a link product with ancillas of any cardinality [PROVED (Lean); entrywise, not an operator identity].** The entries of W ∗ C are finite sums over the wires of W (H.19.1), so the link product is defined for local Choi kernels C_k on ancilla types X_k, Y_k of any cardinality. Only positivity and trace preservation need care.
- `IsCPTPKer J`: J is PSD in Mathlib's sense (every finite section is PSD), and Σ_b J((a,b),(a',b)) = δ_{aa'} as an unconditional sum. `isCPTPKer_iff` shows that it agrees with `IsCPTPChoi` when the output ancilla is finite.
- `posSemidef_of_sections` and `link_posSemidef_any`: a link of PSD kernels is PSD, for any ancillas, by restricting to the finitely many ancilla values in a finite section.
- `psd_norm_le`: |J(p,q)| ≤ (J(p,p) + J(q,q))/2 for PSD J. Together with `hasSum_pi_prod_any`, this makes the partial-trace sums of the link absolutely convergent. `reduceKer` and `isCPTPChoi_reduceKer` restrict the input ancilla to the relevant values and trace out the output ancilla, which gives a finite CPTP Choi matrix with the same link entries. This reduces validity to the finite case.
- `IsValidPMInf W`: W ⪰ 0 and W ∗ C is a CPTP kernel for all ancilla types X, Y and all CPTP kernels C_k. **`isValidPMInf_iff W : IsValidPMInf W ↔ IsValidPM W`**, for every finite W, with no further hypothesis.
- `UnitaryLinkInf W`: for all ancilla types X_k and all unitary kernels A_k (orthonormal rows and columns), W ∗ (|A_k⟩⟩⟨⟨A_k|)_k = |M⟩⟩⟨⟨M| with M a unitary kernel. `IsPurePMInf W := IsValidPMInf W ∧ UnitaryLinkInf W`. **`isPurePMInf_iff σ W : IsPurePMInf W ↔ IsPurePM W`**. For ⇐, AFNB Thm 2 (H.20.2) writes W = |U⟩⟩⟨⟨U|. Then W ∗ |A⟩⟩⟨⟨A| = |lmat U A⟩⟩⟨⟨lmat U A| (`link_choiPM_vecOp`), and lmat U A is a unitary kernel by the compression of H.20.1 (`isIsoKernel_lmat`, `isUnitaryKer_lmat`).
- `isValidPMInf_Wcl_iff`, `isPurePMInf_choiMat_Vsb`, and **`purification_conjecture_false_choiInf`**: Theorem H.12.1 with the predicates `IsValidPMInf` and `IsPurePMInf` in place of `IsValidPM` and `IsPurePM`.
- *What this is not.* For a channel with an infinite-dimensional ancilla the Choi operator is not trace class (H.19.5), and Lean does not define W ∗ J as an operator. The statement is about kernels: matrices indexed by arbitrary types, with PSD finite sections and unconditional sums. Unitary kernels are bounded unitaries by `unitary_of_unitaryKernel`. CPTP kernels correspond, via H.20.3, to Kraus channels, i.e. normal CPTP maps on ℓ². These are the two operator links, but the identity "W ∗ J as an operator" is not stated [OPEN as an operator statement].

**H.20.5 C² ⊗ K_k as a tensor product [PROVED (Lean) for the local factor; INTERPRETIVE for the joint space].**
- `qsplitEquiv`: Mathlib's algebraic tensor product C² ⊗[ℂ] K with its tensor inner product is isometrically isomorphic to K ⊕ K (`PiLp 2`). Hence it is complete (`completeSpace_qubitTensor`), even when K is infinite-dimensional.
- `qubitBasis B`: a Hilbert basis of K indexed by κ gives a Hilbert basis e_b ⊗ B_j of C² ⊗[ℂ] K indexed by Bool × κ (`qubitBasis_apply`).
- `isPure_qubitTensor`: for every pure V, Hilbert spaces K_k of any dimension, unitaries A_k of C² ⊗[ℂ] K_k, and any Hilbert space G with a Hilbert basis indexed by P × ∏κ_k, the link product is the matrix of a unitary of G.
- So the identification "H_k = C² ⊗ K_k" of H.19.3 is now a theorem rather than an interpretation. The joint space G = C^P ⊗ K_1 ⊗ K_2 ⊗ K_3 is still modelled as "any Hilbert space with a basis indexed by P × ∏κ_k" [INTERPRETIVE]. The precise obstruction is that Mathlib has no completed Hilbert tensor product, and the algebraic K_1 ⊗ K_2 ⊗ K_3 is not complete when two of the factors are infinite-dimensional. So the basis description cannot be replaced by Mathlib's tensor product there, as it can for C² ⊗ K_k.

**H.20.6 The Round 9 boundedness gap [OPEN, not attempted beyond scoping].** Round 11 proved automatic boundedness for unitary kernels (`unitary_of_unitaryKernel`). The remaining Round 8/9 gap is different. `IsNormalCPTP` assumes trace-norm boundedness, and a positive trace-preserving linear map on the trace class should be bounded automatically. On paper: ‖Φ(T)‖₁ = Tr Φ(T₊) + Tr Φ(T₋) ≤ ‖T‖₁ for self-adjoint T = T₊ − T₋, hence ‖Φ‖ ≤ 2. The Lean obstruction is the Jordan decomposition of a self-adjoint trace-class operator with ‖T‖₁ = Tr T₊ + Tr T₋. This needs the spectral theorem for compact self-adjoint operators on an infinite-dimensional Hilbert space, which Mathlib does not have. *Correction (Round 15, H.22.4): Mathlib does have it (`orthogonalComplement_iSup_eigenspaces_eq_bot`), and Schmidt.lean already uses it; the gap is unformalised but not obstructed.* The repository's trace class is the span of products of Hilbert–Schmidt operators, and does not provide this decomposition. *Closed (Round 16, H.23.2): the Jordan decomposition comes from Mathlib's continuous functional calculus (S^± = ½(|S| ± S)), and `IsCPTP.isNormalCPTP` is proved; the status label above is superseded.*

**H.20.7 Status.**
- PROVED (Lean): H.20.1 (gap b), H.20.2 under σ and |P| = |F| (gap c), H.20.3 (gap d), H.20.4 in kernel form (gap a), and H.20.5 for the local factor.
- CITED: AFNB Thm 2 [17] in its general form (unequal local dimensions).
- INTERPRETIVE: the joint space G as C^P ⊗ ⨂K_k.
- OPEN: an operator-level link product for non-trace-class Choi operators; AFNB Thm 2 without σ, and its converse without |P| = |F|; automatic boundedness of positive TP maps (H.20.6; closed in Round 16, H.23.2).

### H.21 Round 14: an explicit spacetime circuit and a routed graph for H.12, and further open items (Oct 24 2026) [labels: PROVED = this paper (exact arithmetic where stated); NUMERICAL = floating-point check; CITED = read in the arXiv primary source (IDs checked against the arXiv API); INTERPRETIVE; OPEN]

H.18.6 left two things open for H.12: an explicit circuit on time-delocalised subsystems (TDS), and a valid routed graph. Both are now given. The key is a closed form for H.12 (H.21.2): up to local unitaries it is the time reverse of the Lugano purification in which Bob's bit flip X is replaced by its square root √X. Code: code/stochastic/cyclic/embed/tds_h12.py, tds_h12.log, rg_h12.py, rg_h12.log.

**H.21.1 Phase form, and WBO's general construction run concretely [phase form PROVED, exact; circuit NUMERICAL to 4·10⁻¹⁴].**
- *Phase form [PROVED, exact integer check].* Put Hadamard frames Hd on the past P and on the agents' inputs. Then H.12 becomes a *phase process* D: |z⟩_P|o⟩_{AO} ↦ Φ(o, z)|z⟩_{AI}|o⟩_F, with V_{H12} = (Hd)_{AI} D (Hd)_P and
  Φ(o, z) = i^{pre(z)} · (−1)^{o_A(z_B ∨ z_C)} · i^{o_B z_A z_C} · (−1)^{o_C z_B(1−z_A)},  pre(z) = 3z_C + 2z_A + z_Bz_C + 3z_Az_C + z_Az_B + 2z_Az_Bz_C (mod 4).
  In words: the agents' inputs are a copy of the (rotated) past, and each agent's output only adds a phase that depends on the *other two* agents' inputs. There are no self terms. This is the cyclic dependency of H.12 written in closed form.
- *WBO's proof, run concretely [NUMERICAL].* WBO prove Prop. 1 (H.18.2) by fixing U_C and decomposing the remaining bipartite process into an "A before B" and a "B before A" part. We carried this out numerically for H.12:
  - Fix U_C (a qubit unitary with a qubit ancilla c′). V∗U_C is a bipartite unitary process with past P16 = (P, c′).
  - Its *sector algebra* is the set of operators X on P16 such that X ⊗ 1 commutes with every operator on A_I B_I pulled back through the process. It has dimension 8 and a two-dimensional centre (it is M₂ ⊕ M₂). Its central projectors split P16 into an 8-dimensional "A first" sector and an 8-dimensional "B first" sector, as in Barrett–Lorenz–Oreshkov and Yokojima et al. (refs. 43, 45). The algebra depends on U_C: two Haar-random U_C share only the identity.
  - Matrix units of the M₂ algebra in each sector give WBO's isometries ν₁, ν₂, ν₃. The middle registers have dimension 4 in both sectors, so WBO's padding qubits are not needed.
  - The circuit is: ω₁ = [ν₁ for A-first; ν₁ for B-first] writing a control qubit Q; slot 1 (U_A if Q = 0, U_B if Q = 1); ω₂ (block-diagonal in Q); slot 2 (the other agent); ω₃. U_C enters only through the ω's.
  - *Checks (20 Haar draws of all three agent unitaries with qubit ancillas).* The circuit equals the cyclic composition Cyc(V_{H12}; U_A, U_B, U_C) to 2.0·10⁻¹⁴. The ω's are unitary to 2.7·10⁻¹⁵. WBO's identity for C's time-delocalised subsystem (V = U1 ∗ id ∗ U2 with |Z| = 16, and U2†·[ω-chain]·U1† = U_C ⊗ 1_Z) holds to 3.0·10⁻¹⁴.
  - This realisation is explicit but unstructured: the ω's are computed matrices that depend on U_C as a whole, which Prop. 1 allows. H.21.2 gives a structured one.

**H.21.2 H.12 is a frame-rotated time reverse of a √X-Lugano process [PROVED, exact Gaussian-integer check].**
- *Definition (√X-Lugano process).* Let G = √X = h S h = ½[[1+i, 1−i], [1−i, 1+i]], with h the qubit Hadamard and S = diag(1, i). G is symmetric and G² = X. With lug(a, b, c) = ((1−b)c, (1−c)a, (1−a)b) as in H.15.7e, set
  L_G: |s⟩_P|o⟩_{AO} ↦ (X^{lug_A(o)} ⊗ G^{lug_B(o)} ⊗ X^{lug_C(o)})|s⟩_{AI} ⊗ |o⟩_F.
  This is the Lugano purification L of H.15.7e.2 with Bob's flip X replaced by G. More generally write L_U for any qubit unitary U in Bob's place, so L = L_X.
- **Theorem H.21.2.** V_{H12} = (Hd X_C)_{AI} (Hd X_A)_F · L_G^T · (Hd)_{AO} (X_C Pre Hd)_P, where Pre = diag(i^{pre(z)}) is a phase on the past and Hd = h^{⊗3}.
  - *Proof.* rg_h12.py checks the identity exactly: all entries of 8·V_{H12} and of 128·RHS/16 are Gaussian integers, and they agree entry by entry. By hand: substitute z_C → 1 − z_C in the phase form of H.21.1. The output phases become (−1)^{o_A f_A(z)}, i^{o_B f_B(z)} and (−1)^{o_C f_C(z)}, with f_A = 1 ⊕ lug_A, f_B = lug_B and f_C = lug_C. (For example z_B ∨ (1 − z_C) = 1 − (1 − z_B) z_C = 1 ⊕ lug_A(z).) In the Hadamard frame on an output wire, a phase (−1)^{o f} is the gate X^f and a phase i^{o f} is the gate G^f. Collecting the constant flip from f_A, the substitution and the Hadamards gives the stated factors. ∎
  - *Consequence.* H.12 and H.13.2 are the same construction: H.13.2 is the frame-rotated time reverse of L_X (H.15.7e.2), and H.12 that of L_{√X}. This **corrects H.18.6**, which said, on the basis of H.15.8, that the explicit H.13.2 construction "does not carry over" to H.12. H.15.8 itself stays true: H.12 is not a phased permutation in the Hadamard frame, because G is not a permutation. But the construction does carry over once Bob's flip may be any unitary.
  - *Consistency with H.15.9(A) [PROVED, by the same invariant].* L_G is not a PF purification, so H.21.2 agrees with the NUMERICAL finding that H.12 is LU-equivalent to no L_g or L_g^T with g a PF.

**H.21.3 An explicit TDS circuit for H.12 with time-local instances only [NUMERICAL to 7·10⁻¹⁶; construction PROVED as in H.18.4(c)].**
- *Forward circuit for L_G.* Take WBO's circuit for the Baumeler–Wolf process (H.18.4(b), tds_h13.py) and change one gate: in ω₂, branch Q = 0, the CNOT that writes Bob's flip becomes a controlled-G. Every other gate is unchanged, so U_C enters only through its two time-local instances, U_C (in ω₁) and U_C σ_x U_C† (in ω₃). *Check:* the circuit equals Cyc(L_G; U_A, U_B, U_C) for 20 Haar draws, to 4.0·10⁻¹⁶.
- *Reversal.* By H.18.4(c), the transposed circuit in reverse order realises L_G^T. Absorb the local factors of Theorem H.21.2 into the agents, W̃_X = h W_X (h X^{[X = C]}), and into a fixed preparation on P (X_C Pre Hd) and readout on F (Hd X_A). *Check:* the result equals Cyc(V_{H12}; W_A, W_B, W_C) for 20 Haar draws, to 6.8·10⁻¹⁶.
- **So H.12 runs as an ordinary acyclic quantum circuit with each agent acting once on a time-delocalised subsystem, by an explicit circuit of the same shape as H.13.2's.** The placement in Minkowski space of H.18.5 applies verbatim.
- *Reading [INTERPRETIVE].* The only non-classical gate in the bare circuit is the controlled-√X. H.13.2's bare circuit is a classical permutation circuit, and all its quantumness sits in the frame changes. H.12 needs one genuinely quantum gate inside the loop as well.

**H.21.4 A valid routed graph for H.12 [PROVED, with one CITED step].**
- *The graph.* Take VOKB's routed graph for Lugano (their Fig. 27, ref. 18) and reverse it, as in Corollary H.15.7e.3. rg_h12.py re-encodes it: univocal, adjoint univocal, branches A, B, C: 2 each, X: 5, F: 1; 38 branch-graph arrows; the weak-loops principle holds. So it is valid in VOKB's sense.
- *The fleshing-out.* In VOKB's fleshing-out of Fig. 27, replace node B's CNOT (from l to p_B) by a controlled-G. The routes do not change, because the register p_B and Bob's input are not indexed in the graph (they sit in the agents' unindexed sectors). Exact check in Gaussian integers: each agent output has a unique consistent assignment, the F record determines the agents' outputs, and the composite is exactly L_G (and its transpose exactly L_G^T).
- *CITED step.* VOKB say explicitly (arXiv:2206.10042v3, Sec. 4.3) that the Lugano graph can be fleshed out with "arbitrary operations (as long as they follow the routes)", giving "a larger family of processes". So L_G is a member of VOKB's own family [CITED], and its validity follows from their theorem that fleshing out a valid routed graph gives a valid process. After the local unitaries of Theorem H.21.2, this is a valid routed circuit for H.12.
- *What is ours.* Only the identification of H.12 with this member of VOKB's family (Theorem H.21.2) and the exact checks. We do not claim the √X-Lugano process as a new object; we found no statement that it, or any member of VOKB's family other than Lugano, is purifiable-but-not-a-PF-blend in a classical frame.
- *Conjecture 1 of VOKB (H.15.7c).* Both counterexamples, H.12 and H.13.2, are now fleshings-out of valid routed graphs. So neither is a counterexample to VOKB's Conjecture 1. Theorem H.15.7d and Conjecture 2 predict that any valid graph for H.12 has loops in its branch graph, all of them weak. Our checker confirms the second half for this graph (every loop it finds is weak); it does not tabulate the loops, so the first half is not checked here.

**H.21.5 Status of the H.12 spacetime items.**
- PROVED: the phase form (H.21.1); Theorem H.21.2 (exact); the routed graph of H.21.4, with VOKB's fleshing-out theorem and family CITED.
- NUMERICAL: the generic WBO circuit (H.21.1, to 4·10⁻¹⁴) and the structured circuit (H.21.3, to 7·10⁻¹⁶).
- This closes the H.12 items left OPEN in H.18.6, H.18.7 and H.15.7c–e.

**H.21.6 Classical fine-grained models for H.13.2: the question splits in two [both parts PROVED; elementary].** H.15.7b left OPEN whether a *classical* fine-grained model with multiple use, or with communicating sub-agents (VR Thm 5), reproduces H.13.2. H.18.5 answered the quantum half. The classical half turns out to depend entirely on what "multiple use" allows.
- **(a) Unrestricted multiple use: yes, for every valid classical process [PROVED].** Let p(i|o) be any valid three-party classical process (a point of Valid; it need not be purifiable). Let each agent k use any classical instrument T_k(a_k, o_k | i_k, x_k), with setting x_k and outcome a_k. The following acyclic classical network reproduces the joint statistics Σ_o p(i|o) Π_k T_k(a_k, o_k | i_k, x_k) exactly:
  1. For each agent k, two sub-agents k⁰ and k¹ run the agent's instrument once each, with the same setting x_k, on the fixed inputs 0 and 1, with independent local randomness. Write (a_k^j, o_k^j) for the result of sub-agent k^j.
  2. A central node N reads the six outputs o_k^j. They define a deterministic intervention h_k(j) = o_k^j. N samples i with probability P(i | h) = Σ_o p(i|o) Π_k [o_k = h_k(i_k)].
  3. N announces i_k as agent k's input. Agent k's reported output and outcome are those of sub-agent k^{i_k}, the one that actually received input i_k. The other sub-agent's run is discarded.
  - *Proof.* P(· | h) is a probability distribution for every deterministic h: that is exactly the definition of Valid (fixed-point probability 1 for every deterministic local intervention). Summing over the discarded sub-agents' results, which are independent of everything else, leaves Σ_o p(i|o) Π_k T_k(a_k, o_k | i_k, x_k). The network is acyclic: sub-agents, then N. ∎
  - So the H.15.7b question has the answer "yes" in its unrestricted classical form, for H.13.2 and H.12 and for every valid process, including the non-purifiable E_ex1 (C.2). In particular the causal-inequality violations of H.15.7b and H.15.7d are reproduced, since they use classical instruments. The answer therefore says nothing about purity. Each agent's operation is applied twice, and which application counts is decided afterwards by N.
  - *Novelty.* None claimed. This is the standard trick of querying a classical operation on every input value. We expect it is folklore, but we did not find it stated for process functions or classical processes.
- **(b) Reversible single use on delocalised classical variables: no [PROVED, from D.6].** The classical analogue of a realisation on TDS (H.18.1) is a reversible classical circuit with a random source s, whose agents' operations are bijections h_k (with ancillas) and which, for each value of s, realises a classical reversible process W_s, i.e. the closed loop is a bijection for every choice of h. Then for each s the map o ↦ i of W_s is a process function, because a bijective closed loop needs exactly one consistent assignment for every local function. So the induced classical process is Σ_s q(s) w_s ∈ conv(PF), which is Proposition D.6 (and Barrett–Lorenz–Oreshkov Thm 6, ref. 45). H.13.2 and H.12 are outside conv(PF), so neither has such a model. The non-classicality of the H.18.4 and H.21.3 circuits is therefore necessary.
- **(c) What remains OPEN.** Intermediate notions: a classical model in which each agent's operation is applied once on a delocalised classical variable, but the circuit may be stochastic and irreversible (so that (b) does not apply), and in which the "post-selection" of (a) is excluded. We have not formulated such a notion precisely, so we do not claim an answer. *Update (Round 15, H.22.3):* counting time-local runs instead, the minimum budget for H.13.2 and H.12 is exactly four (one agent twice, the others once, in a dynamically chosen order); the version without any discarding stays OPEN.

**H.21.7 A continuous path of pure boards from conv(PF) through H.12 to H.13.2 [purity PROVED with one CITED step and NUMERICAL; violations NUMERICAL; h12_family.py, h12_family.log].**
- *The family.* For φ ∈ [0, π] let G_φ = ½(1 + e^{iφ})·1 + ½(1 − e^{iφ})·X, so G_0 = 1, G_{π/2} = √X and G_π = X. Let V_φ be the right-hand side of Theorem H.21.2 with L_{G_φ} in place of L_G. Then V_{π/2} = V_{H12} (checked to 3·10⁻¹⁶). In the Hadamard frame G_φ is diag(1, e^{iφ}), so every V_φ is an X-type board with continuous phases.
- *Purity [PROVED with one CITED step; NUMERICAL check].* L_{G_φ} is a fleshing-out of VOKB's Lugano graph (H.21.4), so it is a valid unitary process [CITED, ref. 18, Sec. 4.3]; local unitaries and time reversal keep this. *Check:* for 25 values of φ and 5 Haar draws each, the closed loop is unitary to 3·10⁻¹⁵.
- *Classical restrictions [NUMERICAL].* Read V_φ in the computational frame and maximise each facet violation of conv(PF) over source states, as in H.13–H.14:
  - φ = 0: no facet is violated (the process lies in conv(PF)).
  - Every φ > 0 on the grid (steps of π/24) violates some facet. The maximum relative violation grows monotonically with φ: 0.0107 at φ = π/24, 0.1036 = (√2 − 1)/4 at φ = π/2 (the H.12 value of H.15.9(B)), and 0.1404 at φ = π (the H.13.2 value of H.15.8). The maximum is always on orbit 91.
  - The number of violated orbits grows from 20 (small φ) through 40 (φ = π/2) to 60, then falls to 56 at φ = π.
  - Over the whole path, 60 orbits are violated. The 33 that never are consist of the 31 orbits that Z₄ boards never violate (H.13.4) plus orbits 85 and 90. So nothing on the path violates any of the 22 genuine never-crossed orbits (H.17.5) or the 8 orbits reached only by Z₈ boards (H.14.2).
  - Orbit 82 contains the H.12 certificate facet 1741. Its maximum on the path rises from 0 to 0.0800 at φ = π/2 and to 0.1036 at φ = π, which is its Z₄ maximum (J.3).
- *Reading.* H.12 and H.13.2 are two points on one continuous path of pure processes, which leaves conv(PF) as soon as the gate in Bob's place stops being the identity [NUMERICAL on the grid; small φ not resolved below π/24].
- *What this does and does not say about Purif (open item H.13/H.14.4).* It adds a new, continuous source of evidence that the 22 orbits might be valid on Purif, but the family is X-type, so it does not test non-X-type boards. *Update (Round 15, H.22.2): that caveat was decisive. With frames on the output wires, the endpoint process (time-reversed Lugano) crosses ten of the 22 orbits, a generalised Lugano board two more; 10 remain open.* A smooth curve inside a convex set says nothing about whether the set is a polytope, so the polytope question stays **OPEN**.

**H.21.8 The H.12 bound gap: a lifted relaxation narrows it to [−1.27720, −0.70580] [lower bound PROVED, exact integer certificate; seesaw NUMERICAL; h12_lift.py, h12_lift_dual.py, h12_lift_verify.py, h12_lift_common.py and logs; h12_seesaw2.py, h12_seesaw2.log].**
- *Lifted-A relaxation (defined here).* In the NS relaxation of H.15.9(C), each setting triple x gets its own operator family, so nothing forces A's two POVMs to exist together. The lifted relaxation puts A's two POVMs on two copies A, A′ of A's qubit. The variables are Z_v ≥ 0 on A⊗A′⊗B⊗C (16×16), with v = (c_{A|0}, c_{A|1}, x_B, x_C, c_B, c_C). They satisfy the following constraints:
  - for each (x_B, x_C), the sum over all outcomes is 1;
  - summing out c_{A|1} (respectively c_{A|0}) leaves an operator of the form X ⊗ 1_{A′} (respectively 1_A ⊗ X);
  - the NS constraints for B and for C, as in H.15.9(C).
  The x_A = 0 statistics are read on A and the x_A = 1 statistics on A′.
  - Every product E^A_{·|0} ⊗ E^A_{·|1} ⊗ E^B_{·|x_B} ⊗ E^C_{·|x_C} is feasible, and its objective equals y_A·P. This was checked on random POVM triples (agreement to 10⁻¹²). So with the H.15.7d reduction, the minimum is a lower bound over all instruments.
  - The relaxation is at least as tight as NS, because every feasible point projects to an NS-feasible family.
- *Numerical values [NUMERICAL, SCS].* The primal gives −1.277228 (1,200 iterations, residuals ≤ 3·10⁻⁵). The independently formulated dual gives −1.2771490.
- **Certified bound [PROVED; exact integer arithmetic].** For every choice of instruments, y_A·P(H.12) ≥ −41851085693392143/32768000000000000 ≈ −1.2771938.
  - *Certificate.* For any Hermitian multipliers Λ_{x_B x_C} (normalisation), G (the twirl constraints, through G − T_q G with T_q the Pauli twirl of qubit q) and D (B, C setting independence), the following identity holds on feasible Z: y_A·P = Σ Tr Λ + Σ_v Tr[S_v Z_v]. Hence y_A·P ≥ Σ Tr Λ + 16 Σ_{x_B,x_C} min_v t_v whenever S_v − t_v·1 ≥ 0.
  - *Rounding.* The SCS multipliers are rounded to Gaussian dyadics with denominator 2³⁰ and made exactly Hermitian, and the twirls are applied exactly. The cost matrices (entries in ℚ(i,√2)) are replaced by dyadic matrices from 60-digit evaluation, with an error contribution below 10⁻¹⁵; 10⁻¹² is subtracted to cover it.
  - *Positivity check.* Then 2⁶⁴·S_v is a Gaussian-integer matrix. For each of the 1,024 v, a dyadic t_v is certified by Sylvester's criterion on the 32×32 real form: every leading principal minor is positive, computed exactly by Bareiss elimination. The worst t_v is −8.9·10⁻⁷, so rounding costs about 6·10⁻⁵.
- **Achievable side [NUMERICAL].** 120 further seesaw restarts (seeds 101 and 102, random POVM and random rank-1 projective starts) reach at best −0.6597, the same local optimum as before (H.15.9(C)), and −0.4832. None goes below −0.70580.
- **Status.** The optimum of y_A over all instruments lies in **[−1.27720, −0.70580]** [PROVED both ends]. *Update (Round 15, H.22.1): lifting B instead of A certifies y_A ≥ −1.20330, so the interval is now [−1.20330, −0.70580]. Round 16, H.23.3: [−1.16564, −0.70580].* This improves the lower end from −1.58556 (NS+PPT) to −1.27720. Closing the gap remains **OPEN**. Natural next steps are the lifted relaxation with PPT constraints (about five times the memory of this run, beyond the box), lifting B and C as well, and an upper-side search beyond seesaw.
- *Novelty.* None is claimed for the method. Requiring one party's measurements for both settings to exist on copies is the idea behind symmetric-extension and inflation-type relaxations. Only the numbers for H.12 are new.

### H.22 Round 15: the H.12 gap, the never-crossed orbits, and classical models with bounded reuse (Oct 24 2026) [labels as in H.21]

**H.22.1 The H.12 gap: lifting B certifies y_A ≥ −1.20330; lifting C and a structured search do not help [lower bound PROVED, exact integer certificate; the rest NUMERICAL; h12_lift_common.py, h12_lift_dual.py, h12_lift_verify.py with LIFTP=1, h12_lift_dual_B.log/.pkl, h12_lift_verify_B.log, h12_lift_dual_C_partial.log; h12_struct_search.py, h12_struct_search.log].**
- *Lift-B and lift-C (defined here).* The lifted relaxation of H.21.8 copies one party's qubit so that the POVMs for both of its settings exist together. H.21.8 lifted A. Setting the environment variable LIFTP = 1 (or 2) relabels the parties exactly, so that B (or C) takes the lifted position; the objective y_A and the board are relabelled with them, and nothing else changes. The verifier relabels its exact 2⁻⁶³-grid copy of the board in the same way and checks it against the float board to 10⁻¹⁴. Each lift is a valid relaxation by the argument of H.21.8, so each gives its own lower bound, and the best one counts.
- **Certified bound [PROVED; exact integer arithmetic, as in H.21.8].** For every choice of instruments, y_A·P(H.12) ≥ −315437701416277769/262144000000000000 ≈ **−1.2032993**.
  - The SCS dual of lift-B reached −1.2032564 (status optimal, 689 s). After rounding to Gaussian dyadics and exact twirling, the four worst t_v are between −5.96·10⁻⁷ and −7.15·10⁻⁷, all certified by Sylvester's criterion with Bareiss elimination. The rounding costs about 4·10⁻⁵.
- *Lift-C [NUMERICAL, not certified].* The lift-C dual was stopped after 1,750 SCS iterations with its objective steady near −1.30, weaker than lift-A (−1.27720) and lift-B. So lifting B is the useful choice for y_A, which treats the parties asymmetrically.
- *Achievable side [NUMERICAL, negative].* h12_struct_search.py restricts every party and setting to structured measurements: a deterministic outcome, or a projective qubit measurement along one of the 13 cube axes (Pauli axes, the face diagonals reached by √X-type quarter turns, cf. H.21.2, and the body diagonals), with its two projectors on two of the four outcomes. That is 160 options per party and setting. 3,000 restarts of discrete coordinate descent reach −0.41656 at best; 30 continuous seesaw polishes started from the best discrete optima reach −0.705802, the known value, and nothing lower.
- **Status.** The optimum of y_A over all instruments lies in **[−1.20330, −0.70580]** [PROVED both ends]. The gap shrinks from 0.5714 to 0.4975. Closing it remains **OPEN**. Untried: lifting two parties at once (memory beyond the box), PPT constraints on the lift, and non-projective structured instruments. *(Round 16, H.23.3: intersecting lift-A with lift-B certifies y_A ≥ −1.16564; interval now [−1.16564, −0.70580].)*
- *Novelty.* None for the method (as in H.21.8). Only the numbers for H.12 are new.

**H.22.2 Twelve of the 22 never-crossed orbits are crossed by pure boards: the time-reversed Lugano process in rotated local frames, and a generalised Lugano board [violation PROVED, exact rational certificate; purity CITED (Guérin–Brukner) plus local unitaries; search NUMERICAL; never22_lugU.py, never22_lugU_1.log, never22_hitopt.py/.log, never22_hitcheck.py, never22_hitcheck2.py, never22_lugT_check.py/.log, never22_cert.py, never22_cert.log, never22_cert_lugT.log, rg_lugU.py/.log, never22_best.npy].**
- *The search family (defined here).* Generalised Lugano boards L_{U_A,U_B,U_C}: |s⟩_P|o⟩_{AO} ↦ (U_A^{lug_A(o)} ⊗ U_B^{lug_B(o)} ⊗ U_C^{lug_C(o)})|s⟩_{AI}|o⟩_F, with any qubit unitaries U_k (L_{X,X,X} is the Lugano purification L of H.15.7e.2, L_{X,√X,X} is the board behind H.12, H.21.2). We take L_U or its time reverse L_U^T, and then put an independent SU(2) frame on every agent's input wire and on every agent's output wire (6 frames, 30 real parameters with the U_k). The objective is the largest relative violation, maximised over the source state, of any of the 5,120 facets in the 22 orbits.
  - *Why this family is new here.* H.17.6 rotated only the agents' input wires of X-type boards. A frame on an output wire destroys the lift form (F no longer records o in the measured basis), so these boards lie outside every family searched before.
  - *Purity of the family [PROVED, with the CITED step of H.21.4].* In VOKB's fleshing-out of the Lugano graph, node k applies a controlled-X from the routing index to an unindexed past qubit. Replacing it by a controlled-U_k changes no route, and the consistent index assignment for each agent output does not involve the gates. So the composite is L_{U_A,U_B,U_C} for every choice of gates (rg_lugU.py: exact for Lugano and √X-Lugano, and to 0 deviation for 200 Haar triples; closed loops with random local unitaries are unitary to 2.6·10⁻¹⁵, for L_U and L_U^T). Time reversal preserves validity (Guérin–Brukner App. D; VOKB Def. B.10), and local frames are absorbed into the agents' operations.
- *Search [NUMERICAL].* 4,000 random boards (seed 1): one violates the 22 orbits, by 0.00232 (orbits 14 and 25; time-reversed). Powell from it reaches a relative violation of **0.046472** on orbits 14 and 25, and simultaneously 0.005191 on each of orbits 4–11 (96 violated facets in each of 4–11, 48 in each of 14 and 25). Optimising orbit 14 alone from the same start (never22_hitopt) gives the same 0.046472.
- **Structure of the optimum [NUMERICAL, to the Powell tolerance 2.5·10⁻⁶].** All three optimised node unitaries have eigenphase gap π, so U_k = e^{ia_k} R_k X R_k†. Then L_U = (R_A⊗R_B⊗R_C)_{AI} D_F L_X (R_A†⊗R_B†⊗R_C†)_P with D an o-dependent phase on F (never22_lugT_check). Hence the optimum is, up to local unitaries on the agent wires, a unitary on the past and a phase on the future, the **time-reversed Lugano process L_X^T** of Guérin and Brukner (ref. 38): no new node gate is needed, only input and output frames.
- **Theorem H.22.2 [PROVED; exact rational arithmetic].** Let V = (F_I ⊗ 1_F) · L_X^T · (F_O ⊗ 1_P), where L_X^T is the time-reversed Lugano purification (a 64×64 permutation matrix) and F_I, F_O are products of three qubit unitaries with Gaussian-rational entries (listed implicitly by never22_cert.py MODE=lugT from never22_best.npy). There is a Gaussian-rational source vector ψ such that the classical process p(i|o) = Σ_f |⟨i f|V|o ψ⟩|²/⟨ψ|ψ⟩ violates one facet of each of the orbits **4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 11, 14 and 25**: relative values −0.0464708 (orbits 14 and 25) and −0.0051703 (orbits 4–11). V is a valid pure process (time-reversed Lugano is valid, Guérin–Brukner 2018 [CITED]; H.15.7e.2 [PROVED/CITED]; local unitaries). Hence **these ten orbits are not valid on Purif.**
  - *Certificate.* Each frame is made exactly unitary by the Cayley transform: U = w(1 + iK)(1 − iK)⁻¹ with w ∈ {±1, ±i} and K Hermitian with entries rounded to denominator 1000 from the float optimum (distance 6.3·10⁻⁴ from it). The script then checks, all in exact Gaussian-rational arithmetic: V†V = 1; p ≥ 0, normalised, and every one of the 64 deterministic local interventions has fixed-point probability exactly 1; the facet functional, rationalised, reproduces the integer facet values on all 744 PFs exactly (as in validcert, H.17.5; Valid and aff(PF) both have dimension 37, so the functional is well defined on Valid); and its exact value at p is negative. The same check with the general L_U board (nodes rationalised too, purity via rg_lugU) also passes for all ten facets (never22_cert.log).
  - *Scope.* "Crossed" is meant as in J.1: some facet of the orbit is violated. This board violates 96 of the 384 facets in each of orbits 4–11 and 48 of the 192 in each of orbits 14 and 25. Whether every facet of these orbits is violated by some pure board depends on whether the symmetry group of the PF polytope used to form the orbits maps Purif into itself, which we have not checked. *Settled in Round 16 (Proposition H.23.1a): it does, so every facet of these orbits is violated on Purif.*
- **Consequences (corrections).**
  - The "22 genuine never-crossed orbits" of J.1, H.17.5 and H.15.4 item 7 were never crossed by the boards searched until Round 15; they are not all valid on Purif. With the addendum below, **the open list is now the 10 orbits 26, 28, 30, 31, 54, 57, 66, 69, 75, 81.** *Update (Round 21, H.28.1): orbits 26, 30 and 31 are crossed too; the open list is now the 7 orbits 28, 54, 57, 66, 69, 75, 81 (15 of 22 crossed). Round 22 (H.29): unchanged. Round 23 (H.30): unchanged. Round 24 (H.31): unchanged on Purif; 54 and 66 proved on every family studied. Round 25 (H.32): unchanged. Round 26 (H.33): unchanged; exact bound −2.62·10⁻⁸ for 57 on the families studied. Round 27 (H.34): unchanged on Purif; 57 proved on every family studied. Round 28 (H.35): unchanged on Purif; 75 proved on every family studied.*
  - H.21.7's reading ("new, continuous evidence that the 22 orbits might be valid on Purif") is superseded for these ten orbits. The X-type path itself is unaffected: it still violates none of them.
  - H.15.4 item 7's guess that the 22 are "classical-like constraints that may bind every reversible quantum loop" is false for ten of them.
- **Addendum: orbits 53 and 79 [violation PROVED, exact rational certificate; purity PROVED with the CITED fleshing-out step; never22_lugU.py with ORBITS restricted to the 12 orbits left (seed 2), never22_best2.npy, never22_cert2.log, never22_purity2.py/.log].** With the objective restricted to the 12 orbits left after Theorem H.22.2, one of 4,000 random boards (seed 2) violates them by 0.00341, and Powell reaches 0.015474 on orbit 53 and 0.006727 on orbit 79 (it also violates orbits 14 and 25). Here the node unitaries are not reflections: U_A and U_B have eigenphase gap 0.31725π and U_C has gap π. So the reduction to Lugano above does not apply, and this is a genuinely generalised (time-reversed, framed) Lugano board. never22_cert.py in general mode (nodes and frames Cayley-rationalised, denominator 1000) certifies exactly that one facet of orbit 53 (index 10160; relative value −0.0154741) and one of orbit 79 (index 10137; relative value −0.0067548) are violated, with the same exact checks as for Theorem H.22.2. Purity rests on the fleshing-out argument (rg_lugU; VOKB Sec. 4.3 [CITED]), time reversal and local frames. Numerically, the closed loop with Haar local unitaries is unitary to 2.2·10⁻¹⁵. **The never-crossed open list is now the 10 orbits 26, 28, 30, 31, 54, 57, 66, 69, 75, 81.**
- *Reading [INTERPRETIVE].* The frames matter: the same process in the computational frame gives a PF mixture (H.15.8.1), in the Hadamard frame it gives H.13.2, which crosses other orbits but not these, and in generic non-Clifford frames on both input and output wires it crosses ten "never-crossed" orbits. The never-crossed list measured the reach of the frames tried, not a property of Purif.
- *Novelty.* The process (time-reversed Lugano), its validity and its causal-inequality violation are due to Guérin and Brukner (2018). The facets are the paper's own PF-hull facets, so the statement that framed Lugano^T crosses orbits 4–11, 14 and 25, with exact certificates, is ours; we found no prior statement about these facets and claim nothing beyond it.

**H.22.3 Classical fine-grained models with bounded reuse: one extra run of one agent is necessary and sufficient [PROVED, with one CITED step; NUMERICAL check; code/stochastic/cyclic/embed/class_reuse.py, class_reuse.log].** H.21.6 settled the two extremes (unrestricted multiple use: yes; reversible single use: no). Here we settle the natural counting version of the middle regime: how many time-local runs of the agents' classical instruments does a classical acyclic model need?
- *Setting (defined here).* A *classical model with run budget r* is an acyclic classical stochastic network (irreversible gates and fresh randomness allowed, classical control of which node acts next allowed) in which the agents' instruments T_k(a_k, o_k | i_k, x_k) are black boxes, called r times in total, each call at one time-local slot on an input computed by the network. Afterwards the network announces each agent's input i_k, and the reported (a_k, o_k) must be the result of one of agent k's calls on input i_k. The model *reproduces* a classical process p if the joint law of (i, o, a) equals Σ_o p(i|o) Π_k T_k(a_k, o_k | i_k, x_k) for every choice of instruments and settings. H.21.6(a) is the case r = 6 (each agent called on both inputs); Lemma H.15.7a covers r = 3 with each agent called once.
- **Theorem H.22.3.** Let p be any valid three-party classical process with binary inputs and outputs.
  - (a) *[PROVED, with one CITED step.]* A model with r = 4 reproduces p: agent C is called twice, on inputs 0 and 1, and A and B once each, in an order the network chooses at random as a function of C's two outputs.
  - (b) *[PROVED, from Lemma H.15.7a.]* No model with r = 3 reproduces p if p violates a causal inequality. This holds for the classical restrictions of H.13.2 and H.12 (H.15.7b, H.15.7d).
  - So for both counterexamples the minimum run budget is exactly 4: one extra run of one agent.
- *Proof of (a).*
  1. C's two calls give (a_C^0, o_C^0) and (a_C^1, o_C^1). Write h_C(j) = o_C^j; this is a deterministic local function of C.
  2. Define the *slice* W_h(i_A, i_B | o_A, o_B) = Σ_{i_C} p(i_A, i_B, i_C | o_A, o_B, h(i_C)). For each h it is a valid bipartite classical process: composing it with any deterministic interventions of A and B is composing p with deterministic interventions of all three, which gives total probability 1.
  3. Every valid bipartite classical process is causally separable: W_h = W_h^{A≺B} + W_h^{B≺A}, with both terms non-negative, the first not signalling from B to A and the second not from A to B (Oreshkov, Costa and Brukner, arXiv:1105.4464, Nat. Commun. 3, 1092 (2012), appendix "Causal order in the classical limit", proved there for process matrices diagonal in the pointer basis) [CITED].
  4. The network picks the branch "A first" with probability Σ W_h^{A≺B} (this is independent of o_A, o_B by the no-signalling constraints). In that branch it samples i_A from the normalised marginal, calls A, samples i_B given (i_A, o_A), and calls B. The "B first" branch is symmetric.
  5. Finally it samples i_C from p(i_A, i_B, i_C | o_A, o_B, h(i_C)) / W_h(i_A, i_B | o_A, o_B), and reports C's call on input i_C. The discarded call is independent of everything else, so summing it out gives Σ_o p(i|o) Π_k T_k exactly. The network is acyclic: C's calls, then the dynamically ordered A and B, then the announcement. ∎
- *Check [NUMERICAL].* class_reuse.py finds the decomposition of step 3 by linear programming for every slice and builds the network. The network's exact joint law is compared with the process for 20 random instrument triples on each of nine processes: the Lugano PF, and the classical restrictions of H.13.2 and of H.12 with four source states each (|000⟩ and three random). The maximum deviation is 2.8·10⁻¹⁷.
- *The parallel with the quantum circuits [INTERPRETIVE].* The quantum TDS circuits for H.13.2 (H.18.4) and H.12 (H.21.3) have the same resource count: C has two time-local instances and A, B act once each in a (coherently) controlled order. In the classical model C's second call is queried and then partly discarded; in the quantum circuit both instances of C are used coherently (U_C, then U_C σ_x U_C†) and nothing is post-selected. So r = 4 is not a quantum advantage in the run budget. What the quantum circuit buys is that it needs neither discarding nor knowledge of C's full function.
- *Scope.* The construction extends to N parties by calling all but two agents on every input value; we have not studied whether that budget is minimal for N > 3. "Irreversible single use on delocalised classical variables" without any discarding stays **OPEN**, as in H.21.6(c), because we still have no precise definition of a delocalised classical variable outside the reversible case.
- *Novelty.* The ingredients are standard: querying a classical box on all inputs, and the causal separability of bipartite classical processes (OCB 2012). We found no statement of the run-budget count for classical processes that violate causal inequalities, but we claim no more than the combination.

**H.22.4 Correction to H.20.6: Mathlib does have the spectral theorem for compact self-adjoint operators [CITED, checked in the pinned Mathlib; the boundedness gap itself stays OPEN].**
- H.20.6 said that the Jordan decomposition needed for automatic boundedness "needs the spectral theorem for compact self-adjoint operators on an infinite-dimensional Hilbert space, which Mathlib does not have". That is wrong. The Mathlib pinned by this repository (lean-toolchain v4.34.1; Mathlib commit d13f23b) contains, in Mathlib/Analysis/InnerProductSpace/Spectrum.lean, `ContinuousLinearMap.orthogonalComplement_iSup_eigenspaces_eq_bot` (for IsCompactOperator T and T.IsSymmetric, the eigenspaces have trivial orthogonal complement) and `ContinuousLinearMap.finite_dimensional_eigenspace` (nonzero eigenspaces are finite-dimensional). Both came in with Mathlib PR #36520 (commit 36f4d76, checked in the package's git history).
- The repository already uses the first one: PFUL/Schmidt.lean (Oct 22 2026) builds a Hilbert basis of eigenvectors of |T| from it and proves the Schmidt decomposition with Σ sₙ = ‖T‖₁. H.20.6 (Round 13) was therefore inconsistent with the repository's own Lean files.
- *What the gap actually is.* For self-adjoint trace-class T, apply the same theorem to T itself (compact, by `isCompactOperator_of_mem`) to get an eigenbasis with real eigenvalues λₙ, set T₊ = Σ_{λₙ>0} λₙ|vₙ⟩⟨vₙ| and T₋ = −Σ_{λₙ<0} λₙ|vₙ⟩⟨vₙ|, and show both are trace class with Tr T₊ + Tr T₋ = Σ|λₙ| = ‖T‖₁. Then ‖Φ(T)‖₁ ≤ Tr Φ(T₊) + Tr Φ(T₋) = ‖T‖₁ for positive trace-preserving Φ, and ‖Φ‖ ≤ 2 on general T. This is routine given Schmidt.lean, but it has **not** been formalised this round; the status of the gap is **OPEN, with no known Mathlib obstruction**.
- *Update (Round 16, H.23.2): closed in Lean.* The formalisation does not use the eigenbasis: Mathlib's continuous functional calculus gives T₊, T₋ directly.

### H.23 Round 16: the never-crossed orbits, automatic boundedness in Lean, and the H.12 gap (Oct 24 2026) [labels as in H.21]

**H.23.1 The never-crossed orbits: orbit-level crossing for the twelve crossed orbits, the generalised Lugano family is exhaustive for XOR boards, and further searches find nothing [symmetry and enumeration PROVED by exhaustive check; searches NUMERICAL; the 10 orbits stay OPEN; embed/h16_orbit_sym.py/.log, embed/h16_enum_xor.py/.log, embed/h16_permboards.py/.log, embed/h16_gen_search.py, h16_gen_run.py, h16_lugboard.py, h16_gen_lug.log, never22_lugU_3_open10.log].**
- **Proposition H.23.1a (orbits are Purif-symmetric) [PROVED, exhaustive check].** The 384-element group G (physics/facets/G.npy) used to form the facet orbits (h13/analyze.py) is exactly the group generated by party permutations, input-bit flips and output-bit flips acting on the 744 PF tables p(i|o) (h16_orbit_sym.py: the generated group and G.npy coincide as sets of permutations of the 744 PFs). For each of the 22 orbits of J.1, the G-orbit of one facet (through its set of tight PFs, which determines the facet) is the whole orbit. Every generator is realised on pure processes by a local unitary (Pauli X on one agent's input or output wire, absorbed into the agent) or by relabelling the parties, so it maps valid pure processes to valid pure processes, and Purif onto itself; the facet functional transforms with it and the relative violation is unchanged.
  - *Consequence.* H.22.2's open scope question is settled: **every facet** of orbits 4–11, 14, 25, 53 and 79 (3,584 facets) is violated by some board in Purif, namely the certified board of Theorem H.22.2 or of the addendum transformed by a local X frame and a party relabelling.
  - *Consequence for the open orbits.* For each of the 10 open orbits it suffices to test one representative facet; the 1,536 facets fall into 10 such classes (orbit sizes 192, 192, 192, 192, 96, 192, 96, 192, 96, 96). *Update (H.28.1):* orbits 26, 30 and 31 are now crossed; 7 orbits (960 facets) remain open.
- **Proposition H.23.1b (XOR boards) [PROVED, exhaustive enumeration; h16_enum_xor.py].** Consider classical deterministic boards i = s ⊕ f(o) on three binary parties with f_k depending only on o_{−k} (the form that makes the Lugano-type lift valid). Exactly 744 of the 4,096 choices are valid for every s, and they are the 744 PFs. The 64 with no constant f_k have every f_k a single minterm (truth table 1, 2, 4 or 8 on o_{−k}) or its complement (7, 11, 13 or 14), and each is the Lugano function up to party relabelling, input negation and complementation of outputs; the other 680 have a constant f_k, so that party acts first and the rest is a bipartite PF; these are causal, 192 of them with an order that depends on the first party's output (h16_enum_xor.log: 256 of the 744 admit no fixed order, 64 of them non-causal). Input negation and output complementation are Pauli-X frames. Hence the framed generalised Lugano family L_U, L_U^T of H.22.2 already contains the lift of every XOR board in every local frame, and the controlled-root-of-Pauli boards are the case U_k = X^{1/m} (up to frames), so neither family can add new boards.
- **Search beyond XOR [NUMERICAL].**
  - *Generalised Lugano with frames, restricted to the 10 open orbits.* Seed 3 (8,000 random boards plus Powell from the five best; never22_lugU.py with ORBITS set to the 10, never22_lugU_3_open10.log) finds no violation: the largest violation found is 0 (boards on which a facet is tight), never positive.
  - *Reversible non-XOR permutation boards.* V|o, s⟩ = |f_s(o)⟩_AI|o⟩_F with every f_s a PF and s ↦ f_s(o) a bijection for each o (h16_permboards.py). Of 200 random such boards (backtracking sampler), none is XOR and none is valid as a quantum process: the Araújo et al. projector residual ‖Π_V W − ₍all₎W‖² (M. Araújo, C. Branciard, F. Costa, A. Feix, C. Giarmatzi and Č. Brukner, Witnessing causal nonseparability, New J. Phys. 17, 102001 (2015), arXiv:1506.03776) is between 14 and 70.1 (h16_permboards.log). So classical reversible validity for every s does not give quantum validity here, and this family gives no candidates.
  - *Unrestricted penalty search.* Over all 64×64 unitaries V (P ⊗ AO → AI ⊗ F), maximising a soft maximum of the open-orbit violations minus μ times the projector residual, μ ramped from 10 to 10⁵, started from three random framed Lugano boards (Adam, 3,000 steps each). The residual did not converge (final residuals 0.131, 0.166 and 0.239; largest open-orbit value −0.0051, i.e. no violation), so the runs end at invalid processes and say nothing either way. A generic hit would in any case be hard to certify exactly.
- **Status [OPEN].** The 10 orbits 26, 28, 30, 31, 54, 57, 66, 69, 75, 81 are neither crossed nor proved valid on Purif. We did not find a proof route: the natural candidate, an SOS certificate that the facet functional is non-negative on the classical restriction of every W with Π_V W = W (Araújo et al. 2015) and W² ∝ W, is a degree-two problem in the 2¹²-dimensional W with a rank constraint, out of reach of the tools used here.
- *Novelty.* The symmetry group of the PF polytope, the projector characterisation of validity (Araújo et al. 2015) and the Lugano process (Baumeler and Wolf [15]; its purification, Araújo et al. [17]) are known. The two propositions are bookkeeping facts about this paper's facets and boards.

**H.23.2 The Round 8/9 boundedness gap is closed in Lean: positive trace-preserving maps on the trace class are trace-norm bounded, so `IsNormalCPTP` ⇔ `IsCPTP` [PROVED (Lean); lean/PFUL/AutoBounded.lean, lean/PFUL/AutoBoundedKraus.lean, lean/Round16_axioms.lean/.log; the sharp constant CITED, not formalised].**
- *Statement.* Let H, K be complex Hilbert spaces of any dimension and Φ a map from bounded operators on H to bounded operators on K that sends the trace class to the trace class, is linear there, preserves the trace and sends positive trace-class operators to positive operators (`PFUL.TraceClass.IsPTP`; complete positivity is not needed). Then for every trace-class T
  - ‖Φ(T)‖₁ ≤ ‖T‖₁ if T is self-adjoint (`IsPTP.traceNorm_le_selfAdjoint`), and
  - ‖Φ(T)‖₁ ≤ 2‖T‖₁ in general (`IsPTP.traceNorm_le`).
  Hence every `IsCPTP` map is `IsNormalCPTP` (`IsCPTP.isNormalCPTP`), and the two predicates are equivalent (`isNormalCPTP_iff`).
- *Proof (as formalised).*
  1. *Jordan decomposition* (`jordan`). For self-adjoint trace-class S, take S⁺ and S⁻ from Mathlib's continuous functional calculus (`CFC.posPart_sub_negPart`, `CFC.posPart_add_negPart`, `CFC.posPart_nonneg`, `CFC.negPart_nonneg`). Then S⁺ − S⁻ = S and S⁺ + S⁻ = |S|, so S^± = ½(|S| ± S) are trace class, because |S| is (`absOp_mem`, Round 7) and the trace class is a subspace.
  2. For positive trace-class P, ‖P‖₁ = Tr P is real and non-negative (`traceNorm_of_isPositive`, `trace_of_isPositive`, from ‖T‖₁ = Tr|T| and |P| = P).
  3. For self-adjoint S: ‖ΦS‖₁ ≤ ‖ΦS⁺‖₁ + ‖ΦS⁻‖₁ = Tr ΦS⁺ + Tr ΦS⁻ = Tr S⁺ + Tr S⁻ = Tr|S| = ‖S‖₁.
  4. For general T, write T = R + iJ with R = ½(T + T†) and J = −(i/2)(T − T†) self-adjoint, and ‖R‖₁, ‖J‖₁ ≤ ‖T‖₁ (`traceNorm_adjoint_le`, from Tr A† = conj Tr A, `trace_adjoint`, and the variational formula for ‖·‖₁). Then ‖ΦT‖₁ ≤ ‖ΦR‖₁ + ‖ΦJ‖₁ ≤ 2‖T‖₁.
  All nine new theorems depend only on propext, Classical.choice and Quot.sound (Round16_axioms.log); there is no sorry, admit or native_decide.
- *Corrections to H.20.6 and H.22.4.* Neither the spectral theorem for compact operators nor the Schmidt decomposition was needed: the continuous functional calculus that Mathlib already provides for bounded operators on a Hilbert space (a C*-algebra) gives the Jordan decomposition directly, and the trace-class bookkeeping was already in the repository. H.22.4's plan via an eigenbasis of T would also have worked, but it is longer.
- *Consequences [PROVED (Lean)].*
  - `isCPTP_iff_kraus_any`: on ℓ² of any cardinality, a map is CPTP iff it is a Kraus channel, with no boundedness assumption (Round 13's `isNormalCPTP_iff_kraus_any` with the normality hypothesis removed).
  - `PFUL.Counterexample.valid_CPTP_Vsb_any`: the Switchboard (H.12) is valid against arbitrary CPTP local channels on ancillas of any cardinality (Round 13's `valid_normalCPTP_Vsb_any` without normality).
  - Section 9 caveat (4) and research item 4's "automatic boundedness" are therefore closed. Caveats (1)–(3) are unchanged.
- *Scope.* The constant 2 is not sharp. For positive trace-preserving Φ, in fact ‖Φ‖_{1→1} = 1: the dual map Φ* on B(K) is positive and unital, so ‖Φ*‖ = ‖Φ*(1)‖ = 1 by the Russo–Dye theorem (B. Russo and H. A. Dye, Duke Math. J. 33, 413 (1966); see V. Paulsen, *Completely Bounded Maps and Operator Algebras*, Cambridge 2002, Cor. 2.9), and ‖Φ‖ = ‖Φ*‖ by trace duality [CITED; not formalised]. The constant 1 is not needed for anything in this paper: boundedness alone is what `IsNormalCPTP` asks for. We have also not formalised that Φ* is normal (weak*-continuous); that also follows from boundedness on paper.
- *Novelty.* Automatic boundedness of positive maps between the trace classes is classical (it follows, for example, from the Russo–Dye argument above, or from the Jordan decomposition exactly as here). What is new is only the formalisation against the repository's own trace class, and the resulting removal of the normality hypothesis from the Round 12–13 theorems.

**H.23.3 The H.12 gap: intersecting lift-A and lift-B certifies y_A ≥ −1.16564 [lower bound PROVED, exact integer certificate; solver values NUMERICAL; embed/h16_liftAB_scs.py, h16_liftAB_verify.py, h16_liftAB_linkcheck.py and their .log files].** Target 3 of Round 16.
- *Relaxation.* We impose two lifted families at once: Z^A on (A, A′, B, C), the lifted-A relaxation of H.21.8, and Z^B on (B, B′, A, C), the lift-B relaxation of H.22.1. Each family keeps all of its own constraints. They are linked by requiring the same induced no-signalling family: F^A_{x,c} = F^B_{x,c} for all 8 settings x and 64 outcome triples c, where F^L_{x,c} = ½ Tr_{unused copy} Σ_{unused outcome} Z^L, read in the (A, B, C) qubit order. A product of instruments gives such a pair: both families induce M^A_{x_A,c_A} ⊗ M^B_{x_B,c_B} ⊗ M^C_{x_C,c_C}. h16_liftAB_linkcheck.py checks this identity with random POVMs and random multipliers (agreement 3.6e-15) [NUMERICAL]. Since lift-A and lift-B each contain every instrument strategy (H.21.8, H.22.1), so does their intersection, and its value is at least the larger of the two.
- *Dual and certificate.* The dual adds Hermitian 8×8 multipliers Λ_{x,c} for the linking equalities. The cost of family A becomes C_v + K^A_v(Λ), the cost of family B becomes −K^B_v(Λ), with K^L_v(Λ) = Σ_{x_L} emb(Λ_{x,c}, copy x_L)/2, and the rest is the dual of H.21.8 for each family. For any multipliers: y_A·P = Σ_L (Σ Tr Λ^L + Σ_v Tr[S^L_v Z^L_v]), because the linking terms cancel on the intersection.
  - The model has 2,048 PSD blocks of size 16×16 and 559,104 real variables. It was assembled directly as a sparse SCS problem, because cvxpy did not finish compiling either the primal (h16_lift_AB.py, more than 35 min) or the dual (h16_liftAB_dual.py, more than 25 min at 6.7 GB). Both cvxpy runs were stopped.
  - SCS (eps 10⁻⁵) gives dual value −1.16321 in 1,122 s.
  - Validation: run on family A alone, the same code reproduces the Round 14 lift-A value (−1.27717) and certifies −1.27820 with h16_liftAB_verify.py.
  - h16_liftAB_verify.py follows h12_lift_verify.py. It rounds every multiplier parameter to 2⁻³⁰, applies the twirl and linking maps exactly in integers, and certifies a lower eigenvalue bound for every block by Sylvester's criterion (Bareiss).
  - **Result [PROVED, exact integer certificate]:** min over all instruments of y_A·P ≥ −76391593505924911/65536000000000000 ≈ **−1.16564**.
- **Status.** The optimum of y_A over all instruments lies in **[−1.16564, −0.70580]** [PROVED both ends]. The gap shrinks from 0.4975 to 0.4598. The achievable side is unchanged (seesaw −0.70580, NUMERICAL as a value, PROVED as a strategy). Closing the gap remains **OPEN**. Still untried: adding the lift-C family (memory), PPT constraints on the lifts, and an eps 10⁻⁶ solve (the certified value loses about 0.0024 to the eps 10⁻⁵ eigenvalue slack).
- *Literature.* The construction is a hierarchy-style relaxation: copies of a party's system so that its incompatible measurements coexist, plus consistency constraints between different liftings. It is in the spirit of NPA and moment-matrix hierarchies (Navascués, Pironio, Acín 2007/2008) and of the SDP relaxations of instrument-based causal inequalities. We do not claim that the hierarchy converges [CITED for the method; INTERPRETIVE for the comparison].

**H.23.4 Conjecture F.5.7: the countably controlled case [PROVED, paper; NUMERICAL sanity check].** Target 4 of Round 16. Proposition F.5.10 (Appendix F) extends F.5.9 from finitely to countably many cells. The closed loop M = Σ_c ⊗_k P_{k,c_k} V_k S_{ω_k(c)} converges strongly and is unitary for all local unitaries with ancillas, and grid discretisations adapted to the cells and translations converge strongly to it. The column identity M*M = 1 now has a short proof that needs no decomposition into deterministic functions: expectations in product states are classical stochastic local maps, the process-function property makes the expected fixed-point count 1, and polarisation in each tensor factor finishes the argument. Example [PROVED]: w(o) = ω(⌊o⌋) on ℝ³, with ω Lugano on parities and unbounded odd translation amounts. It is non-causal, countably controlled and not finitely controlled (`embed/f510_check.py`, 3,000 random local maps, always exactly one fixed point [NUMERICAL]). **Still OPEN:** non-causal w that is not countably controlled, e.g. continuous dependence on the outputs.

### H.24 Round 17: uniform limits for F.5.7, rigidity of classically controlled boards, and the H.12 gap (Oct 24 2026) [labels as in H.21]

**H.24.1 Conjecture F.5.7 beyond countable control: uniform limits give a canonical isometric closed loop [PROVED, paper; unitarity OPEN].** Target 3 of Round 17. Proposition F.5.11 (Appendix F) takes Euclidean output spaces and a measurable process function w that is a uniform limit of countably controlled process functions w_n. The F.5.10 unitaries M_n then converge strongly to an isometry M that does not depend on the approximating sequence. The key estimate is ‖(M_n − M_m)ψ‖ ≤ (e^{NεK} − 1)‖ψ‖ for ψ band-limited to momenta in [−K, K], with ε = ‖w_n − w_m‖_∞ and N the total output dimension. It comes from a Taylor expansion of the translations: on the common refinement of the cells, the multiplication operators Δ(o)^α commute with the cell projections. M is unitary exactly when the adjoints M_n*φ stay tight in momentum. This is the remaining obstruction, and we have neither a proof nor a counterexample. Example [PROVED]: the Lugano pattern on the parities of the integer parts, with activated amounts given by arbitrary uniformly continuous functions of the other outputs. This is a non-causal process function, and it is not countably controlled when the amounts take uncountably many values. It is the uniform limit of its dyadic roundings, so F.5.11 applies. **Status:** F.5.7 is now reduced to the tightness question for this class. Process functions that are not uniform limits of countably controlled ones, for example with a jump set that no countable partition captures up to null sets, remain OPEN, as before. Literature: Giacomini, Castro-Ruiz and Brukner (2016) for continuous-variable process matrices. The analytic-vector estimate is standard.

**H.24.2 The never-crossed orbits: classically controlled qubit boards are rigid [NUMERICAL; embed/h17_ctrl_boards.py/.log; the 10 orbits stay OPEN].** Target 2 of Round 17. H.23.1 showed that the generalised Lugano family is exhaustive among XOR boards. Here we test the next natural non-XOR family, *classically controlled boards*: i_k = U_{k, o_{−k}} s_k, where each party's qubit unitary depends on the other two outputs. Validity is the Araújo et al. projector penalty of H.23.1, and values around 1e-29 mean valid.
- Generic control, with four independent Haar unitaries per party: invalid in 3/3 draws (penalty 74 to 99).
- Control by the next party only: invalid in 3/3 draws (42 to 58).
- Two-valued Lugano routing, with one Haar unitary on the activated Lugano cell and another elsewhere: valid in 3/3 draws (≈ 2·10⁻²⁹).
- Lugano routing with the inactive cells refined into distinct Haar unitaries, or even into distinct diagonal phases: invalid in 2/2 draws each (43 to 86).

So, within this family, validity seems to force the control to factor through the Lugano activation bit. The valid members are Lugano boards dressed with local unitaries, and H.23.1 already searched those (`never22_lugU_3_open10.log`) without crossing any of the 10 orbits 26, 28, 30, 31, 54, 57, 66, 69, 75, 81. This is evidence, not a proof. The sample is small, and the boards and the purification argument of H.23.1a were not combined into a proof that the orbits hold on Purif. **Status:** all 10 orbits remain OPEN. Neither a crossing nor a proof was found this round. Literature: the validity criterion is from Araújo, Feix, Navascués and Brukner (Quantum 1, 10, 2017). Classical control of local operations by a process function follows Baumeler and Wolf (2016).

**H.24.3 The H.12 upper end: a massively multistart search does not beat −0.70580 [NUMERICAL; embed/h17_batch_search.py, h17_batch_search_s11.log, h17_batch_search_s12r1.log].** Target 1 of Round 17, achievable side. By H.15.7d every instrument strategy reduces to 4-outcome qubit POVMs E_{a,o|x}, so higher-dimensional instruments cannot help, and the search runs over exactly that set. `h17_batch_search.py` optimises batches of 1024 random POVM products with Adam: 1500 steps, learning rate 0.05 then 0.01, POVMs parametrised as normalised Gram products. As a check, it reproduces −0.7058021862 on the Round 14 seesaw optimum (h12_bound_warm_E.npy). Two runs were made:
- general rank, seed 11;
- rank-1 POVM elements, seed 12.

Each was stopped after 3 of 8 planned rounds, i.e. 3 × 1024 starts each, to free the CPU for the lift solve. Both reach exactly −0.705802 in every round, and nothing lower. The general-rank run also finds a second local optimum at −0.65969. The rank-1 run reaches −0.705802 from 0.7–1.7% of starts. The value is therefore very likely the true optimum of the instrument problem, but this is not proved: the interval for min y_A·P stays [−1.16564, −0.70580] unless H.24.4 improves the lower end. Literature: seesaw and multistart local optimisation for process-matrix and causal-inequality problems are standard (e.g. Branciard et al., New J. Phys. 18, 013008, 2016).

**H.24.4 The H.12 lower end: the three-family intersection lift-A ∩ lift-B ∩ lift-C [set up; solve INCOMPLETE; no new certificate; embed/h17_lift_scs.py, h17_lift_verify.py, h17_lift_scs_ABC_partial.log]. *Update (Round 19, H.26.2): the solve finished and certifies −1.15861 exactly.* Target 1 of Round 17, relaxation side. `h17_lift_scs.py` generalises the direct sparse SCS assembly of H.23.3 to any set of lifted families. Each family other than A is tied to A by its own linking multiplier Λ^L_{x,c}: family A carries +K^L for every link, and family L carries −K^L for its own. The same weak-duality identity as in H.23.3 then holds, with the link terms cancelling pairwise on the intersection. The direct (LDL) solver would need about 10 GB, which would endanger the running glue_k4 job, so the run uses SCS's indirect (CG) solver at eps 10⁻⁵. In about 50 minutes it reached only iteration 250. There the dual objective was ≈ 1.16, the same as the converged A ∩ B value 1.16564 to this precision, with primal and dual residuals 1.6·10⁻⁴ and 7.1·10⁻⁴ [NUMERICAL, unconverged]. The exact verifier `h17_lift_verify.py` adapts h16_liftAB_verify.py to the multi-link keys, but has not yet been run, since there is no converged solution to check. The job is left running under nohup and writes /tmp/r17/lift_ABC.pkl when it finishes. *Update (Round 19, H.26.2):* it finished, and the exact verifier certifies y_A ≥ −1.15861. **Status:** the certified interval stays [−1.16564, −0.70580] (H.23.3, H.24.3). Whether lift-C closes any of the gap is OPEN. The early iterates give no sign that it does.

**H.24.5 Lean [not attempted this round].** Target 4 of Round 17. The finite combinatorial core of the F.5.10 column argument is the expected fixed-point count Σ_s ∏_k q_k(w(s)_k, s_k) = 1 for stochastic local maps q_k. It is already machine-checked as `PFUL.Column.sum_prod_eq_one` for finite alphabets, with the cell alphabet of F.5.9 as the special case. Not formalised: the countable-cell version, the polarisation step, F.5.11, and the sharp Russo–Dye constant (still CITED, H.23.2). All remain open for Lean.

### H.25 Round 18: the H.12 optimum is a certified strict local minimum, unitary closed loops for activation-controlled w, the ABC lift (Oct 24 2026) [labels as in H.21]

**H.25.1 Conjecture F.5.7: activation-controlled process functions [PROVED, paper].** Target 3 of Round 18. Proposition F.5.12 (Appendix F) covers process functions with three properties:
- in each product region of the output space, at most one party is active;
- the active party's own output is unconstrained;
- its translation amount is an arbitrary measurable function of the other outputs, while the inactive amounts are constant.

For such w, the closed loop M = Σ_R Π_R D_R (⊗_{j≠κ} V_j S_{ω_j}) is unitary for all local unitaries, where D_R is the decomposable operator with fibres V_κ S_{h_R(o_{−κ})}. M is also the strong limit of countably controlled closed loops along any almost-everywhere approximation of the amounts.
- *Rows:* the Lugano-type exclusivity (E) makes the terms orthogonal.
- *Columns:* the projection onto a region commutes with D_R, so the amounts drop out of M*M entirely. M*M then equals the column sum of the skeleton (amounts set to 0), which is 1 by F.5.10.

The continuous-amount Lugano example of F.5.11 qualifies for **any** measurable amounts. There the column identity is the polynomial identity ẽ_B õ_C + ẽ_C õ_A + ẽ_A õ_B + ẽ_A ẽ_B ẽ_C + õ_A õ_B õ_C = 1 in commuting projections. So the momentum-tightness condition of F.5.11 (iii) holds in that example, and the canonical isometry is unitary. This also covers amounts that are discontinuous everywhere, which are not uniform limits.

**Still OPEN:** regions in which several parties' amounts vary, or in which the active party's own output is constrained. In particular, unitarity for general uniform limits, such as Lipschitz w, is still open. Literature: direct integrals and decomposable operators (Dixmier 1957; Reed–Simon IV). For coherent classical control of quantum operations by process functions, see Baumeler, Costa, Ralph, Wolf and Zych (2019) and Araújo et al. (2017).

**H.25.2 The H.12 upper end: the −0.70580 strategy is an exact strict local minimum within its family, and block-optimal over all POVMs [critical point and value PROVED by interval Krawczyk test; strict local minimality PROVED (exact rational Sylvester test plus interval radius bound); block optimality NUMERICAL; global optimality OPEN; embed/h18_h12_local.py/.log].** Target 2 of Round 18.
