*Structure [NUMERICAL, then exact].* After polishing, both Round 17 searches give the same optimum up to relabelling, and it has a simple structure:
- Every party outputs its own setting, o_k = x_k.
- The bit a_k comes from a projective qubit measurement along a unit Bloch vector n_{k,x}.

On this family, y_A·P is a cubic polynomial f(n) = Σ_x Σ_μ W_x[μ] ∏_k (1, n_{k,x_k})[μ_k] on (S²)⁶. Its coefficients are W_x[μ] = d_{x,supp μ}·⟨ψ_{o(x)}|σ_μ|ψ_{o(x)}⟩, where d is the y-weighted sign sum over a.

*Exact critical point [PROVED].*
- Newton on the 24-variable Lagrange system g_i + λ_i n_i = 0, |n_i|² = 1, at 60 digits, converges quadratically (residual 5·10⁻⁶¹).
- A Krawczyk test in interval arithmetic (mpmath.iv) shows that a box of radius 10⁻³⁰ contains a unique exact critical point. The coefficients are widened by 10⁻⁵⁵ to cover their 60-digit rounding.
- Its value lies in [−0.7058022388724872945152387879537, −0.7058022388724872945152387878977].

*Second order [PROVED].*
- The Lagrangian Hessian projected on the tangent spaces has eigenvalues 0.5347 to 2.664.
- Hp − 0.2673·1 is positive definite by an exact rational Sylvester test.
- The interval Hessian over the box has radius ≤ 10⁻³⁰, far below the margin.
- So this is a strict local minimum of y_A·P among strategies with deterministic outputs and projective qubit measurements.

*Block optimality [NUMERICAL].* For each party and setting, the SDP over all 4-outcome POVMs with the other parties fixed improves the value by at most 4·10⁻¹². So no single party, in any one setting, can do better with any instrument.

*Closed form [NUMERICAL, negative].* A 50-digit integer-relation search (PSLQ via mpmath.findpoly) finds no integer polynomial of degree ≤ 10 with coefficients ≤ 10⁴ having this root, and no ℚ(√2)-linear form. The value is algebraic, as a critical value of a polynomial system with coefficients in ℚ(√2), but apparently of high degree, unlike the ℚ(√2) value of H.15.7d. The Bloch vectors look generic.

*Status.* −0.70580 is certified as an exact local optimum within the natural family, and as a Nash point over all instruments. Global optimality, i.e. closing [−1.16564, −0.70580], remains **OPEN**. A relaxation tight at −0.70580 is not in sight: the best lifted relaxation is at −1.16564 (H.24.4). *Update (Round 19): the three-family lift gives −1.15861 (H.26.2), and within the family a level-2 moment relaxation is tight (H.26.3).* Literature: the Krawczyk operator (Krawczyk 1969; Moore, Kearfott and Cloud, *Introduction to Interval Analysis*, 2009) and mpmath's interval arithmetic.

**H.25.4 The never-crossed orbits: where a proof must come from, and entangling dressings of Lugano are invalid [validity-only obstruction PROVED (exact rational certificate; reproduces H.17.5 for the 10 open orbits); dressing family NUMERICAL; the 10 orbits stay OPEN; embed/h18_orbits_valid.py/.log, embed/h18_entangled_boards.py/.log].** Target 4 of Round 18.
- *Proof route tested: validity alone [PROVED, negative].* Suppose a proof that a representative facet holds on Purif used only that the process matrix is valid, for example an SDP certificate over mixed valid W with a positivity or validity dual. Such a proof would also cover diagonal W, whose statistics are exactly the classical valid processes, so it would prove the facet on the Baumeler–Wolf polytope Valid. h18_orbits_valid.py rules this out for each of the 10 orbits. It builds Valid from normalisation plus the 64 deterministic-intervention constraints Σ_i p(i|f(i)) = 1, minimises the representative facet (H.23.1a) by LP, and checks the minimiser exactly: it is a rational valid process, and its exact affine coordinates over 38 affinely independent PFs give the facet value from the integer facet values on PFs. For all 10 orbits the minimum relative value is exactly **−1/6**. This matches the H.17.5 validcert values; here it is recomputed independently, for the representatives. So any proof has to use purity, i.e. the rank-one (unitary-dilation) condition, in an essentially non-convex way. Two further facts point the same way. Valid ≠ conv(PF): Valid has 64 facets and 710,760 vertices. And the Lemma B factorisation (H.17.17) is a statement about Pauli boards, not about general unitaries. The natural "unitarity of the lift + Lemma B" route therefore reaches only boards already in the H.17 classification. We did not find a way to make it cover general Purif.
- *New non-XOR family: entangling dressings [NUMERICAL, negative].* Boards |s⟩_P|o⟩ ↦ G·(⊗_k U_k^{lug_k(o)})|s⟩_I |o⟩_F, where G is a fixed entangling gate on the three input wires. Local gates G are absorbed into the frames already searched in H.23.1. The Araújo et al. validity penalty (h16_gen_search.penalty; ≈10⁻²⁹ means valid) is as follows, over 3 draws each:
  - G = 1 (control): 5–8·10⁻³⁰, valid;
  - G Haar on all three inputs: 26–46;
  - G Haar on inputs A and B: 12–33;
  - G random diagonal phases: 13–31;
  - G = CZ on AB: 11–20;
  - Lugano itself (U = X) with CZ_AB or Haar(4)_AB: 32–40.

  Every entangling dressing tested is invalid. Even a diagonal (computational-basis) phase gate, which does not change the classical routing, destroys validity, because the parties can measure in rotated frames. Together with H.24.2 this suggests that within qubit boards the valid ones are, up to local frames, exactly the generalised Lugano boards already searched by H.23.1 (never22_lugU_3_open10.log, no crossing). This is evidence, not a theorem.
- *Status.* All 10 orbits (26, 28, 30, 31, 54, 57, 66, 69, 75, 81) remain **OPEN**. A proof must use purity non-convexly. The remaining crossing candidates lie beyond single-qubit-per-wire Lugano dressings, e.g. qudit wires or process functions with more than one active party per region. Literature: Baumeler and Wolf (New J. Phys. 18, 035014, 2016) for the classical polytope and its deterministic-intervention description; Araújo, Feix, Navascués and Brukner (Quantum 1, 10, 2017) for validity and the purification postulate.

### H.26 Round 19: the four-term threshold is proved, the ABC lift raises the H.12 lower end, the H.12 strategy is optimal to within 2.7·10⁻⁴ in its family (Oct 24 2026) [labels as in H.21]

**H.26.1 "Four terms per outcome is the minimum" [PROVED, exhaustive exact search, per-class-pair model; h17/glue_k4.py, glue_k4.log, glue_k4_done.jsonl].** Target 2 of Round 19. The four-class search of H.17.17, launched in Round 8 and run in the background since, has finished. All 2,722 canonical geometries are done (2,896,784 jobs), with 0 candidates and 0 UNRESOLVED jobs. Together with the class bound (at most four classes; H.17.17, also in Lean) and the ≤3-class closures (H.17.9, H.17.13–H.17.15), every admissible board with at most three Pauli terms per outcome is a PF blend. So four terms per outcome is the minimum for a board outside conv(PF). By H.13.2, four terms do suffice.
- *Audit of the completeness claim [PROVED by exhaustive check; h17/glue_k4_audit.py, glue_k4_audit.log].* The counts in the logs and the checkpoint need reading carefully:
  - The index g in glue_k4_done.jsonl is the **raw position** of a labelled geometry in glue_k4_tuples.json (21,906 entries, all distinct; rerunning glue_k4enum.py reproduces the file byte for byte). It is not an orbit id.
  - Independently recomputing the orbit key of all 21,906 geometries under cube translations × G₄₈ gives exactly **2,722 orbits**. glue_k4_canon.json contains exactly one geometry from each orbit, and nothing else.
  - glue_k4_done.jsonl has 2,889 records with distinct g in 0..21,892: all **2,722 canonical geometries**, plus 167 non-canonical ones from the first run of the log ("21706 to do"), which started in raw order before the canonical filter. The restarts ("2541 to do", "2437 to do") are the canonical geometries still outstanding at each restart, and the final SUMMARY line counts only the last restart.
  - Every canonical geometry has a record, and no record (canonical or not) has a candidate or an UNRESOLVED job. Jobs over the canonical set total 2,896,784.
  - So the coverage is complete. Running one geometry per orbit suffices, because the symmetries are PROVED (H.17.15(d)).
- *Scope.* This holds in the per-class-pair model of H.17.13–H.17.16: supports of V_yV_x† are over-approximated by the H.17.12 pattern lists and filtered by Lemmas A–C, and the leaf test is the one in glue_k4.py. An over-approximation can only add jobs, so a clean run is sound for the model. Two things are not proved: that the model captures every admissible board without the pattern classification's hypotheses, and a formal (Lean) check of the enumeration.
- *Literature.* No comparable term-count threshold for purifiable processes is known to us. The Pauli-support viewpoint follows the stabiliser/Pauli-group literature (e.g. Gottesman 1997), and purifiability is from Araújo, Feix, Navascués and Brukner (2017).

**H.26.1a How the exhaustive proof works, with a worked example (Oct 25 2026) [exposition of H.26.1; the statement is PROVED in the per-class-pair model; worked-example numbers are exact reruns; h17/glue_k4_example.py, glue_k4_example.log (g = 3424), glue_k4_example_37.log (g = 37), glue_k4_example_orbit.py/.log, glue_k4_repro.log].** This subsection explains why "0 candidates and 0 UNRESOLVED over 2,722 geometries" proves H.26.1. It says what is computed, what is exact, and what is assumed, and it walks through one geometry by hand.

*(a) The statement and the model.*
- **Statement [PROVED, per-class-pair model].** Every admissible board with at most three Pauli terms per outcome is a PF blend: p_φ ∈ conv(PF) for every source state φ. Since H.13.2 gives an admissible four-term board outside conv(PF), four terms per outcome is the minimum. *Wording note:* the search does not look for "three-term decompositions" of anything. It looks for a **counterexample**, a ≤3-term board that is not a PF blend, and shows there is none.
- **Boards.** U_o = P_o V_κ(o) for the 8 outputs o ∈ {0,1}³. P_o is a Pauli string (the left Pauli) and V_κ(o) is a class operator of one of the three shapes of H.17.15(a): 1, α + iβA, or α + i(βA + γB) with A, B anticommuting.
- **What the search sees.** The search sees only *support data*:
  - (i) the class map o ↦ κ(o), and each class's label;
  - (ii) the seven left Paulis P_1, …, P_7, with P_0 = I (a common left Pauli changes nothing; H.17.17);
  - (iii) for each pair of classes c ≠ d, the support S_cd of V_dV_c†.

  The real parameters α, β, γ are forgotten. Every test the proof uses is a function of these data alone: admissibility through the Pauli rule (H.8.1), hardness, and SH_grp^r through Lemma D.
- **The model.** "Per-class-pair" means that each S_cd may be any pattern on the H.17.12 list for its two labels, chosen independently for each pair, subject only to Lemmas A–C. H.17.12(b), (c) are PROVED, so the lists contain every support that real parameters can produce. They may also contain supports that no parameters produce (over-approximation). So every real board's data occur in some job, and extra jobs can only add work.
- **The proof, in four steps.** Suppose some board B in the model is not a PF blend.
  1. B is *hard*. Otherwise a W-level-last party (H.1) or an operator-first party (H.6.2) makes it a PF blend.
  2. SH_grp^r fails for **every** absorbed class r. If it held for one r, H.17.11a would make B a PF blend.
  3. B has at most four classes, and ≤3 classes are closed (H.17.9, H.17.13–H.17.15). So B has exactly four classes, each on two vertices, and each non-identity class sits on an edge along its rigid direction (Theorem H.17.17, also in Lean).
  4. The search shows that no four-class support data pass 1 and 2. This contradicts the assumption.

*(b) From a large question to a finite list.*
- **Why a reduction is needed.** The data space is finite but large. There are 1,072 labels per class, 64 Paulis for each of 7 vertices (64⁷ ≈ 4.4·10¹²), and several patterns per class pair.
- **Geometries.** Step 3 fixes the shape. A *labelled geometry* is a pairing of the 8 vertices into 4 classes (105 pairings), with a label for each class. It must satisfy the class bound and Lemmas A and B: across every class-changing edge along q, some admissible pattern of the two labels is q-constant. Vertex 0 carries a non-identity class whose label is a G₄₈-orbit representative. This gives 21,906 labelled geometries (glue_k4enum.py).
- **Symmetries.** The group Γ has 8 × 48 = 384 elements:
  - the cube translations o ↦ o ⊕ t, which relabel the outcomes of the parties in t;
  - G₄₈, which permutes the qubits by π and S-conjugates the qubits in a mask m. It acts on Pauli strings and, through π, on cube coordinates and differences D at the same time (H.17.15(b), (d)).

  Every γ ∈ Γ maps admissible boards to admissible boards and PF blends to PF blends (H.17.15(d) [PROVED]). It also preserves hardness and the SH_grp tests, which are defined from the same supports, X-parts and differences D (a direct check from the definitions, argued here rather than machine-checked). γ preserves Pauli multiplication up to phase, so it maps the pattern list of (a, b) onto that of (γa, γb), and it maps the Lemma A–C filters onto themselves. Hence if a geometry Λ′ = γΛ, then γ is a bijection from the jobs of Λ to the jobs of Λ′ that carries counterexamples to counterexamples. **Every member of an orbit has the same answer**, so one geometry per orbit is enough.
- **Canonical representatives.** glue_k4canon.py applies all 384 elements of Γ to a geometry and renames the class ids in order of first appearance. The **orbit key** is the lexicographically smallest resulting tuple. The **canonical representative** of an orbit is its first geometry in enumeration order. There are 2,722 orbits, and glue_k4_canon.json lists their representatives (raw indices into glue_k4_tuples.json).
- **Empirical check of "same answer" [exact rerun] (glue_k4_example_orbit.log).** Running glue_k4.work on every member of two orbits, not just the representative, gives the same answer each time:
  - all 8 members of the orbit of g = 3424 give 1 job each, with 0 candidates and 0 UNRESOLVED;
  - all 24 members of the orbit of g = 37 give 3 jobs each, with 0 candidates and 0 UNRESOLVED;
  - the 167 non-canonical geometries from the first run are clean as well (H.26.1, audit).

*(c) What one job checks, and why "0 and 0" is a proof.*
- **A job.** A job is one canonical geometry together with one choice of S_cd for each of its 6 class pairs. The choices must survive Lemma A (|S| ≥ 2), Lemma B (a pair adjacent along q has a q-constant support), Lemma C (triple consistency: S_ab ⊆ S_ac·S_bc and cyclically) and the exact K = K′ list of H.17.12(b).
- **Unknowns and constraints.** The unknowns are P_1, …, P_7. Each ranges over the 64 Pauli strings, encoded as 6-bit integers s = x + 8z. For every ordered pair u ≠ w the board must obey the Pauli rule: no string of P_u ⊕ P_w ⊕ S_κ(u)κ(w) may be non-identity on every party of D = u ⊕ w. This is a 64 × 64 Boolean table for each pair.
- **Solver steps.** One job is solved in four steps:
  1. **Quick exit.** Run arc consistency on the Pauli rule alone. If some domain becomes empty, the job has no admissible board and is closed.
  2. **Root.** Any counterexample fails SH_grp^r for r = κ(0) at some pair (u₀, w₀) (step 2 of (a)). So the solver loops over the 28 pairs. For each pair it adds the Lemma D failure condition, which is a Boolean function of x(P_u₀ ⊕ P_w₀), as one more constraint. A counterexample is found under at least one of its failing pairs.
  3. **Search.** Arc consistency deletes a value only if it has no compatible value in some other domain, so it never deletes part of a complete solution. A depth-first search then fixes the vertex with the smallest domain, tries every remaining value, and re-runs arc consistency. Every complete assignment satisfying all constraints is reached; these are the leaves.
  4. **Leaf test.** At a leaf the solver discards the board if it is non-hard (W-level-last or operator-first party), or if SH_grp^r **holds** for some class r (Lemma D for all four classes). Both outcomes are PF blends.
- **Candidates and UNRESOLVED.** A *candidate* is a leaf that survives the leaf test. A job is *UNRESOLVED* if its DFS exceeds the node budget (BUDGET = 2,000,000 nodes, summed over the job's roots) before it finishes.
- **Why "0 and 0" is a proof.** A job with no candidate that is not UNRESOLVED has had its whole search space covered. So no counterexample has that job's data. If this holds for every job of every canonical geometry, then by (a) and (b) no counterexample exists in the model.
- **Exact or floating point? [exact; honest accounting].** Every step of glue_k4enum.py, glue_k4canon.py, glue_k4.py, glue_k4_audit.py and glue_k4_example.py is integer or Boolean arithmetic:
  - Pauli strings are bit vectors, and products up to phase are XORs;
  - the Pauli rule test is a bitwise AND;
  - the affine hulls of Lemma D are computed over F₂;
  - the numpy arrays are Boolean.

  There is no floating point and no tolerance anywhere in the proof chain. Phases and real parameters enter only through which supports are possible, and that was settled earlier: H.17.12(b) analytically, and H.17.12(c) by an exact enumeration with exact Pauli phases ±1, ±i. Floating point does appear in the H.17 numerics: the board searches, the NUMERICAL Lemma D cross-check on saved boards, and the facet checks a candidate would have needed. None of these is used by the proof.
- **What the computation relies on.** It relies on the correctness of the code, which is not formally verified, unlike the class bound (Lean). The mitigations are:
  - the independent audit (d);
  - the LOOSEN control, which shows the search does find hard boards when they exist;
  - the brute-force cross-check of the worked example (e);
  - the orbit-member reruns (b);
  - byte-identical re-enumeration (f).

*(d) Completeness: raw versus canonical indices.*
- **The possible confusion.** The search processes the 2,722 canonical geometries, but its checkpoint glue_k4_done.jsonl records each geometry by its **raw** index g, its position among the 21,906 entries of glue_k4_tuples.json. The log also prints "geometries N to do" at each restart: 21,706 at the first run, which started in raw order before the canonical filter existed, then 2,541 and 2,437 at restarts with the filter. The final SUMMARY counts only the last restart: 2,437 geometries and 2,719,567 jobs in 57,025 s on 3 cores. So no single log line says "2,722".
- **The resolution [PROVED by exhaustive check] (glue_k4_audit.py, glue_k4_audit.log).** The audit:
  - recomputes the orbit key of all 21,906 geometries, with its own key and bookkeeping code but the same group code (G, cls_act, pbits of t3sym.py);
  - finds exactly 2,722 orbits, with exactly one canonical entry in each;
  - finds a done record for 2,722 of the 2,722 canonical geometries, totalling 2,896,784 jobs, with no record (of 2,889, including 167 non-canonical) having a candidate or an UNRESOLVED job.

  The 167 non-canonical records are extra, not missing coverage.

*(e) Worked example: geometry g = 3424 [exact rerun] (glue_k4_example.log).*
- **The data.** glue_k4_tuples.json entry 3424 is cls = [[1, 8], [], [4, 32], [2, 16]], with pairing [(0,1), (2,5), (3,7), (4,6)]. In words, writing qubits as A B C and vertices as bit strings o_A o_B o_C:

  | class | vertices | edge | label | operator |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | 0 | 000, 100 | e_A | (XII, ZII) | α + i(β XII + γ ZII) |
  | 1 | 010, 101 | none (identity class; need not be an edge) | () | 1 |
  | 2 | 110, 111 | e_C | (IIX, IIZ) | α′ + i(β′ IIX + γ′ IIZ) |
  | 3 | 001, 011 | e_B | (IXI, IZI) | α″ + i(β″ IXI + γ″ IZI) |

  Each non-identity class acts on a single qubit, and sits on the edge along that qubit's direction (Lemma H).
- **Canonicalisation.** The orbit of this geometry under Γ meets the enumeration in 8 labelled geometries: 3424, 3430, 3598, 3604, 4576, 4582, 6205 and 6211. All 8 have the same orbit key. 3424 comes first in enumeration order, so it is the canonical representative, and the only one in glue_k4_canon.json.
- **Its single job.** Each class pair has only one admissible pattern, so the geometry spawns exactly **1 job**:
  - S_01 = {III, XII, ZII} (V_1V_0† = V_0†);
  - S_13 = {III, IXI, IZI} and S_12 = {III, IIX, IIZ}, likewise;
  - S_02, S_03 and S_23 are 9-string product supports such as S_03 = {III, XII, ZII, IXI, IZI, XXI, XZI, ZXI, ZZI}. Their Klein groups meet only in 1, so no cancellation is possible (H.17.12(a)).
- **The solver on this job:**
  - After plain arc consistency the domain sizes of P_0, …, P_7 are [1, 1, 4, 16, 16, 4, 16, 16].
  - For r = class 0, the Lemma D failure condition is possible at 24 of the 28 root pairs. Arc consistency empties 16 of them. The DFS searches the other 8: (2,3), (2,7), (3,4), (3,6), (4,5), (4,7), (5,6) and (6,7).
  - The search uses 1,368 DFS nodes and reaches 512 leaves, far below the budget.
  - **All 512 leaves are rejected because SH_grp^r holds for r = class 1 (the identity class)**, so each is a PF blend by H.17.11a. None is rejected for non-hardness.
  - The instrumented solver is a line-by-line copy of glue_k4.solve with counters, and its verdict is asserted equal to glue_k4.solve's. The checkpoint record is {'g': 3424, 'jobs': 1, 'cand': [], 'unres': 0}.
- **Brute-force cross-check [exact; independent of arc consistency and of the root logic, though it uses the same Pauli-rule tables and Lemma D formula].** Enumerating all P_1, …, P_7 directly gives exactly **64 admissible boards**, all of them hard. Each fails SH_grp^r for classes 0, 2 and 3, at 8 pairs each, and passes it for class 1. The two counts agree: 512 = 8 roots × 64 boards, because each board fails the class-0 test at all 8 searched root pairs.
- **Why the identity class rescues these boards.** Take the board with all P_o = I. Absorbing class 1 (V = 1) leaves W_o = V_κ(o), so Aff_o is spanned by the X-parts of the class's own strings: A for class 0, C for class 2, B for class 3. Each class sits on the edge along its own qubit. For every pair of vertices in different classes, D = o ⊕ q contains a qubit that neither class touches (checked for all 24 such pairs), so no vector of Aff_o ⊕ Aff_q covers D, and SH_grp^1 holds. Absorbing class 0 instead, the ratios V_κ(o)V_0† have the larger 9-string supports, and the test fails at 8 pairs. *The choice of absorbed class matters, which is why the leaf asks for failure for every r.*
- **The search is not vacuous here.** The LOOSEN = 1 control on this job (leaf = any hard admissible board) finds one immediately, all P_o = I. These hard boards are excluded by the SH test, not by an empty search space.
- **A contrast, g = 37 (glue_k4_example_37.log).** Classes (XII) on {000, 100}, identity on {010, 110}, (IXI) on {001, 011}, and (IXI, IZI) on {101, 111}.
  - This geometry spawns 3 jobs (S_23 ∈ {III, IXI, IZI, IYI}, {IXI, IZI, IYI}, {III, IZI, IYI}).
  - Each job has 256 admissible boards (brute force) and reaches 1,024 = 4 × 256 leaves.
  - Every leaf is discarded because **party C is operator-first**: no class acts on qubit C. So this geometry is closed by H.6.2, not by SH_grp. The LOOSEN control finds 0 hard boards.
- **Four terms, by contrast.** The H.13.2 board, which is not a PF blend (certificate facet 16962), cannot be written in this model at all: its outcome unitaries have four Pauli terms, so they are not of the shapes in (a). The threshold sits exactly at the boundary of the model's shapes.

*(f) How to reproduce [exact; box timings].*
- **Commands.** Run from code/stochastic/cyclic/switchboard/h17, with Python 3 and numpy:
  - `python3 glue_adj.py` writes /tmp/glue_adj.pkl (rigid sets and neighbour lists);
  - `python3 glue_k4enum.py` writes glue_k4_tuples.json;
  - `python3 glue_k4canon.py` writes glue_k4_canon.json.

  These three take about 166 s in total on one core. A fresh scratch rerun reproduces all three outputs byte for byte (glue_k4_repro.log).
- **The search.** `NPROC=3 nohup nice -n 5 python3 glue_k4.py >> glue_k4.log 2>&1 &` is resumable from glue_k4_done.jsonl. The last segment, 2,437 geometries, took 57,025 s (about 15.8 h) on 3 cores. Earlier segments covered the rest.
- **Checks.**
  - `python3 glue_k4_audit.py` takes about 30 s.
  - `python3 glue_k4_example.py 3424` (and `… 37`) takes about 30 s, mostly spent recomputing orbit keys.
  - `python3 glue_k4_example_orbit.py` takes about 65 s.
- **sha256 of the inputs and outputs:**
  - glue_k4_tuples.json 9accd2a0…a2650;
  - glue_k4_canon.json 69f0f830…93b64;
  - glue_k4_done.jsonl cac852c2…7794f;
  - glue_k4.log 8fe7a885…cbd7;
  - glue_k4_audit.log 9d72fce7…83fa;
  - /tmp/glue_adj.pkl e0595bed…2ace;
  - glue_k4.py 46a6b430…a78d.

  The full hashes are in glue_k4_repro.log and in the commit history.

*(g) What the result does not say.*
- **Scope.** It is PROVED in the per-class-pair model only. Two things are assumed, not proved here:
  - that every ≤3-term outcome unitary of an admissible board has one of the three shapes with the H.17.12 pair supports (the model's hypotheses);
  - that the code is correct; it is not formally verified, and only the class bound and Lemma H are in Lean.
- **It is about boards.** It says nothing about non-Switchboard processes, about more than three parties, or about H.12's causal-inequality value.
- **Labels.** Labels as in H.21:
  - the class bound and the symmetries are PROVED (paper, and Lean for the class bound);
  - the enumeration, canonicalisation, search and audit are PROVED by exact computation;
  - the worked-example counts are exact reruns;
  - the cross-checks of H.17.12(d) and Lemma D on saved boards are NUMERICAL and are not used.
- **Literature.**
  - Exhaustive computer proofs that combine a symmetry reduction with a complete, budgeted search and an independent audit are a standard pattern; the four-colour theorem (Appel and Haken 1977; Robertson, Sanders, Seymour and Thomas 1997) is the classic example.
  - Arc consistency is from constraint satisfaction (Mackworth 1977).
  - We claim only this application.

**H.26.2 The H.12 lower end: lift-A ∩ lift-B ∩ lift-C certifies y_A ≥ −1.15861 [lower bound PROVED, exact integer certificate; solver values NUMERICAL; embed/h17_lift_scs.py, h17_lift_verify.py, h17_lift_scs_ABC.log, h17_lift_verify_ABC.log].** Target 1 of Round 19, completing H.24.4. The three-family solve, started in Round 17 with SCS's indirect solver at eps 10⁻⁵, finished as "solved" after 9,067 s (about 1,000 iterations; residuals 2.8·10⁻⁵ and 1.8·10⁻⁵ at iteration 750). Its primal objective was −1.155758, and the float dual bound, with eigenvalue deficits subtracted, was −1.1586026.
- *Exact verification [PROVED].* h17_lift_verify.py rounds every multiplier to the dyadic grid 2⁻³⁰, applies all maps exactly in integers, and certifies each cone shift by Bareiss–Sylvester, as in H.23.3. The family contributions are +1.391271 (A), −1.632892 (B) and −0.916989 (C). Result: **min over all instruments of y_A·P ≥ −151861226318490447/131072000000000000 ≈ −1.1586092**.
- **Status.** The optimum of y_A over all instruments lies in **[−1.15861, −0.70580]** [PROVED both ends]. The gap shrinks from 0.4598 to 0.4528. Adding the third family gains only 0.0070, against 0.038 for the second (H.23.3). So the lift-family hierarchy seems to saturate far from −0.70580: one more copied party per family will not close the gap. A relaxation that sees the rank-one (projective) structure of the optimum, as in H.26.3, is needed.

**H.26.3 The H.12 upper end: within the deterministic-output projective family, nothing beats −0.70607, and −0.70580 is attained [family bound PROVED for all 64 output patterns by exact SOS certificates; level-2 tightness NUMERICAL; optimality over all instruments OPEN; embed/h19_h12_lasserre.py/.log, h19_h12_sos_cert.py, h19_h12_sos_cert_21.log, h19_h12_sos_cert_41.log, h19_h12_sos_cert_patterns.log, h19_h12_sos_cert_patterns_full.log].** Target 3 of Round 19.
- *Cost of interval branch-and-bound [estimate, not run].* On (S²)⁶ f is affine in each n_{k,x} separately, so one party's two vectors can be minimised in closed form (c − |g₀| − |g₁|). That leaves 8 angles. To separate −0.70580 from the next local optimum (−0.65969) with first-order interval bounds needs boxes of width about 10⁻², i.e. about (2π/10⁻²)⁸ ≈ 10²² boxes. This is infeasible on the box, so a moment relaxation was used instead.
- *Family.* Deterministic outputs o_{k,x} (64 patterns O), with projective a-measurements along unit vectors n_{k,x}. By multilinearity this also covers outputs drawn at random independently of the qubit, with the measurement chosen per output. For each O, f_O is a cubic in 18 variables on (S²)⁶.
- *Level-2 moment relaxation [NUMERICAL].* The relaxation has a 190×190 moment matrix (monomials of degree ≤ 2) and the sphere equalities localised up to degree 4, solved with SCS. Results:
  - The two smallest bounds are −0.7058023, for patterns 21 (o = x for every party, the H.25.2 optimum) and 41 (its mirror image).
  - Next is −0.6596879 (patterns 22 and 42), which matches the second local optimum −0.65969 found by the Round 17 search.
  - All other patterns are ≥ −0.5681.

  So the relaxation is numerically tight at both known local optima.
- *Exact certificates [PROVED for the patterns listed in the log].* h19_h12_sos_cert.py solves the dual, f_O − λ = mᵀGm + Σ_i h_i(|n_i|² − 1) with G ⪰ ε·1. It rounds the coefficients of f_O, which lie in ℚ(√2), to rationals with total error ≤ 2·10⁻²⁹, rounds G and h to dyadics, and moves the coefficient residual exactly into G. It then proves G − (ε/4)·1 positive definite by integer Bareiss elimination. Patterns 21 and 41 were run with ε = 10⁻⁵, and **each gives min f ≥ −0.7060707**, against the achieved −0.7058022 (H.25.2). The other 62 patterns were run with ε = 2·10⁻⁴ and are all certified; the smallest of their bounds is −0.6650886 (patterns 22 and 42). Every one of the 64 certificates passed both exact checks (identity and positive definiteness); see h19_h12_sos_cert_patterns.log.
- **What is proved [PROVED].** Let 𝓕 be the set of strategies in which every party k, for each setting x, emits an output o_{k,x} determined by x, and obtains a by a two-outcome qubit POVM E_{a|k,x} = (1 + (−1)^a n_{k,x}·σ)/2 with |n_{k,x}| ≤ 1. That is, the POVM is projective or unsharp, but unbiased (Tr E_a = 1). All 64 output patterns are included. Because y_A·P is affine in each n_{k,x} separately, the minimum over the balls equals the minimum over the spheres, which is the certified one. By the same multilinearity, the bound also covers every convex mixture of such strategies per party and setting, e.g. outputs drawn at random independently of the qubit. On 𝓕, **min y_A·P ∈ [−0.7060707, −0.7058022]**: the lower end comes from the SOS certificates and the upper end from the explicit H.25.2 strategy. Numerically (level-2 values) the minimum on 𝓕 is −0.70580 exactly, attained only at patterns 21 and 41, and the runner-up is −0.65969.
- **What is not proved.** This is **not** global optimality over all instruments. Optimality over **all** instruments stays **OPEN**. By H.15.7d every strategy reduces to 4-outcome qubit POVMs E_{a,o|k,x}, and 𝓕 misses three kinds of them:
  - biased two-outcome a-measurements, including the trivial one a = const;
  - POVMs whose output o is correlated with the qubit outcome (e.g. o = a);
  - rank-one 3- and 4-outcome POVMs.

  Treating the first two kinds as output labelings of a projective measurement already gives 16⁶ ≈ 1.7·10⁷ polynomial systems, and the third needs a relaxation in about 96 variables. Neither was attempted. Block optimality over all POVMs at the H.25.2 point is NUMERICAL only (H.25.2). The interval over all instruments remains [−1.15861, −0.70580] (H.26.2). Literature: Lasserre (SIAM J. Optim. 11, 796, 2001); Parrilo (Math. Program. 96, 293, 2003); exact rounding of SOS certificates as in Peyrl and Parrilo (Theor. Comput. Sci. 409, 269, 2008).

**H.27 Round 20: the ten open orbits against PF-wired quantum boxes (Oct 25 2026).** The target is the ten facet orbits of conv(PF) that no pure process has yet been shown to violate: 26, 28, 30, 31, 54, 57, 66, 69, 75 and 81, which together contain 1,536 facets. Each orbit's representative facet reaches exactly −1/6 on Valid (H.25.4), so any crossing, and any proof that the facet holds on Purif, must use purity. H.27.1 proves that a large, explicitly purifiable family never crosses these orbits: every forward PF lift, in any dimension, with any node unitaries, frames and source. H.27.2 reports a numerical search over the time-reversed counterpart of that family, which is the family that crossed the other twelve orbits in H.22.2. The orbits stay open.

**H.27.1 Forward PF lifts are PF-wired quantum boxes, and no PF-wired no-signalling box crosses the ten open orbits [reduction PROVED; certificate PROVED, exact rational LP duals; whole-hull statement NUMERICAL; embed/h20_common.py, h20_fwd_enum.py/.json/.log, h20_fwd_ns.py/.log, h20_fwd_cert.py/.log, h20_fwd_cert_all.py/.log, h20_fwd_ns_all.py/.log].**
- *The family (defined here).* A **forward PF lift** starts from a process function w: ∏_k A_k → ∏_k X_k on finite alphabets. It is the board V|s⟩_P|a⟩_{AO} = (⊗_k U_{k,w_k(a)})|s⟩_{AI} ⊗ |a⟩_F. Each node wire AI_k may have any finite dimension, U_{k,x} are arbitrary unitaries on it, the source ρ on P is arbitrary, and the agents act as everywhere in this paper: agent k measures its input with any POVM {M^k_{i_k}} (outcome i_k ∈ {0,1}) and prepares a state σ_k(o_k) on its output wire for the classical output o_k ∈ {0,1}. The family contains the Lugano purification L, every *untransposed* generalised Lugano board L_U of H.22.2 in every frame, the translation lift of every PF (Section 1, Appendix F), and their controlled-unitary dressings.
- *Every statistic of the family lies in Purif [PROVED].* Give each agent an extra input register X_k, and feed it from the translation lift |e⟩|a⟩ ↦ |e + w(a)⟩_X|a⟩_F with source |0⟩. That lift is a valid pure process (Section 1, Appendix A; F.1–F.3 for group alphabets). Inside its own laboratory, agent k first applies the controlled unitary Σ_x |x⟩⟨x|_{X_k} ⊗ U_{k,x} to (X_k, AI_k), then runs its instrument on AI_k and ignores X_k. That is a local operation, so the result is again a quantum process with a purification. Its statistics equal those of V, because F already records a, so X_k carries no coherence that the agent could use.
- **Lemma H.27.1a (reduction to PF-wired boxes) [PROVED].** Because F records a, the statistics of a forward PF lift are p(i|o) = Σ_a q(a|o) P_Q(i | w(a)). Here q(a|o) = ∏_k ⟨a_k|σ_k(o_k)|a_k⟩ is local stochastic pre-processing, and P_Q(i|x) = Tr[ρ ⊗_k U_{k,x_k}† M^k_{i_k} U_{k,x_k}] is a tripartite **quantum box** with settings x_k. For a fixed box, a facet value is multilinear in (q_A, q_B, q_C), so its minimum is attained at deterministic q_k, a_k = h_k(o_k). Then p(i|o) = P_Q(i | w′(o)) with w′ = w ∘ h, a **refined process function**: binary outputs, setting x_k = w′_k(o_{−k}), such that every local 2-colouring g of the settings makes g ∘ w′ a binary PF, which it is because a PF composed with local maps is a PF. Only the partition of the four values of o_{−k} into the fibres of w′_k matters, since the setting labels can be absorbed into the box. An exhaustive enumeration of partition triples (h20_fwd_enum.log) gives **186 refined PFs, 20 of them maximal**, meaning not a coarsening of another. A box on coarser settings is a box on finer settings that ignores the distinction, so the 20 maximal ones suffice. Eight have block counts (2,2,2): these are the Lugano function up to relabellings. The other twelve have a party with a single block, which acts first.
- **Theorem H.27.1 [PROVED; exact rational arithmetic].** For every one of the 20 maximal refined PFs w′, every **no-signalling** box P(i|x) (binary outcomes, settings = blocks), and every facet of orbits 26, 28, 30, 31, 54, 57, 66, 69, 75 and 81, the classical process P(i|w′(o)) satisfies the facet. Hence **no forward PF lift crosses any of the ten open orbits**, and neither does any mixture of forward PF lifts. This holds for every PF on any finite alphabets, every dimension, every node unitary, every frame and every source.
  - *Proof (h20_fwd_cert_all.py).* Write g(P) for the facet value of P ∘ w′. It is affine in P. The NS polytope's affine hull is spanned by the local deterministic boxes D, and each D ∘ w′ is a PF. So g is the unique affine function on the NS space that takes the integer facet value of the PF D ∘ w′ at D, and its coefficients c, c₀ are solved exactly in rationals. HiGHS minimises c·P over the NS polytope and returns a support and a float dual. The script then solves A_Z^T y = c_Z exactly on the support Z, verifies in Fraction arithmetic that every reduced cost c − A^T y is ≥ 0, and checks that b·y + c₀ equals the minimum over local boxes (0 or 1 in integer facet units). Then g(P) ≥ b·y + c₀ ≥ 0 for every NS box. All 20 × 10 (refined PF, representative facet) pairs pass ("ALL 20 x 10 CERTIFIED"; h20_fwd_cert.log repeats the check for the eight Lugano-type PFs). Party permutations and bit flips map refined PFs to refined PFs and boxes to boxes, so the family is invariant under the 384-element group and one facet per orbit covers all 1,536 (H.23.1a).
  - *The NS minimum equals the local minimum in every case.* So within forward PF lifts, quantumness of the box, and even post-quantum no-signalling correlations, buy nothing on these orbits.
- *Whole hull [NUMERICAL].* For the eight Lugano-type refined PFs, a float LP over all 29,344 facets gives an NS minimum ≥ −4.5·10⁻¹⁵ (h20_fwd_ns_all.log). Numerically, then, Lugano-wired no-signalling boxes never leave conv(PF). The twelve single-block PFs give causally wired boxes, which are expected to be causal and hence PF blends; that was not computed for all facets. This is consistent with H.15.8: H.12's time reverse, a frame-rotated *forward* √X-Lugano board, stays inside conv(PF), while H.12 itself, a time-reversed board, does not.
- *Reading [INTERPRETIVE].* Every crossing of conv(PF) by a pure process in this paper (H.12, H.13.2, H.22.2) uses a time-reversed or frame-rotated time-reversed Lugano-type board. H.27.1 explains half of this: forward PF wiring of any box, quantum or not, cannot cross the ten open orbits, and numerically cannot cross any facet. Whatever crosses them, if anything does, must use coherence in the agents' inputs that is not recorded in F.

**H.27.2 Time-reversed PF lifts with qudit node wires: no crossing found [family purity PROVED with the CITED time-reversal step; search NUMERICAL; embed/h20_trev_search.py, h20_trev_validate*.log, h20_trev_open_*.log, h20_trev_summary.py/.log, h20_trev_best.npz].**
- *The family.* Take a forward lift of a maximal refined PF w′ as in H.27.1, with binary output wires, node wires of dimension D_k and node unitaries U_{k,x}, and reverse it in time (V^T). The agents now read the binary old-output wire in an SU(2) frame e_k (outcome i_k), and write a state φ_k(o_k) on the old node wire. The source ψ lives on the 8-dimensional old F. The statistics are p(i|o) = ψ† G_{io} ψ with G_{io}[a,b] = ∏_k conj(e_k(i_k)_{a_k}) e_k(i_k)_{b_k} ⟨U_{k,w′_k(a)} φ_k(o_k), U_{k,w′_k(b)} φ_k(o_k)⟩. Equivalently, the outputs o choose a Schur-product (dephasing) channel ρ ↦ ρ ∘ K_o on a shared three-qubit state, where K_o is a correlation matrix built from the Gram matrices of the vectors U_{k,x} φ_k(o). The agents then measure their qubits in fixed frames. D_k = 2n_k, with n_k the number of blocks, loses no generality: any Gram matrix of the 2n_k vectors U_{k,x}φ_k(o) is realised in that dimension. Projective measurements suffice: a trivial measurement makes that party first, and the remaining two-party process is a PF blend (Appendix G).
- *Purity.* A refined PF is a PF with non-binary inputs: for any local intervention, the composite is a binary PF and so has a unique fixed point. Its forward lift is therefore a valid pure process (Section 1, Appendix A, plus the controlled-unitary step of H.27.1), and so is its time reverse (Guérin–Brukner 2018, App. D [CITED]; as in H.22.2).
- *Search.* We used L-BFGS (torch, float64) on the Rayleigh quotient ψ†Mψ/ψ†ψ, jointly in ψ and the board, with M = Σ T_f G + c_f 1 for the orbit's representative facet f. We reported the exact minimum over ψ, the smallest eigenvalue, at the end. Each (refined PF, orbit) pair got 300 random starts at three scales. Minimising the smallest eigenvalue directly was unreliable: it stalls on the 0 plateau of classical-like boards and missed orbit 14 entirely in 480 starts (h20_trev_validate2.log, h20_trev_validate3.log).
- *Validation on the crossed orbits [NUMERICAL].* The search finds the crossings that are already known.
  - With the eigenvalue objective (all 20 refined PFs, 10 starts each; h20_trev_validate.log, an earlier representative-facet version of the script), it crosses orbits 4–11 at relative value −0.0339663.
  - With the Rayleigh objective and 300 starts (h20_trev_validate4.log), it reaches −0.0464717 on orbits 14 and 25, H.22.2's optimum exactly (Lugano-type w′ = index 10), −0.0154744 on orbit 53 (H.22.2 certified −0.0154741) and −0.0098036 on orbit 79 (H.22.2 certified −0.0067548).
  - The larger per-facet values on orbits 4–11 and 79 do not contradict H.22.2, whose boards were optimised jointly over several orbits. They are float optima and are not certified. On orbits 4–11 the value −0.0339663 is a per-facet optimum found with qudit node wires (D_k = 4). Whether qubit node wires reach it was not tested; the QD=2 switch exists in the script but was not run.
- **Result on the ten open orbits [NUMERICAL, negative].** Searches finished for 8 of the 20 maximal refined PFs: the Lugano-type indices 4, 5, 7, 10, 11 and the single-block indices 12, 13, 16 (h20_trev_open_*.log, h20_trev_summary.log), with 300 starts per (refined PF, orbit). The runs for Lugano-type indices 6, 14, 15 and for the remaining nine single-block indices were stopped before finishing, so they are **not** covered. On every completed pair the best relative value is 0 to the 10⁻⁸ rounding of the log: the representative facet is reached (tight) but never crossed. On the single-block PFs, orbits 54, 57 and 75 do not even reach 0 (best value 1/3). **No time-reversed PF lift in this search crosses any of the ten orbits.** *Update (Round 21, H.28):* the completed search on all 20 refined PFs crosses orbit 26 (w′ = index 6, 1 of 300 starts), and the phase-board subfamily crosses orbits 26, 30 and 31, all exactly certified (Theorem H.28.1). The general search missed orbits 30 and 31. Because only five of the eight Lugano-type variants were run, the representative-facet search does not yet cover every facet of each orbit up to symmetry.
- *Caveats.* These are local searches with random starts, and the validation shows that hits can be rare: orbit 14 needed about 300 starts. A negative result therefore bounds what this optimiser finds, not what the family contains. The family does not include agent instruments whose prepared state depends on the measured outcome, nor boards that are not PF-controlled, nor mixtures of forward and time-reversed wiring on different parties.

**H.27.3 Status of the ten orbits after Round 20 [summary; labels as stated; superseded by H.28.3: orbits 26, 30, 31 are now crossed].**
- *Crossed with certificate:* none.
- *Bound proved:* for all ten orbits, a **partial** result. Every facet holds on the forward-PF-lift family and its convex hull (Theorem H.27.1, PROVED). This is not a proof that the facets hold on all of Purif, which stays **OPEN**.
- *Numerical best value:* all ten orbits: best value found 0 (tight, not crossed) on time-reversed PF lifts (H.27.2, 8 of 20 refined PFs completed), and provably ≥ 0 on forward PF lifts (H.27.1). Untouched: none, but the coverage of H.27.2 is partial.
- *Not attempted this round:* an NPA-type quantum bound, which is moot because the NS bound already closes the forward family; a purity-based SDP/SOS relaxation of all of Purif; and perturbative searches around the −1/6 point of Valid.
- *What would close an orbit.* A crossing would need a time-reversed, or otherwise not-F-recorded, board beyond H.27.2's search. A proof of validity on Purif would need an argument covering time-reversed lifts. By H.22.2 such an argument must distinguish these ten orbits from the twelve that time-reversed Lugano does cross.

**H.28 Round 21: orbits 26, 30 and 31 are crossed by PF phase boards; seven orbits remain open (Oct 26 2026).** Round 21 targeted the ten open orbits of H.27. Three of them are now crossed, each with an exact certificate checked by two independent programs: orbits 26, 30 and 31. That makes **15 of the 22 genuine never-crossed orbits crossed** (twelve in H.22.2 plus these three). The seven still open are 28, 54, 57, 66, 69, 75 and 81, which hold 960 facets. All crossings come from one simple subfamily of the time-reversed PF lifts of H.27.2, the *PF phase boards* defined in H.28.1.

**H.28.1 PF phase boards cross orbits 26, 30 and 31 [crossing PROVED, exact Gaussian-rational certificates from two independent checkers; purity PROVED for the certified boards by an exact validity check, and for the whole family by H.27.2 with the CITED time-reversal step; orbit-level statement PROVED by symmetry and checked directly; embed/h21_trev_search.py, h21_trev_check.py, h21_trev_cert.py, h21_trev_fullcheck.py, h21_trev_basin.py, h21_phase_search.py, h21_phase2trev.py, h21_cert_indep.py and their logs, h21_trev_cand_w6_o26.npy, h21_phase_cand_w4_o{26,30,31}.npy and _cert.json].**
- *How it was found.* We completed H.27.2's search on all 20 maximal refined PFs (300 starts per pair, h21_trev_open_*.log). It produced exactly one crossing: orbit 26, on the Lugano-type refined PF w′ = index 6, in 1 of 300 starts, at relative value −2.42872·10⁻⁵. The optimum had a special Gram structure. For each party, the vectors U_{k,x}φ_k(o) for the two outputs o are orthogonal, and for a fixed o the vectors for different blocks x differ only by a phase. Imposing that structure gives the following family.
- *Definition (PF phase board).* Fix a refined PF w′ (H.27.1). Party k has a block function w′_k(z_{−k}) of the other two parties' bits, and a real phase table θ_k(o_k, x), with x ranging over the blocks of w′_k. The qubit board is
  V |z⟩_P |o⟩_{AO} = exp(i Σ_k θ_k(o_k, w′_k(z_{−k}))) |z⟩_{AI} |o⟩_F,
  read with SU(2) frames on the agents' inputs and a source state ψ on P. In words, each agent receives its own past qubit unchanged, and its output goes to the future carrying a phase. That phase depends on the agent's own output and, through the refined PF, on the other two agents' inputs. It is a phase process in the sense of H.21.1, with a phase that is a sum of single-party terms routed by a refined PF. It is also the time-reversed PF lift of H.27.2 with qubit node wires, diagonal node unitaries U_{k,x} = diag(e^{iθ_k(0,x)}, e^{iθ_k(1,x)}) and φ_k(o) = |o⟩.
- *Relation to H.12 [checked, h21 session].* The phase form of H.12 (H.21.1) has this shape. Agent A's phase term is o_A(z_B∨z_C), B's is o_B z_A z_C and C's is o_C z_B(1−z_A), with phase steps π, π/2 and π, and the factor i^{pre(z)} depends on z only and can be absorbed into ψ. The three block indicators z_B∨z_C, z_A z_C and z_B(1−z_A) form a refined PF, which matches w′ = index 14 of the enumeration up to block labels (is_refined_pf, h20_common.py). So H.12 is a PF phase board with phases fixed to multiples of π/2. Rounds 15–20 never freed those phases continuously in this way.
- *Purity.* For the family: a PF phase board is a time-reversed PF lift, which is pure by H.27.2 (forward-lift validity PROVED; time reversal CITED, Guérin–Brukner 2018, App. D). For the three certified boards we also did not rely on the citation. h21_cert_indep.py builds the 64×64 unitary V′ (board plus frames) in exact Gaussian-rational arithmetic and checks two things. First, V′ is exactly unitary. Second, the Araújo et al. validity projector conditions on its process matrix hold with residual exactly 0, with Tr W = 64 = d_O·d_P (the same projector as H.23.1b). A float closed-loop test with random agent unitaries (h21_trev_fullcheck.py) agrees.
- *Validity is not automatic.* Phases θ_k(o_k, z_{−k}) that are *not* routed through a refined PF generally give invalid boards. Random examples have Araújo penalty 26–88, while the one-party case is valid (h21_diag_tangent.log). At Φ = 0 the penalty Hessian vanishes identically, so the tangent test gives no information there. The span of refined-PF phase patterns has dimension 19.
- **Theorem H.28.1 [PROVED; exact arithmetic].** There are pure (purifiable) three-party processes that violate a facet of each of orbits 26, 30 and 31. Values are for the representative facet in integer units (maximum PF value 3), with relative value = value/3. Each was computed exactly from a Gaussian-rational board: Cayley-rationalised phases and frames with denominator 10⁶, exactly modulus 1 and exactly unitary, and a rational source normalised exactly.

  | orbit | facet | refined PF w′ | exact value | relative value |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | 26 | 240 | index 4 (phase board) | −7.2861609·10⁻⁵ (checker B); −7.2861612·10⁻⁵ (checker A) | −2.4287203·10⁻⁵; −2.4287204·10⁻⁵ |
  | 26 | 240 | index 6 (general time-reversed lift, D_k = 4; checker A only) | −7.2861610·10⁻⁵ | −2.4287203·10⁻⁵ |
  | 30 | 245 | index 4 (phase board) | −2.6894965·10⁻² (both checkers) | −8.9649883·10⁻³ |
  | 31 | 247 | index 4 (phase board) | −2.6894965·10⁻² (both checkers) | −8.9649883·10⁻³ |

  Each p is exactly valid: it is non-negative and normalised under all 64 deterministic interventions. Two programs check each phase-board certificate. Checker A, h21_trev_cert.py (applied after h21_phase2trev.py; h21_phase_cert_w4_o*.log), works from the Gram formula of H.27.2. It also confirms that the facet functional is exact on all 744 PFs. Checker B, h21_cert_indep.py, was written separately with its own exact complex-rational class. It builds the explicit unitary and recomputes p and the facet value from scratch. Each checker rationalises the float point on its own, so they certify slightly different rational boards. For orbits 30 and 31 the values agree to 10 digits; for orbit 26 they differ in the 8th digit. Both values are exact and negative.
- *Whole orbits.* The 384-element symmetry group maps Purif to itself (Proposition H.23.1a), so one violated facet means every facet of the orbit is violated by some pure process. We also checked this directly. For each certified p, the 384 images violate all 192 facets of its orbit (192/192 for each of 26, 30 and 31), while p itself violates exactly one facet among the ten H.27 orbits (h21_cert_indep_w4_o*.log).
- *Robustness [NUMERICAL].* Basin hopping with 300 perturbations in the general family from the orbit-26 point does not improve on −2.4287204·10⁻⁵ (h21_trev_basin_w6_o26_s1.log). So it is a stable local optimum, not a rounding artefact. The optimal phase steps δ_k(x) = θ_k(1, x) − θ_k(0, x) are not recognisable angles. For orbit 26 they are (in units of π) [[−0.543, 0.413], [−0.417, 0.678], [−0.065, 0.440]]; for orbit 30 they are [[0.116, 0.780], [−0.636, 0.701], [−0.228, 0.772]]. Orbit 26's violation is very small, about 3·10⁻⁴ of orbit 30's.

**H.28.2 Searches on the seven remaining orbits: nothing crosses [NUMERICAL, negative; embed/h21_phase_open_*.log, h21_trev_open_*.log, h21_trev_basin_fromphase_w4_o*.log, h21_inst_search.py, h21_inst_valcheck.py, h21_inst_w4_R*.log].**
- *Validation of the phase-board search.* On the crossed orbits, the phase-board search (L-BFGS on the Rayleigh quotient as in H.27.2, 500 starts, about 0.14 s per start) reproduces H.27.2's float optima, with far higher hit rates than the general search: −0.046472 on orbits 14 and 25, −0.015474 on 53, −0.0098036 on 79 and −0.033966 on 4 (h21_phase_validate.log).
- *Phase boards on all 20 refined PFs, 500 starts per (rule, orbit).* All eight Lugano-type rules (indices 4, 5, 6, 7, 10, 11, 14, 15) cross orbits 30 and 31, in 19–39 of 500 starts (6–8%). Rules 4, 5, 6, 7, 10, 11 and 14 (all but 15) also cross orbit 26, in 2–5 of 500 starts. The twelve single-block rules cross nothing. On orbits 28, 54, 57, 66, 69, 75 and 81 the best value on every rule is 0 to about 10⁻¹⁵ (tight, never crossed), or 1/3 where the orbit cannot even be reached. All 20 rules finished (h21_phase_open_*.log). A negative result bounds what this optimiser finds, not what the family contains.
- *General time-reversed lifts (H.27.2 family), now on all 20 rules.* 300 starts per pair (h21_trev_open_*.log). The only crossing is orbit 26 on index 6, and the value on all seven remaining orbits is 0. This search **missed orbits 30 and 31 completely**, even though they sit inside its own family and the phase boards hit them in 6–8% of starts. That is a direct measure of how weak a negative result from the general search is.
- *Basin hopping from the phase optima.* For each of the seven orbits we started from the best w′ = 4 phase board and made 80 perturbation hops in the general family (D_k = 4). The best value stays at about 0 (h21_trev_basin_fromphase_w4_o*.log).
- *Agent instruments and mixed wiring.* h21_inst_search.py lets each agent prepare a state that depends on the measured outcome (Kraus rank L = 2), and lets a set R of parties use time-reversed rather than forward wiring (R is a bitmask; R = 0 is the forward family of H.27.1, and R = 7, all parties time-reversed, contains H.27.2 and the PF phase boards). A float validity check gives deviation 0 for all R (h21_inst_valcheck.py). Runs on w′ = index 4 with 40 starts each over orbits 30, 28, 54, 57, 66, 69, 75 and 81: for every R = 1, …, 7 the best value on every orbit is 0 to about 10⁻¹⁴, or 1/3 where it cannot be reached. These runs do not cross even orbit 30, although R = 7 contains the phase boards that cross it, so at 40 starts this search is weak. R = 0 is settled by Theorem H.27.1 and was not run. *(Corrected in Round 22: the first version of this paragraph had the meaning of R reversed.)* Caveat: partial time reversal is not guaranteed to give a valid process in general, so any hit from this script would need an exact purity check. With 40 starts it also misses orbit 14's known crossing, so its negatives are weak.
- *Not done this round.* Arbitrary diagonal phase boards Φ(o, z) with a validity penalty: the search was too slow (more than 14 minutes for 10 starts) and was stopped. Also not done: boards with non-diagonal node unitaries on qubit node wires outside the H.27.2 search, SOS/SDP relaxations of Purif, and perturbative searches around the −1/6 point of Valid.

**H.28.3 Status of the 22 genuine orbits after Round 21 [summary; labels as stated].**
- *Crossed, exactly certified (15):* orbits 4–11, 14, 25 (H.22.2), 53, 79 (H.22.2), 26, 30, 31 (H.28.1). Each is crossed at orbit level by symmetry.
- *Open (7; 960 facets):* 28 (192 facets), 54 (96), 57 (192), 66 (96), 69 (192), 75 (96), 81 (96). Proved: they hold on every forward PF lift and its convex hull (Theorem H.27.1). Numerically, none of the searches above crosses them: the best value is 0, and on several single-block rules it is 1/3. Whether they hold on Purif is **OPEN**.
- *Reading [INTERPRETIVE].* All 15 crossed orbits are crossed by time-reversed Lugano-type boards. One pattern may be suggestive but has not been analysed. Six of the seven open orbits (all but 28) have representative facets that some single-block rules cannot even reach: their best value there is 1/3. None of the newly crossed orbits 26, 30 and 31 behaves this way. A proof that the seven hold on Purif would have to tell them apart from 26, 30 and 31, which phase boards cross at phases with no evident special angles.

**H.29 Round 22: pure processes with a classical future record, a reduction of the time-reversed lifts to phase boards, and all valid phase processes; the seven orbits stay open (Oct 26 2026).** Round 22 targeted the seven open orbits 28, 54, 57, 66, 69, 75 and 81 (960 facets). **None is crossed.** The counts stay at 15 of 22 crossed and 7 open. The round produced three structural results and one search family that passes validation. The structural results are an exact validity criterion for pure processes whose future records the agents' outputs (H.29.1), a lemma that reduces the time-reversed PF lifts of H.27.2 to PF phase boards (H.29.2), and an exact classification of the valid three-qubit phase processes (H.29.3). The search family is the exact per-component phase-process search, which reproduces orbits 26 and 30. Penalty-based searches, by contrast, failed validation, so their negative results carry little weight (H.29.4). Code and logs are in code/stochastic/cyclic/embed/h22_*.

**H.29.1 Classical-output pure processes (COPP): exact validity criterion [criterion PROVED by derivation and cross-checked numerically; tangent probe NUMERICAL; h22_copp.py, h22_copp_valcheck.py/.log, h22_copp_valcheck2.py/.log, h22_copp_tangent.py].**
- *Family.* V|s⟩_P|o⟩_{AO} = (U_o|s⟩)_{AI}|o⟩_F, with one unitary U_o on the three agent input qubits for each output triple o. Agents measure in the computational basis, and frames are absorbed into U_o, so p(i|o) = |⟨i|U_oψ⟩|². PF phase boards (U_o = E·D_o) and forward PF lifts are of this form.
- *Criterion.* Tracing F gives W = Σ_o |o⟩⟨o|_{AO} ⊗ |v_o⟩⟨v_o|, so the process is valid iff Σ_o U_o†(⊗_k M^k_{o_k})U_o = 1 for every choice of binary POVMs (M^k_0, M^k_1). Expanding in M^k_1 gives an equivalent form. For every nonempty set S of parties, every fixed c ∈ {0,1}^{−S} and every operator X_S on S: Σ_{o_S} (−1)^{|o_S|} U†_{(o_S,c)}(X_S ⊗ 1)U_{(o_S,c)} = 0. The single-party conditions say that U_{o⊕e_k}U_o† acts only on the other two parties' qubits. In words, an agent's output cannot reach its own input.
- *Cross-check [NUMERICAL].* The criterion's penalty vanishes exactly where the Araújo et al. projector penalty (H.23.1) vanishes, with a ratio of about 9 between the two. Tested points: a PF phase board at the orbit-30 optimum (9.6·10⁻²⁹ vs 1.6·10⁻²⁹, reproducing λ = −0.0089649883); random unitaries (about 1500 vs 117); separable phases not routed through a PF (413 vs 41, 99 vs 11, 500 vs 50); valid points from penalty projection (10⁻²¹–10⁻¹⁹ vs 10⁻²²–10⁻²⁰).
- *Tangent probe [NUMERICAL].* We estimated the dimension of the valid set from the rank of the penalty Hessian. At the Lugano phase boards (rules 4 and 10) it is 80, at w′ = 13 it is 88, and at a forward lift on rule 4 it is 80. The value 80 equals 64 (a common right unitary, absorbed by ψ) + 10 (left local unitaries) + 6 (the phase-board phase steps). So at these points the valid set has no deformations beyond the obvious ones and the phase-board family. At the trivial point U_o = 1 the Hessian vanishes identically because the penalty is quartic there, and the value at the forward rule-13 point was not reliable. Both are excluded.

**H.29.2 The time-reversed PF lifts reduce to their extreme points: phase boards, plus rank 2 for four-block parties [Lemma PROVED; extreme-point rank bound CITED (Li–Tam 1994); n_k = 2 case PROVED elementarily; rank-2 search NUMERICAL; h22_rank2_search.py, h22_rank2_*.log].**
- **Lemma H.29.2a.** In the time-reversed PF lift of H.27.2 on a refined PF w′, the statistics depend on the node unitaries and the states φ_k only through the correlation matrices K_k(o)[x, x′] = ⟨U_{k,x}φ_k(o), U_{k,x′}φ_k(o)⟩. These are n_k × n_k positive semidefinite matrices with unit diagonal, where n_k is the number of blocks of party k and o ∈ {0,1}. Any pair (K_k(0), K_k(1)) is realised with node dimension D = 2n_k: put the two Gram realisations in orthogonal halves. For fixed source, frames and other K's, the facet value is affine in each K_k(o) separately. Its minimum over the family is therefore attained when every K_k(o) is an extreme point of the complex elliptope. Extreme points of rank r satisfy r² ≤ n (Li and Tam, SIAM J. Matrix Anal. Appl. 15 (1994) 903, CITED). For n_k ≤ 3, every extreme point has rank 1, K = vv† with |v_x| = 1, and this is exactly a PF phase board. For n_k = 4 the rank is at most 2. For n_k = 2 the step is elementary: K is fixed by one complex number c with |c| ≤ 1, and an affine function on the disk is minimised on the circle |c| = 1.
- *Consequence.* On the 14 refined PFs whose parties all have n_k ≤ 3 (all eight Lugano-type rules and six single-block rules), the infimum of any facet over the H.27.2 family equals its infimum over PF phase boards. This explains why the general search of H.28.2 missed orbits 30 and 31 while the phase-board search hit them: the general search optimised over the interior of a set whose minimum lies on its extreme boundary. On those 14 rules, H.28.2's phase-board runs (500 starts per pair) are thus runs over the extreme points of the whole H.27.2 family. They remain a numerical search, not a proof.
- *Rank-2 search on the six rules with a four-block party (0, 3, 8, 12, 18, 19).* Parties with n_k = 4 use n_k unit vectors in C² (rank ≤ 2). Parties with n_k ≤ 3 use phases. This contains all extreme points. Allowing C² for every party also contains them, but the optimiser then drifts into the interior (0/20 hits on orbit 30). Validation on rule 4, where the parametrisation reduces to phase boards: orbit 30 at −8.9650·10⁻³ in 6 of 40 starts and orbit 26 at −2.4287·10⁻⁵ in 1 of 40 (h22_rank2_val_w4.log). Results, 500 starts per rule over the seven orbits: on every rule and every one of the seven orbits the best value is 0 to about 10⁻¹⁵ with no start below −10⁻⁹, or 1/3 where the orbit cannot be reached (rules 0, 3: orbit 66; rules 8, 18: orbits 69, 81; rules 12, 19: orbits 54, 57, 75). Together with H.28.2, every extreme point type of the H.27.2 family on all 20 refined PFs has now been searched, with no crossing.

**H.29.3 All valid three-qubit phase processes: 34 components, including a new family [two-party factorisation PROVED; branch enumeration exact (sympy row spaces, Smith normal form); three-party condition checked NUMERICALLY at random points; h22_phaseproc.py, h22_pp_components.py/.json/.log].**
- *Setting.* Take diagonal phase processes D_o = diag(e^{iφ(o,z)}), read in SU(2) frames, and apply the criterion of H.29.1. The single-party conditions forbid any o_k z_k interaction. Up to phases that depend only on o (irrelevant) or only on z (absorbed into ψ), φ is therefore a real combination of 12 mixed monomials: o_k z_j (6), o_k z_j z_l (3) and o_j o_k z_l (3), with {j, k, l} = {0, 1, 2}. PF phase boards use only the first 9. The three monomials o_j o_k z_l, in which two outputs jointly steer a phase on the third party's input, are new.
- *Two-party condition.* Fix a pair {j, k} with third party l, and fix (z_l, o_l). The condition factorises as (1 − e^{iR_j})(1 − e^{iR_k}) = 0, with R_j = a_{j,k} + c_j z_l + g_k o_l and R_k = a_{k,j} + c_k z_l + g_j o_l. So in each of the 12 (pair, z_l, o_l) cases either j does not influence k or k does not influence j. This is the one-way-influence structure of process functions, now at the level of phases.
- *Enumeration.* The 2¹² branch choices give 1219 distinct lattices of linear conditions. 1217 satisfy the three-party condition at all random test points. 2 fail it somewhere, and the valid subsets of those 2 were not enumerated. The 1217 branches have **34 maximal valid components**, all torsion-free (Smith invariants 1), so each is a subtorus:
  - 8 three-dimensional components that use the c-monomials. These are the Lugano-type PF phase boards (for example rule 4 ↔ nonzero coordinates {2, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8});
  - 8 three-dimensional components that use the g-monomials o_j o_k z_l instead of c. These are **new**: they are not PF phase boards;
  - 18 five-dimensional components that mix the three types.
  Penalty projection from random starts lands on valid points in 200 of 200 starts, and only 4% of those points have all g = 0 (h22_phaseproc.py scan). So the g-directions are generic, not exceptional.
- *Caveats.* The three-party condition was checked at random points, not symbolically. The two failing branches might still contain valid subsets outside the 34 components. *Update (Round 23, H.30.3): both caveats are closed. The three-party condition is now checked exactly by a character-class argument, and the two failing branches (the cyclic patterns) are valid only on six subtori, each inside one of the 34 components, so the classification is PROVED.* Whether the new g-components are equivalent to PF phase boards under a symmetry outside the 384-element group (for example a change of frame that exchanges the roles of inputs and outputs) was not analysed [OPEN].

**H.29.4 Searches on the seven orbits: nothing crosses; the exact phase-process search passes validation, penalty searches do not [NUMERICAL, negative; h22_pp_comp_search.py, h22_ppc_*.log, h22_copp_search.py, h22_phaseproc.py, h22_pp_o*.log].**
- *Exact per-component search (passes validation).* Inside each of the 34 components validity is exact (φ = N y), so no penalty is needed. We minimise the Rayleigh quotient over (y, frames, ψ) by L-BFGS and report the exact minimum over ψ (the smallest eigenvalue), re-checking validity at the best point (penalty ≤ 5·10⁻²⁶ everywhere).
  - Validation, orbit 30: −8.964988·10⁻³, the H.28.1 value. Across the seed-1 (10 starts per component) and seed-2 (40 starts) runs it is reached on all 8 Lugano components and on no other component. Hit rates are 1–4 per 40 starts.
  - Validation, orbit 26: −2.428720·10⁻⁵, the H.28.1 value, on Lugano component 9 (1 of 40 starts).
  - Neither the 8 g-components nor the 18 five-dimensional components cross orbit 26 or 30 at these sample sizes.
  - The seven orbits, 40 starts per component (seed 2): the best value on each is −1.8·10⁻¹⁵ to −8·10⁻¹⁶, i.e. 0, with no start below −10⁻⁹. A second run with 150 starts per component (seed 3; 5,100 starts per orbit) gives best values between −2.0·10⁻¹⁵ and −1.0·10⁻¹⁵, again with no start below −10⁻⁹ (h22_ppc_o*_s3.log).
- *Penalty searches (fail validation).* The COPP search (U_o = exp(iH_o) with a staged validity penalty and a final projection; h22_copp_search.py) reaches only −10⁻¹¹ on orbit 30 in 3 random starts. Started from random Lugano phase boards (pbL mode) it ends at positive values. The penalty phase-process search over the 12-torus (100 starts per orbit; h22_pp_o*.log) finds 0 of 100 starts below −10⁻⁸ on orbit 30 (best −4.1·10⁻¹³). On the seven open orbits it gives best values between −2.8·10⁻¹² and 0 and no start below −10⁻⁸. Both searches converge to valid but nearly trivial points. Their negatives on the seven orbits are therefore close to uninformative, which is why the exact search above was built.
- *Exact certificates.* There is no crossing, so neither h21_trev_cert.py nor h21_cert_indep.py was run this round. A crossing on a g-component would need h21_cert_indep.py (explicit unitary), since checker A's Gram formula covers only PF phase boards and lifts.
- *Not done.* A certified SDP/SOS bound that uses purity. A general pure-process search beyond COPP that passes validation. A perturbative search around the −1/6 point of Valid (H.17.5). Two-component mixtures and instruments on the new g-components.

**H.29.5 Status after Round 22 [summary].** Still **15 of the 22 genuine orbits crossed** (exactly certified) and **7 open** (28, 54, 57, 66, 69, 75, 81; 960 facets). The following now hold:
- the seven hold on forward PF lifts (Theorem H.27.1, PROVED);
- on the time-reversed lifts of H.27.2, the problem reduces to extreme points (Lemma H.29.2a, PROVED with a CITED rank bound), and the extreme-point searches find no crossing (NUMERICAL);
- the exact search over all 34 components of valid three-qubit phase processes finds no crossing, while it does reproduce both crossings of orbits 26 and 30 (NUMERICAL).
Whether the seven hold on Purif is **OPEN**. *Reading [INTERPRETIVE].* Every crossing found so far, in any round, lies on a Lugano-type component. On the families examined, the seven open orbits behave like constraints of process functions. A purity-based proof would have to explain why the Lugano components cross 26, 30 and 31 but not these seven.
- *Correction recorded this round.* The H.28.2 instrument paragraph first stated the meaning of the wiring mask R backwards (R is the set of time-reversed parties). It was corrected in commit 1e95e03, and a note in the text records the correction.

**H.30 Round 23: a span lemma proves the seven open orbits on every non-Lugano family; the phase-process classification is now exact; the seven stay open on Purif (Oct 26 2026).** Round 23 tried to prove the seven open orbits 28, 54, 57, 66, 69, 75 and 81 (960 facets) using purity. A proof for all of Purif was not reached. **What is proved is a statement on an explicit subclass.** On every valid three-qubit phase process outside the eight Lugano-type components, and on every time-reversed PF lift (H.27.2) on the twelve single-block refined PFs, every facet of the seven orbits holds. So do the facets of orbits 26, 30 and 31. Each family's statistics lie in an exactly computed linear space S (Lemma H.30.1). On Valid ∩ S, two independently written programs certify every facet with exact LP duality (Theorem H.30.2). The round also completes the enumeration of H.29.3 exactly (H.30.3). A search started at the −1/6 points of Valid failed validation (H.30.4). The counts are unchanged: **15 of 22 genuine orbits crossed, 7 open.** Code and logs are in code/stochastic/cyclic/embed/h23_*.

**H.30.1 The statistics span of a family [Lemma PROVED by derivation; integer generators, exact ranks; sampled spans agree NUMERICALLY; h23_span.py, h23_span_lp.py, h23_span_lp_comp.log, h23_span_lp_rule.log, h23_affhull.py/.log/.json].**
- *Expansion.* For a COPP (H.29.1) read in SU(2) frames e_k with source ψ on the three input qubits,
  p(i|o) = Σ_{z,z′} conj(ψ_z) ψ_{z′} χ_{zz′}(o) Π_k conj(e_k[i_k, z_k]) e_k[i_k, z′_k].
  This covers two families. For a phase process, χ_{zz′}(o) = e^{i(φ(o,z′) − φ(o,z))}. For the time-reversed PF lift on a refined PF w′ (H.27.2, Lemma H.29.2a), χ_{zz′}(o) = Π_k K_k(o_k)[w′_k(z_{−k}), w′_k(z′_{−k})].
- *Frame factor.* As a function of i_k, the k-th frame factor lies in span{1, (−1)^{i_k}} if z_k = z′_k. If z_k ≠ z′_k it lies in span{(−1)^{i_k}}, because two columns of a unitary are orthogonal, so the constant part vanishes.
- *Output factor, phase processes.* On a component {x : Mx ∈ 2πZ^r}, φ = x · m(o, z), where m is the vector of the 12 mixed monomials. All 34 components are torsion-free (H.30.3), so the valid set is ker M + 2πZ^12. Then χ_{zz′}(o) = e^{i x·(m(o,z′) − m(o,z))} takes the same value on any two outputs o, o′ whose integer difference vectors differ by an element of rowspace(M). So χ lies in the span of the indicators of these classes of o.
- *Output factor, time-reversed lifts.* The k-th factor is 1 if z and z′ lie in the same block of party k. Otherwise it is an arbitrary function of o_k.
- **Lemma H.30.1.** For each family, all statistics, for every source (pure or mixed), every frame and every point of the family, lie in the linear space S spanned by the integer vectors (o-pattern) ⊗ (i-pattern) over all pairs (z, z′). If a facet's minimum over Valid ∩ S is ≥ 0, the facet holds on the whole family and on its convex hull.
- *Dimensions [exact; sampled check NUMERICAL].*
  - The 26 non-Lugano phase components have dim S = 20, 22 or 24.
  - The 12 single-block rules have dim S = 22 or 24.
  - The 8 Lugano components (7–10, 22–25) and the 8 Lugano-type rules (4, 5, 6, 7, 10, 11, 14, 15) have dim S = 38, which is the full affine hull of Valid (Valid has affine dimension 37, the same as the PFs).
  - For every family, 300 random members span exactly S (residual ≤ 6·10⁻¹⁵), so S is not larger than it needs to be.
  - The 38 non-Lugano families give 23 distinct spaces. Several rules and components share a space: rule 0 = comp 0, rules 1, 2 = comps 1, 2, rule 3 = comp 3, rule 8 = comp 27, rules 9, 17 = comps 28, 29, rule 12 = comp 6, rules 13, 16 = comps 11, 26, rule 18 = comp 30, rule 19 = comp 33. The remaining spaces belong to comps 4, 5, 12–21, 31, 32.
- *Why the method stops at Lugano.* On the Lugano-type families S is the whole space, so Valid ∩ S = Valid. There the LP minimum is the −1/6 of H.17.5 for all ten orbits, and the method gives no information. These are exactly the families on which orbits 26, 30 and 31 are crossed (H.28.1, H.29.4).

**H.30.2 Exact certificates on every non-Lugano family [Theorem PROVED in exact rational arithmetic by two independent checkers; all-facet extension NUMERICAL; h23_span_cert.py, h23_span_cert_{1..5}.log, h23_span_certB.py, h23_span_certB_{1,2,3}.log, h23_span_certB_lugano_control.log, h23_span_allfac.py, h23_span_allfac_rules.log, h23_span_allfac_comps.log].**
- **Theorem H.30.2.** Let p be the statistics of any of the following, with any SU(2) frames and any source state, or a convex mixture of such statistics:
  - (a) a valid three-qubit phase process (H.29.3) whose phase vector lies in one of the 26 non-Lugano components (0–6, 11–21, 26–33);
  - (b) a time-reversed PF lift (H.27.2: any node dimension, node unitaries and states, so any correlation matrices K_k(o)) on one of the 12 single-block refined PFs (0, 1, 2, 3, 8, 9, 12, 13, 16, 17, 18, 19).

  Then p satisfies **every one of the 1,536 facets** of orbits 26, 28, 30, 31, 54, 57, 66, 69, 75 and 81. This includes all 960 facets of the seven open orbits. In integer facet units the certified minimum on Valid ∩ S is 0 or 1 for every facet. 1 means the facet cannot even be made tight in that family; it corresponds to the relative value 1/3 seen in H.28.2 and H.29.2.
- *Proof.* By Lemma H.30.1, p ∈ Valid ∩ S. For each of the 23 distinct spaces S and each of the 1,536 facets, the checkers certify min_{Valid ∩ S} (g·p + g₀) ≥ 0, giving 23 × 1,536 = 35,328 certificates per checker. Here g is the facet's affine functional, which is fixed on Valid because Valid and the PFs have the same affine hull.
- *Checker A (h23_span_cert.py).*
  - It writes p = Bc with an integer basis B of S.
  - It takes the dual support from a HiGHS simplex solve and solves exactly for ν ≥ 0 and λ with Bᵀ(g − ν − Aeqᵀλ) = 0 and Σλ + g₀ = t, where t ∈ {0, 1}.
  - It re-verifies the identity in Fraction arithmetic against every basis column.
- *Checker B (h23_span_certB.py), written separately.* It differs from checker A at every step:
  - S is built from different generators: class indicators from an integer basis of ker M for components, and block-output and equal-bit indicators for rules. B then computes S^⊥ = N exactly with DomainMatrix over ℚ.
  - The functional g is solved exactly from 38 affinely independent PFs and checked on all 744 PFs. A instead rationalises a least-squares fit.
  - The LP is posed in p-space, min g·p subject to Aeq p = 1, Nᵀp = 0 and p ≥ 0, and solved by the interior-point method.
  - The certificate g = Aeqᵀλ + Nᵀμ + ν with ν ≥ 0 and Σλ + g₀ = t ≥ 0 is checked coordinate by coordinate in exact rationals. Then g·p + g₀ = t + ν·p ≥ t on Valid ∩ S.
- *Results.* Both checkers certify all 1,536 facets on all 23 spaces with 0 failures (logs above). The two checkers return the same bound set {0, 1} on every space.
- *Negative control.* Run on Lugano rule 4 and Lugano component 7 for orbits 26 and 30, checker B certifies nothing. All 384 facets come back "LP<0" at −1/2 in integer units, which is −1/6 relative. This is what H.30.1 predicts.
- *Beyond the ten orbits [NUMERICAL].* A float LP over all 29,344 facets on all 12 single-block rules and on comps 4, 5, 12–21, 31 and 32 finds no facet with minimum below −10⁻⁹; the most negative value is −1.3·10⁻¹³. These cover every one of the 23 spaces. So numerically Valid ∩ S ⊆ conv(PF): on every non-Lugano family the statistics are PF blends.
- *What this covers and what it does not.*
  - Combined with H.30.3, part (a) covers every valid three-qubit phase process except those lying only in Lugano components.
  - Part (b) covers all of H.27.2 on the single-block rules, not just the extreme points of Lemma H.29.2a.
  - It does **not** cover:
    - the 8 Lugano-type rules and components, which is where every known crossing lives;
    - time-reversed lifts on Lugano-type rules;
    - COPPs that are not phase processes;
    - pure processes whose future does not record the outputs;
    - agents with general instruments.
  - On all of these the seven orbits remain **OPEN**.
- *Novelty [cautious].* The span argument is elementary: unitarity kills the constant part of an off-diagonal frame factor, and characters of a torus are linearly independent. We have not seen it used for causal-inequality or PF-hull questions, but we have not searched the literature thoroughly.
- *SDP/SOS on the Lugano families [not done, OPEN].* On the Lugano families, purity enters only through the positive semidefinite correlation matrices K_k(o) (Lemma H.29.2a) and the rank-one source. A moment relaxation in these variables would be the natural next step. It was not set up this round.

**H.30.3 The phase-process classification of H.29.3 is now exact [PROVED, exact integer and rational arithmetic; h23_pp_enum2.py/.log/.json, h23_pp_enum2_rows.py, h23_pp_exact.py/.log].**
- *Torsion.* The Smith normal form of all 1219 branch lattices has every invariant equal to 1. So every branch is a subtorus ker M + 2πZ^12, and no torsion cosets exist.
- *Three-party condition, exactly.* On a branch, each COPP condition (H.29.1) is a signed sum of characters e^{i x·v} of the torus ker M. Two characters coincide iff the difference of their integer vectors is orthogonal to ker M, and distinct characters are linearly independent. So a condition holds on the whole branch iff, in every class of equal characters, the signed count Σ(−1)^{|o_S|} vanishes. h23_pp_exact.py checks this for every party set S, every c, z and z′, using an integer kernel basis.
  - 1217 of the 1219 branches are exactly valid on their whole torus.
  - All 34 components of H.29.3 are exactly valid.
- *The two failing branches.* These are the two cyclic one-way patterns. One has free coordinates {1, 2, 5}, the other {0, 3, 4}; in each, every party's phase is steered by exactly one other party around a cycle. The three-party condition factorises as (1 − e^{iαΔ₁})(1 − e^{iβΔ₂})(1 − e^{iγΔ₃}). So a point is valid iff one of the three coefficients vanishes mod 2π. Each of the six resulting two-dimensional subtori is exactly valid and contained in a component: 0, 30 and 6 for the first branch, 3, 27 and 33 for the second. Earlier, 300 + 300 penalty-minimisation starts on these branches all landed inside the 34 components (h23_pp_enum2.log), which agrees with this.
- **Result.** The valid three-qubit phase processes are exactly the union of the 34 subtori of H.29.3. This upgrades the classification from "three-party condition NUMERICAL" to PROVED. It rests on the one- and two-party reductions of H.29.3, which were already PROVED. One question from H.29.3 remains OPEN: whether the eight g-components are equivalent to PF phase boards under a symmetry outside the 384-element group. By H.30.2 they cannot cross any of the ten orbits.

**H.30.4 Search started at the −1/6 points of Valid: fails validation [NUMERICAL, negative and uninformative; h23_near16.py, h23_near16_val_o26.log, h23_near16_val_o30.log, h23_near16_o*.log].**
- *Method.* For the representative facet, take a vertex p* of Valid with relative value −1/6.
