Build a COPP with exactly these statistics: ψ = |0⟩, U_o a Householder map with U_o|0⟩ = √p*(·|o) and random phases. Such a start is invalid. Then minimise the facet value plus μ·(validity penalty) with μ increased from 10⁻² to 10⁴, as in h22_copp_search.py, and report the exact minimum over ψ at the end.
- *Validation [fails].* Each orbit got 10 starts. On orbit 30 every start reached a valid point (penalty ≤ 2·10⁻¹²), with values between −2·10⁻⁹ and +9·10⁻⁹. On orbit 26 the penalties were ≤ 3·10⁻¹¹ and the values lay between −2·10⁻¹⁰ and +4·10⁻⁶. **Neither re-finds the known crossings** (−8.96·10⁻³ and −2.43·10⁻⁵). The penalty drives the process to nearly trivial valid points, as in H.29.4.
- *The seven orbits.* Run anyway with 3 starts per orbit (h23_near16_o{28,54,57,66,69,75,81}.log). Every start ends at a valid point (penalty ≤ 2·10⁻¹³), with best values between −3.7·10⁻¹¹ (orbit 28) and +4·10⁻⁸. No start falls below −10⁻⁷.
- *Conclusion.* Because validation failed, these runs say nothing about the seven orbits. A perturbative search that keeps validity exactly near the −1/6 points remains **not done**.

**H.30.5 Status after Round 23 [summary].** Still **15 of 22 genuine orbits crossed** (exactly certified) and **7 open** (28, 54, 57, 66, 69, 75, 81; 960 facets). The following hold:
- the seven, and in fact all ten H.27 orbits, hold on forward PF lifts (Theorem H.27.1, PROVED);
- they hold on every non-Lugano valid phase process and on all time-reversed PF lifts on single-block rules (Theorem H.30.2, PROVED, two exact checkers);
- the phase-process classification is exact (H.30.3, PROVED).

So for the families studied, the open question has been reduced exactly to **the Lugano-type families**: Lugano phase components and time-reversed lifts on the eight Lugano-type rules, whose extreme points are PF phase boards (Lemma H.29.2a). It also remains open for every pure process outside these families. *Reading [INTERPRETIVE].* Everything that crosses any facet in this paper sits on a Lugano-type family, and everything off those families is (numerically) a PF blend. A proof for the seven would need to use the PSD structure of the Lugano correlation matrices, since linear span arguments see all of Valid there.

**H.31 Round 24: a purity-based SDP proves orbits 54 and 66 on every Lugano-type family, so both now hold on every family studied; the other five stay open there, and all seven stay open on Purif (Oct 26 2026).** Round 24 attacked the seven open orbits 28, 54, 57, 66, 69, 75 and 81 on the Lugano-type families, where the linear span method of H.30.1 cannot work. **What is proved.** All 16 Lugano-type cases reduce exactly to one: PF phase boards on rule 4, at one representative facet per orbit (H.31.1). On that family a positive-semidefinite relaxation that uses purity, the *class-lift relaxation*, certifies every facet of **orbits 54 and 66**, with exact rational dual certificates checked by two independent programs (Theorem H.31.3). With Theorems H.27.1 and H.30.2, orbits 54 and 66 therefore hold on every family of pure processes studied in this paper (Corollary H.31.3a). For orbit 57 the relaxation's bound is 0 to within 2·10⁻¹⁰, but the exact rounding failed, so 57 is NUMERICAL only. For orbit 75 the reduced relaxation gives ≈ −3·10⁻⁷ (inaccurate), which is also near 0 but is not proved. For orbits 28, 69 and 81 the relaxation is negative and proves nothing. A validated exact-parametrisation search over the whole reduced family finds no crossing of any of the seven (H.31.5). **The controls are what give these negatives weight.** The relaxation fails exactly where it must, on the crossed orbits 26, 30 and 31, with bounds below the known crossings (H.31.2). The search re-finds all three known crossings, including orbit 26's rare one (H.31.5). **The tally on Purif is unchanged: 15 of 22 genuine orbits crossed, 7 open.** Code and logs are in code/stochastic/cyclic/embed/h24_*.

**H.31.1 All Lugano-type cases are one case [PROVED, elementary; exact enumeration in h24_lugsym.py/.log].**
- *Setting.* Take a time-reversed PF lift (H.27.2) on a Lugano-type rule w′, that is, a rule whose block counts are (2,2,2). By Lemma H.29.2a (the case n_k = 2, which is elementary), the minimum of any facet over this family is attained at PF phase boards. Absorb every phase that depends only on z into the source. Then a board is v_o = D_o ψ, with D_o = diag_z(Π_k u_k^{o_k x_k(z)}), |u_k| = 1 and x_k(z) = w′_k(z_{−k}) ∈ {0,1}. Its statistics are p(i|o) = |⟨f_i|v_o⟩|², with product frame vectors f_i = ⊗_k e_k(i_k). After this, nine real parameters remain: three phases and three Bloch directions. The source is eliminated exactly, because min_ψ ψ†Mψ = λ_min(M).
- **(a) Rules.** Flipping the bit z_l in the source register maps the phase boards of rule 4 onto the phase boards of another rule. The extra phases depend only on z, so they go into ψ, and the flip goes into the frames. The eight images of rule 4 under the z-flips are rules 4, 6, 10, 14, 5, 7, 11, 15, which are all eight Lugano-type rules. So the eight rules have the same set of statistics.
- **(b) Components.** Each Lugano-type phase-process component equals, modulo phases that depend only on z or only on o, the phase boards of one Lugano-type rule: 7→4, 8→5, 9→10, 10→11, 22→6, 23→7, 24→14, 25→15. This is checked as an exact equality of integer row spaces in both directions. Phases that depend only on o are a global phase of v_o, and they do not change p.
- **(c) Facets.** The set F of rule-4 statistics is invariant under the 384-element group: flipping i_k changes the frame; flipping o_k maps u_k to ū_k and adds a phase that depends only on z, which goes into ψ; permuting the parties gives a Lugano-type rule, which reduces to rule 4 by (a). Hence every facet in an orbit has the same minimum over F as the orbit's representative.
- *Consequence.* For each orbit, a single facet on a single family decides all 16 cases (eight components, eight rules with their time-reversed lifts), for every frame and every pure or mixed source, and for convex mixtures. Goal 2 of the round (finer decompositions) was met in this form. Splitting the span by sub-branch does not help, because the reduced family's statistics span is already the full 38-dimensional space (H.30.1).
- *Numerical check of (a) [NUMERICAL; h24_rulecheck.sh/.log].* The exact search of H.31.5 was run directly on each of the other seven Lugano-type rules 5, 6, 7, 10, 11, 14 and 15. Every rule gives the same orbit-30 crossing, −8.964988·10⁻³, and the same minima ≈ −1.5·10⁻¹⁵ for orbits 54 and 66, as on rule 4.
- *Numerical check of (c) [NUMERICAL; h24_orbitcheck.py/.log].* All 96 facets of orbit 54 and all 96 of orbit 66 were minimised over F (100 starts each). The minima are −2.2·10⁻¹⁵ and −2.8·10⁻¹⁵, the same as at the representatives.

**H.31.2 Purity-based relaxations; validation [definitions; bounds NUMERICAL; h24_sdp1.py, h24_conic.py, h24_sdp2.py, h24_sdp2r.py, h24_sdp2_w4_*.log, h24_sdp2r.log].**
- *Level 1 (SDP1).* Variables Z(i,o) = ρ∘G_{io}, one PSD matrix per pair, with entries in the H.30.1 pattern span and Σ trace = 1. It is too weak: −2.7·10⁻² on orbit 28.
- *Class lift (SDP2).* Lift the pure board to X[(o,z),(o′,z′)] = ψ_z ψ̄_{z′} Π_k u_k^{e_k} with e_k = o_k x_k(z) − o′_k x_k(z′). The 64×64 matrix X is PSD. It is constant on the 360 *classes* (z, z′, e), because the phases enter only through e. For each i, Z(i) = diag(1⊗f̄_i) X diag(1⊗f_i) is PSD and constant on classes. The map i ↦ Z(i)_c lies in the frame span ipat(z,z′) of Lemma H.30.1, Σ_i Z(i)_c = X_c when z = z′, and Σ_z X_{(z,z,0)} = 1. These are all exact consequences of the board form, so SDP2 is a relaxation. Mixed sources are covered by convexity. The facet is linear in Z. The problem has nine PSD cones of size 128×128 (real form), 3,240 variables and 1,719 equations, and is solved with SCS (Clarabel runs out of memory).
- *Reduced relaxation R.* Drop the cone on X, so that X enters only linearly. R is weaker than SDP2 but much faster, and its dual is what gets rounded (H.31.3).
- *Validation [passes].* The relaxation must not certify the crossed orbits, and its bound must lie at or below the known crossing values. Results on rule 4 (relative units; −1/6 is the worst point of Valid):

  | orbit | known crossing | SDP2 | R |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | 26 | −2.4287·10⁻⁵ | −3.14·10⁻² | ≤ SDP2 ᵃ |
  | 30 | −8.9650·10⁻³ | −1.88·10⁻² | ≤ SDP2 ᵃ |
  | 31 | −8.9650·10⁻³ | −1.92·10⁻² | ≤ SDP2 ᵃ |

  Both relaxations certify none of the three, and both bounds lie below the crossings, as they must. ᵃ R is SDP2 with one cone removed, so its bound is at most the SDP2 bound. The R runs on 26, 30 and 31 in h24_sdp2r.log did not finish within their time bound (which was used up by orbit 75).
- *The seven (representative facets, rule 4).*

  | orbit | SDP2 | R | exact certificate |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | 28 | −2.30·10⁻² | ≤ SDP2 ᵃ | — |
  | 54 | +3·10⁻⁸ | −9·10⁻¹¹ | **yes** (H.31.3) |
  | 57 | −1.3·10⁻⁸ | −1.2·10⁻¹⁰ | rounding failed (H.31.4) |
  | 66 | +5·10⁻⁹ | +4·10⁻¹² | **yes** (H.31.3) |
  | 69 | −4.99·10⁻⁵ | ≤ SDP2 ᵃ | — |
  | 75 | not finished ᵇ | −2.8·10⁻⁷ ᵇ | rounding failed ᵇ (H.31.4) |
  | 81 | −2.92·10⁻² | ≤ SDP2 ᵃ | — |

  ᵇ The SDP2 run on 75 timed out. R on 75 stopped at SCS's iteration limit (2,401 s) with primal −2.85·10⁻⁷ and dual −1.71·10⁻⁷, so the value is approximate.

**H.31.3 Exact certificates for orbits 54 and 66 [Theorem PROVED in exact rational arithmetic by two independent checkers; h24_cert.py (builder and checker A), h24_certB.py (checker B), h24_cert_o{54,66}b.json/.log, h24_certB_o{54,66}.log].**
- **Theorem H.31.3.** Let p be the statistics of any of the following, with any SU(2) frames and any source state (pure or mixed), or a convex mixture of such statistics:
  (a) a PF phase board on any of the eight Lugano-type rules 4, 5, 6, 7, 10, 11, 14, 15;
  (b) a time-reversed PF lift (H.27.2) on any of these rules;
  (c) a valid three-qubit phase process in any of the Lugano-type components 7–10, 22–25 (H.29.3).
  Then p satisfies every facet of orbits 54 and 66 (96 facets each).
- *Proof.* By H.31.1, it suffices to take the representative facet (514 for orbit 54, 561 for orbit 66) on case (a) for rule 4. For each facet, g denotes a rational affine functional that equals the facet on all 744 PFs, and so on aff(PF) = aff(Valid) (H.30.2). Every board yields a point of R (H.31.2). The certificate consists of eight Hermitian matrices H_i ⪰ 0 (64×64) and a scalar μ = −g₀. For every class c = (z, z′, e), the vector i ↦ W[i,c] − conj(h_c(i)) − μ·[c = (z,z,0)] is orthogonal to ipat(z,z′). Here h_c(i) = Σ_{(a,b)∈c} H_i[a,b], and W[i,c] = Σ_o g[i,o]·#{(z,z′) : ((o,z),(o,z′)) ∈ c}. With these properties, g·p + g₀ = Σ_i ⟨H_i, Z(i)⟩ + μ + g₀ ≥ μ + g₀ = 0 at every point of R. ∎
- *Construction.* The SCS dual of R has a sharp rank gap: the H_i have rank 21 (orbit 54), or 21 and 20 (orbit 66), out of 64, and the eigenvalues jump from about 10⁻⁶ to about 10⁻¹⁴. In reduced row form, the kernels are real with entries in {0, ±1/4, ±1}. They were rounded to exact rationals (facial reduction). We then wrote H_i = P_i G_i P_iᵀ, with P_i an exact integer basis of the complement of the kernel (entries in {0, ±1, ±4}). This leaves 3,528 (orbit 54) or 3,364 (orbit 66) real unknowns in the G_i, and 1,206 linear equations, of rank 1,172 or 1,140. The numerical G_i were rounded to denominators ≤ 10¹⁴, and a pivot subset of unknowns was corrected by an exact flint solve. The largest correction was 3·10⁻¹¹ or 2·10⁻¹², against smallest eigenvalues of G_i of at least 2.7·10⁻⁴ or 6·10⁻⁷. All 1,206 equations then hold exactly. The bound is exactly 0, which is tight: the facets are attained on the family, for example at trivial phases.
- *Checker A* (in h24_cert.py) verifies all the equations in exact arithmetic. It checks that each G_i is positive definite by exact elimination on its real 2r×2r form.
- *Checker B* (h24_certB.py) is written separately. It recomputes the board exponents, the classes and the frame spans from the rule. It verifies exactly that the certificate's g reproduces the facet on all 744 PFs. It forms H_i = P_i G_i P_iᵀ exactly and checks every class condition exactly (0 violations out of 360 classes for each orbit). It checks positive definiteness by strict sign alternation of the exact characteristic polynomial of each real form, a method different from A's. As a sanity check, on 20 random boards it compares the identity g·p + g₀ = Σ⟨H_i, Z(i)⟩ + μ + g₀ numerically and finds agreement to 9·10⁻¹⁶.
- **Corollary H.31.3a.** Orbits 54 and 66 hold on every family of pure processes studied in this paper: forward PF lifts in any dimension (Theorem H.27.1); every valid three-qubit phase process (Theorem H.30.2 off the Lugano components, together with H.31.3(c) and the exact classification H.30.3); and time-reversed PF lifts on all 20 refined PFs (H.30.2 for the 12 single-block rules, H.31.3(b) for the 8 Lugano-type rules). Whether they hold on all of Purif remains OPEN.
- *Scope caveats.* The theorem covers binary parties with qubit wires in the families listed. It says nothing about higher-dimensional node wires on Lugano-type rules beyond the H.27.2 family, or about pure processes outside the families studied.

**H.31.4 Orbits 57 and 75, and the three negative orbits [NUMERICAL; h24_cert_o57*.log, h24_cert_o75b.log, h24_primal69.py/.log, h24_sdp2r.log].**
- *Orbit 57.* Both relaxations give 0 to within solver tolerance: SDP2 −1.3·10⁻⁸, and R −1.2·10⁻¹⁰ at facet 521 and −1.7·10⁻¹⁰ at facet 588. The exact rounding of H.31.3 failed, however. The rank gaps of the dual are weak (ratios 2·10³ to 5·10⁶), the ranks differ between the H_i (16 or 17), and the kernels do not round to any small-denominator grid (error 10⁻² at denominator 48). The optimal dual face may be irrational, or SCS may not have converged on it. So orbit 57 on the Lugano families is **NUMERICAL** (relaxation bound ≥ −2·10⁻¹⁰), not PROVED. Two further attempts also failed, both logged. SCS at ε = 10⁻⁹ on facet 529 did not finish within its 2400 s bound (`h24_cert_o57f529.log`). An interior-point (Clarabel) solve of R would give a maximal-rank dual, which is the right input for facial reduction, but it does not fit in the box's 16 GB of memory (`h24_cert_o57clar.log`).
- *Orbit 75.* The reduced relaxation R gives ≈ −2.8·10⁻⁷, an inaccurate value at SCS's iteration limit. So 75 may behave like 57, with a bound of 0 up to solver accuracy. An exact-rounding attempt as in H.31.3 (h24_cert.py at ε = 10⁻⁹, `h24_cert_o75b.log`) had not produced a certificate when this was written, so 75 is NUMERICAL.
- *Orbits 28, 69, 81.* The relaxations are clearly negative (table in H.31.2), so they prove nothing. Since the exact search finds no crossing (H.31.5), the negative values are presumably relaxation gaps. For orbit 69, the gap is small (−5·10⁻⁵). We checked whether this gap is a crossing. The SDP2 primal optimum for 69 (−4.985884·10⁻⁵, `h24_primal69.log`) has a high-rank moment matrix X: its leading eigenvalues are 2.52, 1.59, 1.30, 1.28, 0.91 and 0.15. A rank-one X would be needed for it to come from a phase board. So the gap is relaxation slack and not a crossing. This agrees with the 24,000-start search in H.31.5. (NUMERICAL.)

**H.31.5 Exact-parametrisation search over the reduced family [NUMERICAL, validated; h24_lugsearch.py, h24_lugsearch_val.log, h24_lugsearch_open.log, h24_lugsearch_o69big.log].**
- *Method.* The search uses the nine-parameter model of H.31.1, value = λ_min(Σ_{i,o} T[i,o] D_o† |f_i⟩⟨f_i| D_o) + c. Every parameter value is a valid process, so no penalty is needed. Batched Adam runs from uniform random starts (1,500 steps), and then the best 50 are polished with L-BFGS. By H.31.1 this one search covers all 16 Lugano-type cases.
- *Validation [passes].* The search must re-find the known crossings, and it does. Orbit 30: −8.964988·10⁻³ (70 of 2,000 starts end below −10⁻⁷). Orbit 31: −8.964988·10⁻³ (135 of 4,000). Orbit 26: −2.428720·10⁻⁵ (2 of 4,000). These are the H.28.1 values to all printed digits. Orbit 26 is rare, at about one start in 2,000.
- *The seven.* With 6,000 starts each, the polished minima are: 28: −1.2·10⁻¹⁵; 54: −1.3·10⁻¹⁵; 57: −1.4·10⁻¹⁵; 66: −1.9·10⁻¹⁵; 69: −3.9·10⁻¹⁶; 75: −1.0·10⁻¹⁵; 81: −5.8·10⁻¹⁶. No start ends below −10⁻⁷. Orbit 69 was rerun with 24,000 starts: none ends below −10⁻⁷, and the polished minimum is −3.6·10⁻¹⁶ (`h24_lugsearch_o69big.log`). **No crossing.**
- *Reading.* The search is validated, so its negatives carry weight. Even so, a crossing rarer than orbit 26's (about 1 in 2,000 starts) could be missed. The search is evidence, not proof.

**H.31.6 Status after Round 24 [summary].** On Purif the count is unchanged: **15 of 22 genuine orbits crossed** (exactly certified) and **7 open** (28, 54, 57, 66, 69, 75, 81). For the families studied:
- **orbits 54 and 66 hold on every family studied** (Corollary H.31.3a, PROVED with two exact checkers);
- orbit 57 holds on the Lugano families to within 2·10⁻¹⁰ in the relaxation (NUMERICAL), so it is open there as a proof;
- orbit 75 has a reduced-relaxation bound ≈ −3·10⁻⁷ (inaccurate, NUMERICAL);
- orbits 28, 69 and 81 are open on the Lugano families. The relaxation is clearly negative there, and the validated search finds no crossing (NUMERICAL);
- everything holds off the Lugano families (H.30.2) and on forward lifts (H.27.1).

So the open region for the families studied is now **five orbits (28, 57, 69, 75, 81) on PF phase boards on rule 4**, a single nine-parameter family. *Reading [INTERPRETIVE].* For 54 and 66, purity plus the class structure of the phases is already enough. For the other five, a stronger relaxation (for example a party hierarchy with partial frames, or a higher moment level in the u_k) would be the next step. Orbit 69's small gap suggests it is the closest of them.

**H.32 Round 25: zero-set kernels give orbit 66 a robust certificate; facial reduction stalls on 57 and 75; a validated grid-seeded search finds no crossing of the five (Oct 27 2026).** Round 25 worked on the five orbits that are still open on the reduced family of H.31.1 (28, 57, 69, 75, 81), and tried to make the exact certificates more robust. **What is proved.** Orbit 66 gets a second exact certificate, independent of H.31.3, on the minimal faces forced by the zero set (Theorem H.32.2). Its margin, 3.6·10⁻³, is several thousand times the smallest eigenvalue (≈ 6·10⁻⁷) of the Round-24 certificate's G_i (h24_cert_o66b.log), and both checkers accept it. This adds no new orbit: 66 was already PROVED (Theorem H.31.3). **What is not proved.** Orbit 57: facial reduction from the zero-set face stalls: the margins satisfy |t| ≤ 1.1·10⁻⁷ on all 190 of the orbit's 192 facets for which a zero-set face was obtained, and never become clearly positive (H.32.3). Orbit 75: the first facial-reduction step has a negative margin (H.32.4). So 57 and 75 stay NUMERICAL. **Search.** A deterministic grid-seeded multistart (H.32.5) is validated on 26, 30 and 31, including orbit 26's rare crossing. It finds no crossing of 28, 57, 69, 75 or 81. As in H.31, the control is what gives this negative weight: the same search re-finds all three known crossings. **The tally on Purif is unchanged: 15 of 22 genuine orbits crossed, 7 open; on the families studied, five open (28, 57, 69, 75, 81).** Code and logs are in code/stochastic/cyclic/embed/h25_*.

**H.32.1 Zero-set kernels [PROVED, elementary; numerical inputs].** Take any certificate of the form in H.31.3, g·p + g₀ = Σ_i ⟨H_i, Z(i)⟩ + μ + g₀ with H_i ⪰ 0 and μ + g₀ = 0. Let a board with value v_o = D_oψ attain facet value 0. Then Σ_i ⟨H_i, Z(i)⟩ = 0 at that board. Each Z(i) at a board is the rank-one matrix w_i w_i^†, where w_i[(o,z)] = conj f_i[z]·(D_oψ)_z (index 8o + z). So H_i w_i = 0 for every i. Hence **every exact certificate with bound 0 has span{w_i over the zero set} ⊆ ker H_i** (complementary slackness). This is a necessary condition, and it can be read from the boards alone, with no solver. It also explains why Round 24's rounding needed the kernels: they are forced. *Numerical input.* The zero set is sampled by batched Adam plus L-BFGS from random starts, keeping boards with value < 10⁻¹², and every eigenvector of M with eigenvalue < 10⁻¹⁰ (h25_zeroset.py). The rank of the span uses a relative threshold of 10⁻⁴, and its basis is rationalised and then checked (residual < 10⁻⁴; observed ≈ 10⁻¹⁵). The sampled span might be smaller than the true one. That would only make the restricted problem infeasible, never invalid. Any certificate found is checked exactly, so sampling errors cannot produce a false proof. *Check.* The proved Round-24 certificate for 66 satisfies ‖H_i w_i‖ ≤ 2·10⁻¹⁵ on the sampled zero set.

**H.32.2 A strictly feasible certificate for orbit 66 on the zero-set face [Theorem PROVED in exact rational arithmetic by two independent checkers; h25_zeroset.py, h25_cert.py (builder and checker A), h24_certB.py (checker B), h25_cert_o66zs.json, h25_cert_o66zs.log, h25_certB_o66zs.log].**
- *Method.* Restrict H_i = P_i G_i P_iᵀ, where the columns of P_i span the rational complement of the zero-set kernel of H.32.1. Then maximise t subject to the linear conditions of H.31.3 and G_i ⪰ tI. If t > 0 the face is strictly feasible. In that case we round G to a dyadic grid (2⁻²⁴), restore the linear conditions exactly (an exact rational solve on pivot columns, correction 6.3·10⁻⁷), and check G_i ≻ 0 exactly by rational elimination.
- *Result.* For orbit 66 (facet 561), the kernel dimensions are 1, 1, 1, 1, 2, 2, 2, 2 for i = 0, …, 7, the equality system has rank 1,140, and the margin is t = 3.6·10⁻³. Checker A (exact fmpq positive-definiteness) and checker B (charpoly sign test, all 360 class conditions, and the identity on 20 random boards to 7·10⁻¹⁶) both accept. **Theorem H.32.2.** The certificate proves facet 561 ≥ 0 on PF phase boards on rule 4. By H.31.1, it therefore proves the whole of orbit 66 on all 16 Lugano-type cases, independently of the Round-24 certificate.
- *Reading.* This is the same theorem as H.31.3 for 66. What is new is the method. The faces forced by the zero set are exactly the right size, so the certificate has a large margin and a small correction. Round 24 instead read the kernels off a near-singular solver output.

**H.32.3 Orbit 57: facial reduction stalls [NUMERICAL, negative; h25_frloop.sh, h25_frloop_o57*.log, h25_facetscan.sh, h25_facetscan_o57.log, h25_zs_o57.log].**
- *Facial reduction.* When the maximal margin is t ≈ 0, the dual of the margin problem gives PSD matrices Y_i (Σ tr Y_i = 1) whose ranges are further forced kernel directions (Borwein–Wolkowicz). We add these directions, after rationalising them, and repeat.
- *Facet 521.* Each step added one vector each for i = 3 and i = 5. The margins were 5.7·10⁻¹⁰, 9.1·10⁻⁸, 1.8·10⁻⁹ and −7.4·10⁻⁸, which never became clearly positive. From step 4 onward the new vectors are no longer small-denominator rationals (denominators in the thousands at tolerance 10⁻⁵). The chain therefore stalls. Either the minimal face is irrational, or the margin is zero up to solver noise.
- *All facets (stopping budget).* We set a budget: one zero-set face (400 starts) and one margin solve per facet of orbit 57 (192 facets), with no further chain (h25_facetscan.sh, time-bounded). The scan finished (h25_facetscan_o57.log, final). The margin solve ran on 190 of the 192 facets. For the other two (26329 and 28673) the zero-set step produced no output within its 600 s bound, and they were not retried. Every one of the 190 margins lies in [−9.3·10⁻⁸, +1.07·10⁻⁷], none exceeds 10⁻⁶, and none gives a valid certificate. So no facet of orbit 57 offers a strictly feasible zero-set face at this resolution, and we stopped there.
- *Status.* Orbit 57 on the Lugano families stays **NUMERICAL** (R bound ≥ −2·10⁻¹⁰ from H.31.4; face margins ≈ 0). An exact proof would need an algebraic (irrational) face, or a stronger relaxation.

**H.32.4 Orbit 75 [NUMERICAL; h25_zs_o75.log, h25_cert_o75zs.log, h25_cert_o75d1e-6.log, h24_cert_o75b.log].** The zero-set kernel has dimension 2 for every i. The first margin solve gives t = −8.1·10⁻⁸, and the dual Y_i have ranks 17–18 with spread eigenvalues, so they are not rank-deficient in a way that facial reduction could use. Together with the R value ≈ −2.8·10⁻⁷ (H.31.2), this suggests that R(75) is slightly negative, so that R itself cannot certify 75 even with exact arithmetic.  One more bounded attempt asked for the weaker bound facet ≥ −10⁻⁶·scale (DELTA = 10⁻⁶, with the zero-set kernels kept). SCS ended *optimal_inaccurate* after 2,852 s with t = −7.3·10⁻⁷ (h25_cert_o75d1e-6.log). That is uninformative, and we stopped there.Round 24's direct rounding also fails: the SCS dual has no clear rank gap (h24_cert_o75b.log).

**H.32.5 Grid-seeded multistart over the reduced family [NUMERICAL, validated; h25_gridms.py, h25_gridms_val.log, h25_gridms_open.log; failed variant h25_grid.py, h25_grid_val.log].**
- *Method.* A deterministic grid covers the nine parameters. The phases a_k take 8 values 2πj/8, and the three Bloch directions each take 12 Fibonacci points, giving 8³·12³ = 884,736 points. It is evaluated exhaustively. From each of the 512 phase triples the K lowest grid points are kept as seeds. Each seed is then descended independently (batched Adam, 2,000 steps), and the best end point is polished with L-BFGS. Every parameter value is a valid process (H.31.5), so no penalty is needed.
- *A failed variant.* A pure grid (12³·20³ = 1.4·10⁷ points) with one coupled L-BFGS polish of the 2,000 lowest points **misses** orbit 26: its grid minimum is −10⁻¹⁵, because the negative region is too thin for a grid point to land in. We discarded it.
- *Validation [passes].* K = 16 gives 8,192 seeds. Orbit 26: −2.428720·10⁻⁵ (4 of 8,192 seeds). Orbit 30: −8.964988·10⁻³ (648 of 8,192). Orbit 31: −8.964988·10⁻³ (428 of 8,192). These are the H.28.1 values.
- *The five.* K = 32 gives 16,384 seeds per orbit, eight times the rarity of orbit 26's crossing (h25_gridms_open.log). The polished minima are: 28: −1.2·10⁻¹⁵; 57: −1.5·10⁻¹⁵; 69: −5.1·10⁻¹⁶; 75: −1.1·10⁻¹⁵; 81: −7.6·10⁻¹⁶. No seed ends below −10⁻⁹. **No crossing** (NUMERICAL).
- *Reading.* Together with H.31.5 (6,000 random starts per orbit, 24,000 for 69), the search space has now been covered by two independent seedings. A crossing much rarer than orbit 26's could still be missed. The search is evidence, not proof.

**H.32.6 Status after Round 25 [summary].** On Purif the count is unchanged: **15 of 22 genuine orbits crossed, 7 open** (28, 54, 57, 66, 69, 75, 81). For the families studied: 54 and 66 hold everywhere studied (PROVED; 66 now with two independent certificates, H.31.3 and H.32.2). Five orbits (28, 57, 69, 75, 81) remain open on PF phase boards on rule 4. Of these, 57 has face margins |t| ≤ 1.1·10⁻⁷ on every face tried, and its R bound is ≥ −2·10⁻¹⁰ (NUMERICAL). 75 appears to have a slightly negative R bound (NUMERICAL). 28, 69 and 81 have clearly negative relaxations, and the validated searches find no crossing (NUMERICAL). *Not done.* An exact critical-point analysis (for example, Lagrange or KKT equations for λ_min of the 64×64 pencil in nine angles) was not attempted. Its size puts it beyond exact local tools. A higher-level moment hierarchy did not fit in memory either: Clarabel on full R already does not (H.31.4). *Reading [INTERPRETIVE].* On the zero-set face 57 looks exactly tight but irrational, while 75 looks like a relaxation gap of order 10⁻⁷. If so, 57 needs an algebraic certificate, and 75 needs a stronger relaxation.

**H.33 Round 26: a Hermitian sum-of-squares relaxation in the nine-angle family bounds any crossing of orbit 57 by 2.62·10⁻⁸ (Oct 27 2026).** Round 26 attacked the five orbits still open on the reduced family of H.31.1 (28, 57, 69, 75, 81) algebraically. The facet value is written as a polynomial positivity problem in the source and spinor variables, with the phases on the unit circle, and certified by a decomposable (PPT-type) Hermitian sum of squares (H.33.1). **What is proved.** For orbit 57 there is an exact certificate, accepted by two independent checkers, that the facet value is ≥ −2.62·10⁻⁸ on every PF phase board on rule 4. By H.31.1 the same bound therefore holds on all 16 Lugano-type cases, and so on every family studied (Theorem H.33.3). That is a bound on the size of any crossing, about a thousandth of the depth of orbit 26's crossing on the same family (−2.43·10⁻⁵). It is **not** a proof that orbit 57 holds. **What is not proved.** The numerical optimum of the relaxation for 57 is γ* ≈ +5·10⁻⁹, that is, zero to solver accuracy, but its Gram matrices have no clean rank gap (H.33.2), so no exact certificate of γ ≥ 0 was obtained. On 28, 69, 75 and 81 the relaxation gave no usable answer within the time bounds (H.33.4). **Validation.** The relaxation certifies 54 and 66 (both already PROVED) and does not certify 30: its objective there is about −0.019, below the true minimum −0.00896 (H.33.2). Orbit 26 did not converge within 50 minutes. **The tally on Purif is unchanged: 15 of 22 genuine orbits crossed, 7 open. On the families studied five are open (28, 57, 69, 75, 81), and 57's possible violation is now bounded by 2.62·10⁻⁸.** Code and logs are in code/stochastic/cyclic/embed/h26_*.

**H.33.1 The relaxation [definition; soundness PROVED, elementary].** Write each frame through an unnormalised spinor ξ_k ∈ C²: e₀ = ξ_k and e₁ = (−ξ̄_{k1}, ξ̄_{k0}). Write u_k for the phases. Let p(ψ, ξ, u) = Σ_{i,o} T[i,o] |⟨f_i(ξ)|D_o(u)ψ⟩|² + c|ψ|²Π_k|ξ_k|². This is a Hermitian form in ψ, bihomogeneous of degree (1,1) in each ξ_k, and a Laurent polynomial in u. For unit ψ and unit ξ_k, min_ψ p equals the facet value λ_min(M) + c of H.31.1. The checks against h24_lugsearch.Mmat at random points agree to 10⁻¹⁵ (h26_hsos.py). A *certificate of level γ* is an identity p − γ|ψ|²Π|ξ_k|² = Σ_{S⊆{0,1,2}} v_S†Q_Sv_S with Q_S ⪰ 0. It holds coefficientwise as polynomials in ψ, ψ̄, ξ, ξ̄ and as Laurent polynomials in u, so only |u_k| = 1 is used. Here v_S is the vector of monomials ψ_w · Π_{k∉S} ξ_{k,b_k} · Π_{k∈S} ξ̄_{k,b_k} · u^m, with m ≤ x(w) componentwise (176 monomials per block, 8 blocks). Each block is a sum of Hermitian squares, so any feasible γ is a lower bound on the minimum (soundness). The blocks S ≠ ∅ are partial-transpose (decomposable) terms. *Control:* the single block S = ∅ gives only γ* = −1.91 on orbit 30 and −1.50 on the proved orbit 66 (h26_hsos_runs.log), so the partial-transpose blocks carry the strength. The SDP is solved with SCS (eps 10⁻⁸; memory ≈ 1.8 GB). Interior-point solvers do not fit (H.31.4).

**H.33.2 Validation and the numerical optimum [NUMERICAL; h26_hsos_runs.log, h26_hsos_o30_scs.log, h26_qspec_o57.log].**
- *Proved orbits:* γ* = +3.3·10⁻⁹ (54) and +5.3·10⁻⁹ (66), *optimal*. Both are zero to solver tolerance, as they must be: the minima are 0 and attained.
- *Crossed orbit 30 (true minimum −0.00896):* SCS did not reach eps 10⁻⁸ within 50 minutes. A looser run (eps 10⁻⁶) settles at an objective of about −0.019 (primal residual ≈ 10⁻⁵ after 7,000 iterations). So the relaxation does not certify 30, as soundness requires. Orbits 26 and 31 were not run to completion: the run on 26 was killed at its 50-minute bound with no output, and 31 was not attempted. Since the relaxation is sound, it cannot certify a crossed orbit. The run on 30 is a check of the implementation, which the two checkers of H.33.3 also guard.
- *Orbit 57:* γ* = +5.5·10⁻⁹, *optimal*, in 49 s. Each of the eight Gram blocks has about 70 eigenvalues below 10⁻⁶ and about 120 below 10⁻⁴, out of 176. That is a continuum with no rank gap, so facial reduction has no clean face to use. This matches the irrational or noisy face of H.32.3. A run at eps 10⁻⁹ did not converge within 25 minutes.

**Theorem H.33.3 (a bound on any crossing of orbit 57) [PROVED in exact rational arithmetic by two independent checkers; h26_lbA.py (checker A), h26_lbB.py (checker B), h26_lbA_o57.log, h26_lbB_o57.log].** Let F be the normalised facet functional of the representative facet 521 of orbit 57. Then on every PF phase board on rule 4, F ≥ −δ with δ = 2.62·10⁻⁸. By H.31.1 the same holds for every facet of orbit 57 on all 16 Lugano-type cases. With H.27.1 and H.30.2 it therefore holds on every family studied.
- *Certificate.* The SCS Gram blocks are projected onto the coefficient identity (each Gram entry belongs to exactly one coefficient, so the projection is explicit) and rounded to Z[i]/2⁴⁸. The exact residual r = p − Σ_S v_S†Q̃_Sv_S has coefficient L¹ norm 5.1·10⁻¹¹. For each S a dyadic τ_S ∈ {2⁻³¹, 2⁻³⁰} makes Q̃_S + τ_S I positive definite. At unit ψ, unit ξ_k and |u_k| = 1, every monomial has modulus ≤ 1 and |v_S|² = Σ_w |ψ_w|² n_m(w) ≤ 4. Hence p ≥ −4Σ_Sτ_S − ‖r‖₁ = −δ.
- *Checker A* uses the coefficient rules of h26_hsos.poly and an exact fraction-free elimination in Python on the 352×352 real form of each block, requiring all pivots to be positive.
- *Checker B* is written independently. It rebuilds p by formal polynomial multiplication of the amplitudes ⟨f_i|D_oψ⟩ and their conjugates, rebuilds every monomial of v_S as an exponent vector, recomputes the residual in exact fractions, and tests positive definiteness with FLINT's fraction-free LU (identity permutation and positive leading minors, Sylvester). It also recomputes the factor 4 = max_w n_m(w).
- *Scope.* This is a lower bound, not the inequality: F ≥ 0 for orbit 57 remains OPEN. The bound is about 10³ times smaller than orbit 26's crossing on the same family (−2.43·10⁻⁵), so any crossing of 57 on the families studied would have to be about 10³ times shallower than that one.

**H.33.4 Orbits 28, 69, 75 and 81 [NUMERICAL, negative; h26_hsos_runs.log].** SCS did not converge within 50 minutes on 28 or 69 (no output). On 75 a run at eps 10⁻⁸ did not converge within 40 minutes (no output; h26_hsos_runs.log). 81 was not run, to stay within the memory budget. For 28 and 69 earlier relaxations were already clearly negative (H.31.4), so a non-converging run is what one would expect, and it is no evidence either way. On the five orbits, convergence tracks the earlier relaxations: the runs that certify (54, 66, 57) converge in 1–7 minutes, and the others do not. No exact bound was attempted for 28, 69, 75 or 81.

**H.33.5 Not done [summary].** *Exact critical-point analysis* (resultants or Gröbner bases on the nine-angle KKT system) was not attempted. Even after the reductions of H.33.1 the system is out of reach of the exact tools on the box. *Extended-precision SDP for 57 (Task 2)* was not run. There is no small multiple-precision SDP solver on the box. SDPA-GMP needs a build, which the disk budget (about 16 GB free) ruled out, and a pure-mpmath interior-point method on eight 352×352 blocks is far too slow. *Higher levels* (multipliers |ξ_k|², the full m basis) were not run, for memory and time. *Reading [INTERPRETIVE].* The Hermitian SOS is numerically exact on 57, as on 54 and 66, so 57 looks like a true inequality on this family with an irrational optimal face. The exact bound of H.33.3 is what the present tools can extract from it.

**H.34 Round 27: orbit 57 holds on every family studied (an exact rational sum-of-squares certificate on a twice-reduced face) (Oct 27 2026).** Round 27 returned to the Hermitian decomposable SOS of H.33.1 for orbit 57. The aim was to turn its numerically zero optimum into an exact proof. Three things made the difference. The problem has a torus symmetry that splits each Gram block into weight blocks of size at most 22 (H.34.1). The zero set of 57 on the reduced family turns out to be an explicit four-parameter family (H.34.3). And one more facial-reduction step, read off the dual of the max-margin problem, gives a face on which the certificate is strictly feasible (H.34.4). **What is proved.** For the representative facet 521 of orbit 57, the facet polynomial p is exactly a sum of Hermitian squares with rational Gram matrices, at level γ = 0. Two independent exact checkers accept this (Theorem H.34.6). So the facet value is ≥ 0 on every PF phase board on rule 4. By H.31.1 the same holds for every facet of orbit 57 on all 16 Lugano-type cases, and with H.27.1 and H.30.2 orbit 57 holds on every family of pure processes studied in this paper (Corollary H.34.6a). This supersedes the bound −2.62·10⁻⁸ of Theorem H.33.3 on those families. **What is not proved.** Whether 57 holds on all of Purif remains OPEN, as for 54 and 66. Orbits 28, 69, 75 and 81 were not attempted with this pipeline in Round 27. **Tally.** On Purif it is unchanged: 15 of 22 genuine orbits crossed, 7 open. On the families studied, 54, 57 and 66 are proved and four orbits are open (28, 69, 75, 81). Code and logs are in code/stochastic/cyclic/embed/h27_*.

**H.34.1 Torus-weight reduction [the symmetry is checked exactly on every coefficient; h27_export.py].** The polynomial p of H.33.1 is invariant under ξ_{k1} → e^{iφ_k}ξ_{k1} together with ψ_z → e^{iφ_k z_k}ψ_z. Equivalently, every key (z, w, ξ-exponents, ξ̄-exponents, d) has weight −z_k + w_k + ξ_k − ξ̄_k = 0, and h27_export.py asserts this for every coefficient of every orbit exported. Averaging any certificate over the torus (Reynolds operator) gives another certificate, so each Q_S may be taken block-diagonal by the weight wt_k = w_k + b_k (k ∉ S) or w_k − b_k (k ∈ S). This gives 216 blocks of size at most 22, with Σn² = 13,824 against 8·176² = 247,808. Each complex key equation is kept once per conjugate pair, which leaves 1,206 real equations with exact rational right-hand sides, exported as JSON. The reduction is only a modelling choice. The final certificate is checked directly against p (H.34.6), so its soundness does not depend on it.

**H.34.2 Solvers and validation [NUMERICAL; h27_reduced_f64.log].** *Julia.* Julia 1.11.7 with Clarabel.jl 0.11.1 (native API, no JuMP), JSON3 and GenericLinearAlgebra is installed user-locally under /workspace/julia (depot at /workspace/julia/depot; not in the repository). h27_solve.jl reads the exported JSON and assembles the problem in Float64 or BigFloat. A 2×2 test SDP solves in both Float64 and BigFloat (256 bits). Subproblems with the first 27, 54 and 108 of the 216 weight blocks solve in Float64 in 1.9 s, 7.4 s and 124 s (timing only; these are not valid relaxations). On the full problem (29,057 variables) Clarabel.jl is much slower than the Rust solver here. A Float64 run on the crossed orbit 30 reached only 8 interior-point iterations in its 30-minute limit, where the iterate was γ ≈ −0.0195, not converged but consistent with the Rust and SCS values below. The first attempt, on 57, was stopped after 10 minutes. Earlier we read the missing log output as a hang in setup; in fact the output was buffered. So Clarabel.jl gives no converged full-problem result, and BigFloat on the full problem is out of reach at this speed (h27_julia_f64.log). *Rust Clarabel.* The same JSON solved with the Rust Clarabel (Python bindings, h27_pyclar.py, tolerance 10⁻¹⁰) passes validation. Orbit 30 is not certified: γ* = −0.018799, below its true minimum −0.00896, and in agreement with SCS (H.33.2). Orbits 54 and 66 give γ* ≈ 6·10⁻¹⁰ and 4·10⁻¹¹, that is, zero. For 57 the solver stops at AlmostSolved with γ* = +8.1·10⁻⁸, which is above zero and therefore a solver inaccuracy. The full problem is degenerate, with a large forced kernel. High-precision arithmetic turned out not to be needed: the face reduction below makes the problem well-conditioned (margin 1.2·10⁻³), so a double-precision solve rounds to an exact certificate.

**H.34.3 The zero set of orbit 57 [NUMERICAL; h27_zs.py, h27_zs_o57.log].** All random-start minimisers (Adam followed by L-BFGS, as in h25_zeroset.py) lie, to three digits, on one of two explicit four-parameter families. In the parametrisation of H.31.1 (u_k = e^{ia_k}, ξ_k = (cos t_k/2, sin t_k/2·e^{ip_k})) the first is u_1 = u_2 = −1, t_0 = π/2 (ξ_0 = (1, e^{ip_0})/√2), ξ_2 = (1, 0), with a_0, p_0 and ξ_1 free. The second is the same family with the roles of parties 0 and 1 exchanged. On 600 points sampled exactly on these families, the facet value λ_min(M) + c is |·| ≤ 1.6·10⁻¹⁵, and the kernel of M is one-dimensional. Random-start zeros are accurate only to about 10⁻⁶ (the minimum is quadratic), which blurs the kernel ranks (h27_zs_o57.log, first run). The exact family gives clean ones. Stacking v_S(ψ, ξ, u) over the zeros and splitting by weight gives, in every one of the 216 blocks, a kernel span with a singular-value gap above 10⁶. The total kernel rank is 192, and every RREF basis has integer entries. This description of the zero set is not used in the proof.

**H.34.4 Facial reduction: the missing second step [NUMERICAL; h27_face.py, h27_aug.py, h27_face_o57.log].** By complementary slackness (H.32.1), each weight block must vanish on the zero-set kernel. Restricting each block to its complement, Q_b = N_bG_bN_b†, leaves face dimension 1,216 (Σd² = 11,056). On this face max γ = −3·10⁻¹², but the max-margin problem (γ = 0 fixed, maximise t with G_b ⪰ tI) gives t = −2.5·10⁻¹³: the face is not yet minimal, which is the stall of H.32.3 in a cleaner form. The dual of the max-margin problem is exactly rank one (complex) in 12 blocks, with values 3/80 and 1/20. Its range gives 12 further kernel vectors, all rational (patterns (1, −1), and entries ±1, ±1/6, 2/3). After adding them, the face has dimension 1,204 (Σd² = 10,844) and the max-margin problem is strictly feasible, with t = 1.2·10⁻³ (Rust Clarabel, status Solved). *Reading [INTERPRETIVE].* These vectors are forced by second-order behaviour along the zero set, not by the zero values themselves. The H.32 facial reduction stopped one step short of them.

**H.34.5 Rounding [construction; not trusted; h27_exact.py, h27_exact_o57.log].** The kernels are rounded to their integer RREF, after a check that the entries are integers to within 10⁻⁵. N_b is the integer null-space basis, computed with FLINT. The numerical Q_b is pulled back to G_b = N_b⁺Q_bN_b⁺†, rounded to 2⁻⁴⁰, and corrected exactly. The correction is the minimum-norm solution of the 1,152 independent equations, solved with a rational FLINT solve of size 1,152; its largest entry is 1.1·10⁻⁷. All 1,206 equations then hold exactly, including the 54 that were dropped as dependent. All 208 nonempty G_b are exactly positive definite, with smallest float eigenvalue about 1.7·10⁻⁴. The certificate is h27_cert_o57.json.gz (gzip, 0.2 MB): for each block it stores S, the weight, the basis indices, the integer N_b and the rational G_b. Rerunning the whole pipeline with an independent sample of zeros (seed 2) reproduces every step: margin −2.3·10⁻¹³ on the zero-set face, 12 extra kernel vectors, margin 1.2·10⁻³ after them, and a certificate accepted by both checkers (h27_pipeline_seed2_o57.log).

**Theorem H.34.6 (orbit 57 on the reduced family) [PROVED in exact rational arithmetic by two independent checkers; h27_chkA.py, h27_chkB.py, h27_chkA_o57.log, h27_chkB_o57.log].** Let p be the facet polynomial of H.33.1 for the representative facet 521 of orbit 57 (c = 1/4, T with denominator 96). Then p = Σ_{S⊆{0,1,2}} v_S†Q_Sv_S identically, as polynomials in ψ, ψ̄, ξ, ξ̄ and Laurent polynomials in u, with Q_S = Σ_b E_bN_bG_bN_bᵀE_bᵀ ⪰ 0. Hence the facet value is ≥ 0 on every PF phase board on rule 4. It is attained, since the families of H.34.3 are zeros, so the minimum is 0. That attainment is NUMERICAL to 10⁻¹⁵ and is not needed for the inequality. *Checker A* builds p with h26_hsos.poly from rational T and c, and compares coefficients key by key using the Round-26 key rule: all 1,206 key coefficients of the residual are exactly zero. It checks that each G_b is Hermitian, and proves it positive definite by fraction-free (Bareiss) elimination of the integer-scaled real form [[Re, −Im], [Im, Re]]. *Checker B* is independent of h26_hsos and of the export. It rebuilds p by formal polynomial arithmetic from the amplitudes, as in h26_lbB.py, and rebuilds the v_S monomials as exponent vectors. It forms N_bG_bN_bᵀ with FLINT fmpq_mat, and proves each G_b positive definite with FLINT's fflu (identity permutation and positive leading minors). Both report a residual of 0 on all 208 blocks. *Negative controls.* Raising γ to 10⁻⁶ leaves 64 nonzero residual coefficients, and perturbing one Gram entry by 10⁻¹² leaves one. Both checkers reject both certificates.

- **Corollary H.34.6a.** Orbit 57 holds on every family of pure processes studied in this paper: forward PF lifts in any dimension (Theorem H.27.1); every valid three-qubit phase process (Theorem H.30.2 off the Lugano components, together with H.34.6 via H.31.1 on them); and time-reversed PF lifts on all 20 refined PFs (H.30.2 for the 12 single-block rules, and H.34.6 via H.31.1 for the 8 Lugano-type rules). Whether it holds on all of Purif remains OPEN.

**H.34.7 Not done [summary].** *High-precision solves.* A BigFloat Clarabel.jl solve of 57 was not run and turned out not to be needed. Clarabel.jl in Float64 did not converge on the full problem within 30 minutes (H.34.2). The certificate path used only Python: NumPy/PyTorch for the zeros, Rust Clarabel for the face SDPs, and FLINT/Fractions for the exact steps. Julia played no part in it. *Algebraic rounding.* PSLQ/LLL over Q(√2) was not needed: all kernels and the certificate are rational. *Other orbits.* The zero-set and facial-reduction pipeline (h27_zs.py → h27_face.py → h27_aug.py → h27_exact.py → h27_chkA/B.py) was not run on 28, 69, 75 or 81. On 28, 69 and 81 the earlier relaxation bounds are clearly negative (H.31.4, H.33.4), so this relaxation may not certify them at all; 75 is the natural next candidate.

**H.35 Round 28: orbit 75 holds on every family studied (an exact sum of twelve Hermitian squares) (Oct 27 2026).** Round 28 ran the Round 27 pipeline (H.34) on orbit 75, representative facet 595 (c = 1/4), on PF phase boards on rule 4. The Round 27 facial-reduction step did not apply, but the numerical optimum turned out to be so low-rank that it could be rounded directly. **What is proved.** The facet polynomial p of H.33.1 for facet 595 is exactly a sum of 12 Hermitian squares, with integer coefficient vectors and weights 1/6 and 1/3, at level γ = 0. Two independent exact checkers accept this (Theorem H.35.4). So orbit 75 holds on PF phase boards on rule 4, and by H.31.1, H.27.1 and H.30.2 on every family of pure processes studied in this paper (Corollary H.35.4a). **What is not proved.** Whether 75 holds on all of Purif remains OPEN. **Tally.** On Purif it is unchanged: 15 of 22 genuine orbits crossed, 7 open. On the families studied, 54, 57, 66 and 75 are proved and three orbits are open (28, 69, 81). Code and logs are in code/stochastic/cyclic/embed/h28_*. The h27_* scripts take the orbit as an argument and were reused unchanged, except for a comment in h27_zs.py.

**H.35.1 The zero set of orbit 75 [NUMERICAL; h28_zeros_o75.log].** In 600 random starts (Adam followed by L-BFGS), the minima range from −1.25·10⁻¹⁵ to 1.2·10⁻⁹. Every one lies on the zero family of orbit 57 (H.34.3): 418 on family A (u_1 = u_2 = −1, t_0 = π/2, ξ_2 = (1, 0)), 417 on its party-0↔1 swap B (235 on both), and none on neither. So h27_zs.py was used with its exact A ∪ B family (300 points per component). The zero-set face again has dimension 1,216.

**H.35.2 The Round 27 facial-reduction step does not apply [NUMERICAL; h28_pipeline_o75.log].** On the zero-set face max γ = −1.65·10⁻¹¹ (Rust Clarabel, AlmostSolved). The max-margin problem gives t = −5.9·10⁻¹⁰ (AlmostSolved). So the face is not strictly feasible. Unlike 57, the max-margin dual has no clean rank gap, so the augmentation of H.34.4 (h27_aug.py) does not apply: its threshold selects the whole face. *The primal is the useful object.* The numerical Gram matrix at the γ optimum has exactly 12 complex eigenvalues ≥ 0.1, and every other eigenvalue is ≤ 1.4·10⁻⁵. They sit in 8 of the 216 weight blocks, one block for each S ⊆ {0,1,2}: two for S = 0, 3, 5, 6 and one for S = 1, 2, 4, 7. The eigenvalues are 2 and 10/3 (two-direction blocks) and 8/3 (one-direction blocks), and the eigenvectors are rational to solver accuracy, with entries in {0, ±1, ±1/2}.

**H.35.3 Rounding [construction; not trusted; h28_lowrank.py, h28_lowrank_o75.log].** Each of the 12 eigenvectors is normalised to largest entry 1, rationalised with denominator ≤ 12 and scaled to an integer vector w (entries 0, ±1, ±2). Its weight μ is the Rayleigh quotient wᵀQw/|w|⁴, rationalised. This gives μ = 1/6 for the 8 squares in the two-direction blocks and μ = 1/3 for the other 4. All other directions are dropped. The output is an h27-format certificate with N_b = [w …] and G_b = diag(μ), so the Round 27 checkers apply unchanged. No exact linear solve or correction was needed: the rounded certificate is already exact. Four of the squares have 8 terms, four have 9 and four have 11; all 12 are listed in h28_sos_o75.txt (h28_print_sos.py).

**Theorem H.35.4 (orbit 75 on the reduced family) [PROVED in exact rational arithmetic by two independent checkers; h27_chkA.py, h27_chkB.py, h28_chkA_o75.log, h28_chkB_o75.log].** Let p be the facet polynomial of H.33.1 for the representative facet 595 of orbit 75. Then p = Σ_{j=1}^{12} μ_j |⟨w_j, v_{S_j}⟩|² identically, as polynomials in ψ, ψ̄, ξ, ξ̄ and Laurent polynomials in u, with μ_j ∈ {1/6, 1/3} and integer vectors w_j supported on one torus-weight block each (h28_cert_o75.json.gz). Hence the facet value is ≥ 0 on every PF phase board on rule 4. *Checks.* Both checkers (written for H.34.6 and reused unchanged) recompute p from the rational T and c, confirm that all 514 coefficient keys agree exactly (residual zero), and confirm positive definiteness of each G_b, by fraction-free elimination (A) and by FLINT fflu with Sylvester minors (B). *Negative controls.* Both reject the certificate when γ is raised to 10⁻⁶ (64 nonzero residuals), when one weight is changed by 10⁻¹² (73 nonzero residuals), and when one weight is negated (not positive definite). *Independent cross-check [NUMERICAL; h28_numcheck.py, h28_numcheck_o75.log].* At 2,000 random points the smallest eigenvalue in ψ of the sum of squares agrees with the facet value λ_min(M) + c of H.31.1 to 1.7·10⁻¹⁵. This check does not go through the polynomial construction of h26_hsos.py. *Reproducibility.* An independent rerun with zero-set seed 2 gives γ* = −1.4·10⁻¹², the same 12 directions, and a byte-identical certificate (h28_pipeline_seed2_o75.log).

- **Corollary H.35.4a.** Orbit 75 holds on every family of pure processes studied in this paper: forward PF lifts in any dimension (Theorem H.27.1); every valid three-qubit phase process (Theorem H.30.2 off the Lugano components, together with H.35.4 via H.31.1 on them); and time-reversed PF lifts on all 20 refined PFs (H.30.2 for the 12 single-block rules, and H.35.4 via H.31.1 for the 8 Lugano-type rules). Whether it holds on all of Purif remains OPEN.

*Reading [INTERPRETIVE].* For 75 the certificate is a short sum of squares, which suggests that the earlier difficulties (H.31.4, H.32.4) were numerical rather than a gap in the relaxation. Each of the 12 squares lies in the zero-set face, so it vanishes on the zero family, as H.32.1 requires. Why 75 has such a small certificate while 57 needed a face of dimension 1,204 is not understood.

**H.35.5 Orbits 28, 69 and 81 [NUMERICAL; h28_others.log].** These remain OPEN on the families studied. *Search.* Random-start minimisation on the reduced family (Adam followed by L-BFGS, 600 starts with seed 1 and 4,000 with seed 2 per orbit) gives minima between −1.4·10⁻¹⁵ and 0 for all three, so no crossing was found. The zero set of 28 is large: 90% of the starts end below 6·10⁻¹³. *Relaxation, basis of H.33.1.* With the torus-weight blocks and no zero-set restriction, the largest γ is −0.0230 for 28 and −0.0292 for 81 (Rust Clarabel, Solved). For 69 it is −4.99·10⁻⁵ (Clarabel, AlmostSolved), and SCS gives primal −4.959·10⁻⁵ and dual −4.958·10⁻⁵. These match the earlier SDP2 gaps (H.31.4). The zero-set face can only lower these values, because it is a restriction of the same problem. So no certificate at γ = 0 exists on it in this basis, whichever zeros are used. The near-zero face values seen first for 69 and 81 came with solver status NumericalError, at Gram matrices with eigenvalues down to −1.2·10⁻³ and −2.8·10⁻². For 28 the face equations are inconsistent outright. *Larger basis.* Allowing every u-monomial m ∈ {0,1}³ for each ψ index (MB=full: blocks up to 64, 5,832 rows; SCS, because the Clarabel assembly ran out of memory) gives −0.0100 for 28 and −0.0292 for 81, unchanged for 81. For 69 it gives −2.86·10⁻⁷, with dual −2.87·10⁻⁷ (SCS at its iteration limit, status inaccurate). That Gram matrix is not low-rank (575 eigenvalues above 10⁻⁶), so the rounding of H.35.3 does not apply. *Reading [INTERPRETIVE].* For 28 and 81 the gap to the apparent minimum 0 is large and stable, which points to relaxation slack rather than a crossing: a crossing that deep would be easy for the searches to find. For 69 the larger basis shrinks the gap by more than two orders of magnitude, so 69 may be certifiable at this level with an accurate solver and facial reduction. That was not achieved within the time bounds.

## Appendix I. Robustness: approximately blended versus approximately liftable (Oct 22 2026)

Norm: for classical processes, ||p - q||_1 = sum over (i,o) of |p(i|o) - q(i|o)|. For machines, the switchboard residual is rho(U) := sqrt(R(U)), the l2 norm of the forbidden Pauli coefficients (H.8.1).

**I.1 Blend-close implies liftable-close [proved, trivial].** If ||p - q||_1 <= eps with q in conv(PF), then p is within eps of a purifiable process, namely q (C.1). So delta = eps, in any norm. This uses only conv(PF) ⊆ Purif.

**I.2 The converse is the conjecture [proved, trivial; the conjecture is false by H.12.1, so the converse fails].** "dist(p, Purif) <= delta implies dist(p, conv PF) <= eps(delta) with eps(delta) -> 0" for all p holds iff the closure of Purif equals conv(PF). If the conjecture holds, then eps = delta. If it fails at some p*, then eps(0) >= dist(p*, conv PF) > 0. So no quantitative converse can be proved without proving the conjecture. A non-trivial robustness statement must therefore compare *approximately pure machines* with the hull, as follows.

**I.3 Residual controls operator purity [proved].** In family C, with or without ancillas, every entry of M M^dag - 1 has modulus at most 2 sqrt(2) rho(U).
*Proof.* Write K = (allowed part) + sum over forbidden sigma of c_sigma sigma. The allowed part reproduces the exact row inner products (H.8.1, H.8.2). A forbidden X^delta Z^b contributes c_{delta,b} sum_e (+-1) prod_k N_k(e_k). By Cauchy-Schwarz on unit rows, sum_e |prod_k N_k(e_k)| <= prod_k sum_{e_k} |N_k(e_k)| <= 1. Only the 8 strings with a = delta contribute, so the deviation is at most sum_b |c_{delta,b}| <= sqrt(8) rho. ∎

**I.4 Facet violation versus residual [numerical].** Facet violations bound distance: for a facet with +-1 coefficients and bound 0, f.p <= ||p - q||_1 for every q in the hull, so a violation v gives dist_1(p, conv PF) >= v. push.py minimises R subject to lambda_max(F_m(U)) >= v for facet-82 members (the best source state phi for each machine), in float64 with L-BFGS and an increasing penalty. Starts were random, Lugano, and the H.7 solutions rand_1, rand_15 and lug_21. There were 108 runs (curve.jsonl, curve2.jsonl).

| target v | runs | min R found | min R / v^2 | median R / v^2 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.01 | 21 | 3.27e-5 | 0.327 | 0.431 |
| 0.03 | 21 | 2.81e-4 | 0.312 | 0.387 |
| 0.1 | 21 | 3.19e-3 | 0.319 | 0.367 |
| 0.3 | 21 | 2.94e-2 | 0.326 | 0.370 |
| 0.5 | 8 | 9.27e-2 | 0.371 | 0.383 |
| 0.7 | 8 | 1.93e-1 | 0.394 | 0.419 |
| 0.9 | 8 | 3.52e-1 | 0.434 | 0.453 |

**Numerical statement (WITHDRAWN after H.12; the exact counterexample has R = 0 with positive violation).** For every switchboard machine found by push.py, facet-82 violation <= 1.79 rho(U), i.e. R >= 0.31 v^2. The ratio R/v^2 is nearly flat for small v, so near the hull boundary the violation grows at most linearly in rho: the modulus looks Lipschitz, not Hölder-1/2. The floor rises slowly for large v. These are local optima: the true infimum of R/v^2 could be lower, and only facet 82 was tested. For contrast, the H.7 sampled-unitary runs reached violations near 0.9 at sampled residual 0.03-0.05. That residual is a sampled average of ||M M^dag - 1||^2 and is not comparable with R. In the exact residual the trade-off is quadratic in v, so a violation is not cheap.

**I.5 Converse direction, numerically [numerical; WITHDRAWN after H.12].** Read the other way: a switchboard machine with rho(U) <= delta has statistics, at every source state, that violate facet 82 by at most about 1.79 delta. A machine whose statistics violate it by v is at least rho >= 0.56 v from pure. So in family C, numerically and for facet 82, "delta-close to liftable" (in the machine residual rho) implies "eps-close to the hull's facet-82 side" with eps ~ 1.8 delta. A full hull-distance version, over all 93 facet orbits, was not run.

## Appendix J. Facet-orbit tables for H.13-H.14 (Oct 23 2026)

Generated by code/stochastic/cyclic/switchboard/h13/tables.py from the committed data: facets from physics/facets/pf.out.gz, orbit labels and Z_4 maxima from xviol.npz, Z_8 maxima from xviol_z8.npz, exact Z_4 maxima from exactmax_part1/2.log, and Z_8 certificates from cert_z8.log. Strings are written i_A i_B i_C. "p(i|o)" is the probability of input string i given output string o.

*Representatives.* Each orbit's representative is its lowest-index facet. The integer form shown is the one with minimal ℓ¹ norm among the affine representatives that agree with the facet on all 744 process functions (found by LP, then scaled to integers and checked exactly). The representatives are exact. Other members of the orbit follow by the 384 symmetries (relabelling parties and local relabelling of inputs and outputs).

*Maxima.* "Rel." means the largest violation found divided by the facet's range on process functions. The Z_4 exact column gives the minimal polynomial of λ_min, the least eigenvalue of the facet operator Σ g P_{o,i} + g0 in the facet's normalisation. The violation is its root at the float-selected maximising board; this exact value is [proved, exact] for that board, while the claim that it is the maximum over the class is [numerical]. The Z_8 column is [numerical].

### J.1 The 22 genuine never-crossed orbits, plus orbit 92 (positivity) [numerical; orbit 92 proved in H.17.5]

Status for all 23 listed orbits (22 genuine, plus orbit 92 = positivity): never violated by the Z_4 (386 classes) and Z_8 (10,603 classes) X-type boards, maximised over source states [numerical].

*Update (H.17, Oct 23 2026).* None of the 23 orbits is violated by Z_16 X-type boards, continuous X-type phases, locally rotated (local Clifford and SU(2)^3) X-type boards, or pure non-X boards with ≤3 terms per outcome [numerical]. Orbits 4–11, 14, 25, 26, 28, 30, 31, 53, 54, 57, 66, 69, 75, 79 and 81 are violated at exact rational points of the valid polytope, so validity alone does not imply them [exact-certified, H.17.5]. Orbit 92 is the positivity orbit p(i|o) ≥ 0. On Valid its functional equals p(111|000) exactly, so it holds for every process [proved, H.17.5]. Only the other 22 orbits are genuinely open. *Update (Round 15, H.22.2): orbits 4–11, 14 and 25 are crossed by a pure board (the time-reversed Lugano process in rotated input and output frames, exact certificate), and orbits 53 and 79 by a generalised Lugano board (H.22.2, addendum). The open list is now the 10 orbits 26, 28, 30, 31, 54, 57, 66, 69, 75, 81.*
